The robust rule: a statement runs to its top-level `;`; a `CASE…END` expression has no `;`, so it is contained entirely within one statement. Format by recursing on *units* (statement | BEGIN-block | IF/WHILE/FOR), where a block only consumes `END`s seen at unit-start position — a CASE's `END` is mid-statement and never reaches the block loop. This also makes the formatter idempotent (structure comes from BEGIN/END/`;`, never from whitespace) and is the same insight behind the body scanner's subprogram skipping.

**Item-4 architecture analysis (the held migration plan).** *Current:* all DDL → `FirebirdDdlExecutor.ExecuteAsync` → the **metadata working tx** (manual Commit). *Consumers:* TableDetail structure edits (Pola/Ograniczenia/Indeksy — rely on Rollback-to-undo), Opis/View/Procedure descriptions, New Table CREATE, View/Procedure Compile, SQL-Editor DDL (F5 → metadata lane). *Desired:* every DDL auto-commits; transactions only for data. *What breaks:* Rollback-of-structural-edit + atomic multi-statement structural edits (⚠️ ADD FIELD-with-generator emits CREATE GENERATOR + CREATE TRIGGER + ALTER — must stay one autonomous tx, not per-statement) + the metadata Commit/Rollback UI. *Can the metadata tx be removed?* The **working transaction yes** (DDL → autonomous auto-commit on a transient connection — the proven `ExecuteAdminBatchAsync` path; reads use the implicit per-command read tx); the metadata **connection stays** (C2 read isolation from the data working tx). *Migration (phased, no screen breakage):* **A** `FirebirdDdlExecutor.ExecuteAsync` → one autonomous tx per apply (atomic multi-statement); **B** SQL-Editor metadata DDL → same; **C** remove metadata Commit/Rollback buttons + bar, narrow `CommitAll`/`RollbackAll` + `ShowTransactionButtons` to data; **D** shrink the metadata `TransactionService` to connection+lock only. Data lane (Dane edit, Procedure Execute, SQL DML/SELECT) unchanged. Risk: medium; each phase independently shippable.

### V1.4 follow-up — formatter blank lines + Execute-dialog polish (partial, uncommitted, 2026-06-16)

Two of the eight pre-freeze follow-up items done; the rest (structured Easy mode) still pending. **1235 → 1239 tests** (+4). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

- **Item 1 — PSQL formatter preserves logical blank lines.** `FormatPsql` now carries a parallel `List<bool> blank` flag (true when ≥2 source newlines precede a significant token); `EmitPsqlUnit`/`EmitPsqlBranch` call `MaybeBlankLine` before each unit + comment. Author blank lines separating declaration groups / loops / IF / SUSPEND survive; runs of 2+ blanks collapse to ONE; never a leading blank, never doubled — so idempotency holds (`Format(Format(x)) == Format(x)`). Pinned by `PreservesLogicalBlankLines` + `CollapsesMultipleBlankLinesToOne` (23 PSQL tests total).
- **Item 6 — Execute Procedure dialog polish.** `SizeToContent="Height"` + `MaxHeight=700` (auto-sizes to the param list); `TimePicker UseSeconds="True"` (TIMESTAMP/TIME to the second); per-kind defaults seeded in the row ctor (DATE/TIMESTAMP=now, TIME=now, NUMERIC=0, BOOLEAN=false, text=empty); **NULL checkbox checked by default for every param** (IBExpert-style); **in-memory per-procedure param history** (`ExecuteProcedureHistory`, process-lifetime, no persistence) restored on open / saved on Accept via `ApplyHistoryValue`. Dialog VM ctor now takes the procedure name; the view passes `_currentVm.ProcedureName`. Pinned by `ExecuteDialog_DefaultsAndNullChecked` + `ExecuteDialog_History_RoundTripsDuringSession` (two existing `Resolve_*` tests updated to set `IsNull = false` now that NULL defaults to checked).
- **Item 8 — metadata auto-commit: Phase A only, unchanged.** No B–D; awaiting live-DB validation.
- Items 2/3/4/5/7 (structured Easy mode) — see the dedicated milestone below (now done).

### Structured Easy Mode — Variables/Cursors/Subprograms as model elements (shipped 2026-06-16, uncommitted)

Items 2/3/4/5/7 implemented as one unit. Easy mode is no longer a text-derived projection: the procedure body is a canonical model `{inputs, outputs, variables, cursors, subprograms, executableBody}`; the DECLARE section is regenerated from the model; the body editor holds ONLY the executable `BEGIN…END`. **1239 → 1253 tests** (+14). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

**Core (round-trip engine, fully unit-pinned).** New [ProcedureBodyModel.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Sql/ProcedureBodyModel.cs): `ProcedureVariable {Name, TypeText, NotNull, Default}` (TypeText free-text → every Firebird form round-trips verbatim — VARCHAR(n), NUMERIC(p,s), domain, `TYPE OF COLUMN`, `CHARACTER SET`/`COLLATE` all live inline, no information loss), `ProcedureCursor {Name, Declaration}` + `ProcedureSubprogram {Name, Kind, Declaration}` (full `DECLARE …;` text, verbatim), and `ProcedureBodyModel {Variables, Cursors, Subprograms, ExecutableBody}`. `ProcedureBodySplitter.Split(body)` walks the top-level DECLARE section (reusing `SqlScanHelpers` + `ProcedureSignatureParser.ParseSegment` for variables) and keeps the `BEGIN…END` as `ExecutableBody`; `DdlGenerator.BuildProcedureBody(model)` is the deterministic inverse (variables → canonical `DECLARE VARIABLE`; cursors/subprograms verbatim; then the executable body). `ParseCursorName` / `ParseSubprogram` derive a row's display name/kind from its edited declaration. Round-trip is idempotent (`Split∘Build∘Split == Split`) — pinned by [ProcedureBodyModelTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ProcedureBodyModelTests.cs) (10).

**App (editable model elements).** New [ProcedureLocalRowViewModels.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ProcedureLocalRowViewModels.cs): `ProcedureVariableRowViewModel` reuses the field infrastructure — same `DomainSpec` list + "(none)" sentinel as the New Table / Pola grids (no second type system), TypeText canonical; `ProcedureCursorRowViewModel` / `ProcedureSubprogramRowViewModel` hold an editable `Declaration` with the name/kind auto-derived on edit. [ProcedureDetailTabViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ProcedureDetailTabViewModel.cs) now owns `ObservableCollection`s of these row VMs with Add/Delete/Move + selection commands (generic `DeleteRow`/`CanUp`/`CanDown`/`MoveRow<T>` helpers), `ExecutableBody` (the Easy body editor) replacing the old full-body `BodyText`, `SyncEasyModelFromBody` (Source→Easy split) + `BuildBodyModel`/`BuildFullSource` (Easy→Source regenerate). **All text-insertion-at-caret (`Insert*` snippet commands) removed** — locals are added as model rows, not typed into the editor. `MainWindowViewModel.CreateProcedureDetail` best-effort-loads domains into the grid's combo.

**View** ([ProcedureDetailTabView](src/EmberTern.App/Views/ProcedureDetailTabView.axaml)). Variables → editable `DataGrid` (Name / Type / Domain combo / Not Null / Default) + Add/Delete/Move toolbar. Cursors + Subprograms → IBExpert split: list (left) + **editable** SQL editor (right) + Add/Delete/Move toolbar. The cursor/subprogram editors are full editors — syntax highlighting + autocomplete + Ctrl+Click + double-click navigation via `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach`, plus Alt+F format (in-place for the local editors, via the VM command for body/source). A `_focusedEditor` tracks which editable editor the toolbar Format/Comment act on.

**Round-trip tests** ([ProcedureEasyModeRoundTripTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ProcedureEasyModeRoundTripTests.cs), 4): Source→Easy populates the structured model; Source→Easy→Source→Easy preserves every part; Easy→Source→Easy preserves every part (incl. derived cursor/subprogram names); Compile-from-Easy reassembles from the model. A full-model snapshot (params + variables + cursors + subprograms + executable body) is compared for exact equality across the round-trip.

**Limitation (documented, by design).** Variable Size/Scale/Sub Type/Charset/Collate/`TYPE OF` are authored **inline in the free-text Type column** (the natural Firebird declaration form), not as separate structured cells — this is deliberate: free-text TypeText is the canonical value, guaranteeing zero information loss on round-trip (a fixed Size/Scale/charset column set cannot represent `TYPE OF COLUMN`/`COLLATE` and would silently drop them). The Domain combo reuses the field-definition domain infrastructure. Type case may normalise (semantics preserved; idempotent). *(Superseded by the round-2 milestone below — full structured columns now ship.)*

### Structured Easy Mode round 2 — full field grids, cursor/subprogram workflow, highlighting (shipped 2026-06-16, uncommitted)

Second feedback pass (10 items). **1253 → 1267 tests** (+14). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

- **#1 Full field-definition grids + dropdowns.** New shared [`ProcedureFieldRowBase`](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ProcedureFieldRowBase.cs) backs Input / Output param + Variable rows with the **full 12-column field model** — Name / **Type (dropdown)** / TYPE OF / **Domain (dropdown)** / Size / Scale / Sub Type / Charset / Not Null / Collate / Default / Description — reusing `FieldDefinition` + `DdlGenerator.FormatTypeOrDomain` + `DomainSpec` (no second type system). Columns built once in the view code-behind (`BuildFieldColumns`), shared by all three grids; Type/Domain are real `ComboBox` cells. **Round-trip safety**: structured editors *compose* the canonical `TypeText`, which stays exactly as loaded until a structured field is edited — so `TYPE OF COLUMN`, `CHARACTER SET`, `COLLATE`, and any exotic form survive load→save with zero loss (parsed for display, preserved verbatim).
- **#2 Compile lock RCA + diagnostics (no architecture change, per instruction).** RCA: a procedure Execute (`SELECT * FROM proc(…)`) runs on the **Data lane** and auto-begins the data working tx, which holds the *selectable procedure* "in use" until it ends (no autocommit, by design). Firebird's metadata-update in-use check is **cross-attachment** — the autonomous metadata Compile (separate attachment, `Pooling=false`) still fails with "object … is in use" while ANY transaction holds the procedure. The unified Rollback disposes the data tx; if Compile still fails after rollback, another transaction holds it. Added env-gated (`EMBERTERN_TX_DIAG`) MON$TRANSACTIONS ⋈ MON$ATTACHMENTS dump in [`FirebirdConnectionService.ExecuteAutonomousAsync`](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdConnectionService.cs)'s in-use catch so the exact holding attachment+transaction is captured live for the next validation pass.
- **#3 Cursors.** Name editable in the left list + **Scroll checkbox**; editing name/Scroll rewrites the declaration header (`ProcedureBodySplitter.RewriteCursorHeader` / `CursorIsScroll`) keeping the SELECT body; Scroll survives Easy→Source→Easy.
- **#4 Subprograms.** Name editable in the left list; **Add asks Procedure / Function** ([`SubprogramKindDialog`](src/EmberTern.App/Views/SubprogramKindDialog.axaml)) seeding the matching template; kind survives round-trip (`RewriteSubprogramName`).
- **#5 Order preservation.** Add / Move Up / Move Down / Delete regenerate the DECLARE section in the same order — pinned by round-trip tests across variables/cursors/subprograms ([ProcedureEasyModeStructuredTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ProcedureEasyModeStructuredTests.cs)).
- **#6 BEGIN/END highlighting.** `END` was in the XSHD `Function` block (gold), `BEGIN` in `StatementKeyword` (purple). Moved `END` to the statement-keyword class in both [FirebirdSql.xshd](src/EmberTern.App/Assets/FirebirdSql.xshd) + [.Light.xshd](src/EmberTern.App/Assets/FirebirdSql.Light.xshd) → both render the same purple.
- **#7 Occurrence highlighting.** New [`OccurrenceHighlighter`](src/EmberTern.App/Completion/OccurrenceHighlighter.cs) (AvaloniaEdit `IBackgroundRenderer`) boxes every occurrence of the selected identifier; attached via `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach` (procedure editors) + the main SQL/DDL editors. New theme token `OccurrenceHighlightBrush` (both themes).
- **#8 Sub-tab contrast.** `TabItem.sub-tab`: inactive subtle, **selected = full contrast + SemiBold + accent underline**, larger font/padding, clearer hover.
- **#9 Cursor/Subprogram editors** keep the IBExpert list-left/editor-right split with editable names + autocomplete + Ctrl+Click + double-click + Alt+F format (via `SqlEditorBehavior`).

**Known limitations.** (a) Editing a structured cell on an exotic type (e.g. Size on a `TYPE OF` column) normalises it — an explicit user edit, not a silent loss. (b) #2 is RCA + diagnostics only (no behavior change) — the live MON$ capture is needed before deciding any fix. (c) Updated screenshots can't be produced from this environment; visual items (#6/#7/#8) await the user's validation pass.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

118. **Firebird's metadata-update "object in use" check is cross-attachment — an autonomous DDL transaction on a separate connection does NOT isolate it from a working transaction that holds the object.** A selectable-procedure `SELECT * FROM proc(…)` holds the procedure "in use" for its transaction's lifetime; `ALTER PROCEDURE` (even on a fresh `Pooling=false` attachment) fails until that transaction ends. "Isolated metadata compile" only avoids lock contention on the working tx's *data*, not the engine's object-in-use guard. To diagnose which attachment/transaction pins an object, dump `MON$TRANSACTIONS ⋈ MON$ATTACHMENTS` from a transient connection at the moment of failure (gated by `EMBERTERN_TX_DIAG`).

### Refresh-storm fix #2 — scoped post-transaction refresh + Domain/Type sync (shipped 2026-06-17, uncommitted)

Trace-confirmed root cause of the post-commit "transaction cascade" (the user supplied EmberTern + IBExpert traces). **1267 → 1274 tests** (+7). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

**Root cause (definitive, from the trace).** After a transaction settle, `MainWindowViewModel.OnTransactionStateChanged` ran a **blanket `foreach (tab in WorkspaceTabs) RefreshAfterTransactionAsync()`** — a FULL `RefreshStructureAsync` (fields + constraints + indexes + 4 dependency queries + data preview, ~7 heavy `RDB$` queries each) over **EVERY open TableDetail tab**, even tabs completely unrelated to the operation. In the trace, executing/editing a *procedure* triggered a structure refresh of two unrelated open table tabs (`XXX_GG_EMBERTRACE`, `NAGL`) → TRA_99122…99136+, each query an implicit auto-committed transaction, each commit re-firing the user's `ON TRANSACTION_COMMIT` audit trigger (`XXX_WS_TRANS_ON_COMMIT`). IBExpert refreshes ONLY the object it changed (~8 bounded transactions) and never touches unrelated open tabs. NOT infinite recursion — a bounded **fan-out** (N tabs × M queries) amplified by the audit trigger; the implicit metadata-read commits don't re-fire `TransactionStateChanged` (driver-level, not the working-tx `TransactionService`), so it doesn't truly recurse, but the volume reads as a storm.

Why the blanket structure refresh is obsolete: since Phase A, structure DDL (Add/Drop/Move field, constraints, indexes, Compile) runs in an **autonomous** transaction and **self-refreshes at apply-time**. The metadata working tx no longer carries revertible DDL, so "rollback reverts ALTERs → refresh every tab" is dead weight.

**Fix** ([MainWindowViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs)). Replaced the blanket loop with a **pure, scoped selector** `SelectRefreshTargets(refresh, tabs, activeTab)`:
- **Structure** (metadata rollback, e.g. a raw F5 `ALTER`) → refresh ONLY the **active** TableDetail tab (the object the user is on). If the active tab isn't a TableDetail (e.g. a procedure), refresh **nothing** — exactly the user's scenario.
- **DataOnly** (data rollback) → reload the preview ONLY for tabs with **pending data edits** (`TableDetailTabViewModel.HasPendingDataEdits`, set on cell/row edit, cleared on reload/commit) — revert optimistic writes on the edited tab(s) only.
- **None** (commit) → nothing (unchanged).
Plus a re-entrancy guard `_postTxRefreshInFlight` (gotcha #119) so two coalesced settle events can't start overlapping refresh batches; per-VM `RefreshStructureAsync` coalescing (`_refreshInFlight`) still applies underneath.

**Domain → Type field-sync bug** ([ProcedureFieldRowBase.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ProcedureFieldRowBase.cs)). Selecting a Domain blanked the Type column (the old code nulled `BaseType`). Fixed: selecting a domain now **mirrors the domain's resolved type into the Type/Size/Scale cells for display** (`SyncTypeDisplayFromDomain`, guarded by `_syncingType` so it doesn't read as a user "pick a base type" that would clear the domain) — the canonical `TypeText` still becomes the domain NAME (round-trip-safe). A domain-typed variable loaded before the domain list arrives resolves its Type cell once `AvailableDomains` fires `CollectionChanged`. Picking a plain base type over a domain still clears the domain.

**Tests** ([RefreshStormFixTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/RefreshStormFixTests.cs), +7): data-rollback refreshes only edited tabs (not all); metadata-rollback refreshes only the active tab; settle with a non-TableDetail active tab refreshes nothing (the storm scenario); None selects nothing; Domain selection fills the Type cell + keeps the domain canonical; base-type-over-domain clears the domain; domain-typed variable resolves its Type cell when domains arrive after load.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

119. **A transaction settle must NEVER blanket-refresh every open TableDetail tab — scope it to the object that was actually touched.** A full `RefreshStructureAsync` is ~7 heavy `RDB$` queries, EACH an implicit auto-committed transaction. On a database with an `ON TRANSACTION_COMMIT` (or START/ROLLBACK) trigger, every one of those commits re-fires the trigger — so refreshing N unrelated tabs after one settle produces an N×7 transaction storm that re-runs the user's audit trigger N×7 times. Since structure DDL is autonomous + self-refreshing (Phase A), the only legitimate post-settle refreshes are: the **active** tab on a metadata rollback (raw F5 DDL), and **tabs with pending data edits** on a data rollback (revert optimistic writes). Route through a pure `SelectRefreshTargets` selector + a re-entrancy guard; never `foreach (WorkspaceTabs) Refresh…`. This is the deeper, permanent form of gotchas #102/#108/#109 — "refresh only what changed, like IBExpert."

### Editor refinement sprint — unified toolbar, Easy-on-new, modified-row, Compile-gating fix (shipped 2026-06-19)

Closes the editor-experience sprint and establishes the **shared editor standard** all future object editors (Trigger / Function / Package) must follow. **1289 → 1324 tests.** Build clean (0/0), smoke OK, verified live.

**Object editor status (current):**
- **Table Editor** (`TableDetailTabViewModel`) — buffered designer: edit the working model → ⚡ Compile applies the queued DDL atomically (one autonomous tx) → auto-commit. Sub-tabs Pola / Ograniczenia / Indeksy / Zależności / Dane / Opis / DDL. Inline Pola edits (Name/Type/Domain/Size/Scale/NotNull/Default/Description) + Add/Drop/Move field, FK drop; Add/Drop PK/FK/Check/Unique; Add/Drop index + Recompute statistics; Set NULL; editable description. **Compile is dirty-gated** (`CanCompile => executor != null && HasPendingChanges`) and now reliably enables after ANY inline edit (see the fix below).
- **New Table Designer** (`NewTableTabViewModel`) — a workspace tab (not a modal). Editable field grid with per-row cell enable/disable for the Computed-By dependency model; live DDL preview; ⚡ Compile creates the table, refreshes the tree, and reopens it as a TableDetail tab.
- **View Editor** (`ViewDetailTabViewModel`) — Source ⇄ Easy. Easy = editable name + column list over an AS-SELECT body editor; Source = full `CREATE [OR ALTER] VIEW`. Tabs Editor / Fields / Dependencies / Data / Description / DDL. Compile always available when a DDL executor is wired; editable description (`COMMENT ON VIEW`); shared `SqlFormatter`; autocomplete + Ctrl/double-click navigation. **New View starts in Easy** with an editable View-name field.
- **Procedure Editor** (`ProcedureDetailTabViewModel`) — Source ⇄ Easy. Easy = editable name + Input/Output param grids + structured Variables/Cursors/Subprograms over a body-only editor. Tabs Editor / Description / Dependencies / DDL / Result + persistent exec-info bar. Execute on the Data lane (parameterized, typed dialog); Compile always available; Comment/Uncomment Body. **New Procedure starts in Easy** with an editable Procedure-name field.

**Approved UI standards (the editor contract — do not deviate per editor):**
- **Unified 5-section toolbar** in the main app toolbar, identical order for every object editor: `[Mode] | [Main actions] | [Collection: + −  |  ↑ ↓] | [Helper actions] | [Close]`. One shared `StackPanel`; each button gates only via `IsVisible` bound to uniform router flags (`ShowModeSection`, `ShowMainSection`, `ShowCollectionTools`, `ShowCollectionReorder`, `ShowHelperSection`, `IsClosableTabActive`). **No editor introduces its own toolbar element or ordering.**
- **Collection actions live in the toolbar**, grouped `[+ −]` then `[↑ ↓]` with a thin sub-separator (`ShowCollectionReorder`). The whole Collection section + its flanking separator **collapse together** when the active editor/sub-tab exposes no collection. Routing is a single VM-level `ActiveCollection()` switch returning `(Add, Remove, Up?, Down?, Reorder)` — a future editor adds one `case`, not a new toolbar.
- **No in-content collection toolbars.** The per-grid `+ − ↑ ↓` strips inside View/Procedure Easy panels are gone (preserves vertical workspace); the collection routes to the active sub-tab via `ActiveEasyCollectionIndex`.
- **Active tab styling** (bottom-tabs + sub-tabs share one language): `ElevatedPanelBrush` raised segment + `SemiBold` + full-contrast `ForegroundBrush`, `CornerRadius 4`, hover via `HoverOverlayBrush`, FluentTheme `PART_SelectedPipe` hidden. **No accent ring / border / underline / outline, no height increase** — a raised segment, not a highlighted button. (Accent is reserved for connection state, DEV MODE, and primary toolbar actions.)
- **Object-editor conventions:** edit affordances are **hidden** (not greyed) when a capability is absent (read-only system tables); the Source/Easy toggle is the first toolbar element and enabled for every object (incl. new); a new object exposes an editable **name field** in its Easy header (the CREATE header is hidden in Easy, so the name lives there) and supplies the object name on Compile.
- **Revert / Discard button next to Compile (MANDATORY for every object editor).** Each editor has an undo-the-uncompiled-edits button in the Main section beside Compile (`Icon.Undo`, `Button.icon`), and it **MUST confirm** before discarding (destructive `ConfirmRequest` → shared `ConfirmDialog`) so an accidental click never loses work. Two flavours: the **Table designer** (buffered model) discards the in-memory `PendingChanges` and reprojects the grids to catalog truth, no DB round-trip — `DiscardPendingChangesCommand`, gated `CanDiscardPending => HasPendingChanges`; the **source editors** (View/Procedure/Trigger, and future Function/Package) reload the object from the DB via `RefreshAsync` — `RevertChangesCommand`, gated `CanRevertChanges => IsDirty && !IsNew` (a not-yet-created object has no DB state to revert to, so the button disables in the New flow — use Close to abandon). Wiring: the detail VM raises its existing `ConfirmationRequested` event, the factory (`CreateViewDetail`/`CreateProcedureDetail`/`CreateTriggerDetail`/`CreateTableDetail`) hooks it to `MainWindowViewModel.RequestConfirmAsync`. A future editor adds the same command + event + factory wiring + one toolbar button — see gotcha #143.

**Modified-row color (final).** `RowModifiedColor` is **indigo/violet `#407C4DFF`** (~25%, both themes), replacing amber. The pending-state trio reads at a glance — green = added, indigo = modified, red = dropped — none colliding with the blue selection or the amber warning/transaction state. Amber was rejected: a low-alpha WARM wash over the near-black grid composites to brown (reads as a warning, not a pending edit); a cool hue stays true on dark and lands as a soft lavender on light.

**Easy-on-new (View + Procedure).** `CanUseEasyMode => true` for every object (toggle enabled immediately); `New View`/`New Procedure` set `EasyMode = true` so they start in Easy, parsing their seed template into the structured model. A new editable name field (`EditableViewName` / `EditableProcedureName`) sits atop each Easy panel — read-only for existing objects, editable for new — feeding `BuildFullSource` and the reopen-by-name path. The old `if (IsNew) EasyMode = false` guard is removed from both VMs.

**Table Compile-gating fix (the regression you hit).** The Pola **Type** and **Domain** cells are always-visible `ComboBox`es in `IsReadOnly` template columns (the gotcha #56 focus-race workaround), so changing them never fires the DataGrid's `RowEditEnding` → `EnqueueRowEdits` was skipped → no pending change → Compile stayed greyed after a type/domain change. Fix: `FieldRowViewModel` now routes **every** editable-property change through `OnInlineFieldEdited → EnqueueRowEdits`, and `EnqueueRowEdits` is **idempotent per row** (tracks each row's queued statements in `_inlineRowEdits`, replaces on each call) with a re-entrancy guard — so a type/domain change (or any cell) enqueues, an edit-then-revert clears, and successive edits don't duplicate. Text columns + NotNull already worked via `RowEditEnding`.

**Known open items (intentionally NOT in this sprint):**
- **Issue #1 — procedure lock on Execute → Rollback → Compile** (cross-attachment `object in use`): still open; awaits the live `MON$` dump (`EMBERTERN_TX_DIAG=1`). See "Known Open Issues" below.
- **Metadata auto-commit Phases B–D** (SQL-editor metadata DDL → autonomous; remove the now-inert metadata Commit/Rollback infra): held pending live validation; Phase A shipped.
- **Trigger / Function / Package editors:** not built. They must adopt the 5-section toolbar (one `ActiveCollection()` case each), Source/Easy where applicable, and the active-tab styling — no new toolbar pattern.
- **Compile dirty-gating consistency:** Table is dirty-gated; View/Procedure Compile is always-enabled-when-executor-wired (approved). Not unified — deliberate.
- **TPB-profile vestiges**, click-to-change-profile chip, config export/import, `AppearanceSettings` consumer — as previously recorded.

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

124. **An always-visible interactive control (ComboBox/CheckBox) inside an `IsReadOnly` `DataGridTemplateColumn` bypasses the DataGrid edit cycle — `RowEditEnding`/`CellEditEnding` never fire for it.** Any "detect that the user changed this cell" logic hung off the grid's edit events will silently miss those columns. Route change-detection through the row VM's `PropertyChanged` (the bound property DOES update via TwoWay) instead of the grid's edit events. When that detection feeds a queue, make the enqueue idempotent-per-row (track + replace) so the VM-PropertyChanged path and any surviving `RowEditEnding` path can both fire without duplicating, and guard re-entrancy if the handler writes back to the row.

125. **Easy-mode-for-new needs an editable name field because the CREATE header is hidden in Easy.** For an existing object the name is fixed (and the Easy name field is read-only display); for a new object the user must type it somewhere, and Source mode keeps it in the `CREATE …` header text that Easy mode doesn't show. Add an `Editable<Object>Name` property that `BuildFullSource` uses, seed it from the parsed template / catalog name, make `CanUseEasyMode` true for new objects, and start the New flow in Easy. The parser's `Name` flows into the field on every Source→Easy sync so the two stay consistent.

### Branding refresh — new tern logo + icon-only logo-first titlebar (shipped 2026-06-19)

Replaced the official logo artwork and restructured the titlebar branding. Only `MainWindow.axaml` + the three binary assets changed; build clean (0/0), tests unchanged (1324).

**New logo + assets** ([src/EmberTern.App/Assets/Branding/](src/EmberTern.App/Assets/Branding/)). The official logo is a **circular tern-head badge** — a black tern head on an orange disc. It went through several iterations (jagged-crest head → smooth-crest head → round badge); the round composition was kept because it fills a square far better than the head-only variants, which are much wider than tall and looked small in the taskbar against VS/Rider/DataGrip. Source supplied by the user as a genuine RGBA transparent PNG (`ptak_logo_przezroczyste_tlo_v4.png`, 976×973). Regenerated in place (filenames unchanged → zero reference changes): `logo.png` = pristine full-res transparent master; `EmberTern_logo.png` = 256×256; `EmberTern.ico` = 16/24/32/48/64/256, all 32bpp; every asset transparent. The negative-space curl inside the badge is a **transparent cut-out** in the source (not white), so it shows the tile colour through it — dark on dark, light on light; intentional, not a hole to fill.

- **Fit framing (no crop).** `logo.png` is the pristine full master. The square logo + every `.ico` frame **fit the WHOLE artwork** into the square — content-bbox centred, scaled to the larger dimension with a ~2% margin, **nothing clipped**. The round badge is ~square (976×973) so it fills the tile edge-to-edge with no vertical gap — the optical-mass problem of the head-only variants is gone. The user supplies an already-tightly-cropped source, so the generator must NOT add its own bleed/crop: an earlier "balanced ~3% bleed" pass on a head-only variant ate the tail feathers off the right edge and had to be redone (see gotcha #127). Pipeline: in-session PowerShell + System.Drawing (alpha-bbox → centred square window sized to `max(w,h)*1.02` → `HighQualityBicubic` → hand-assembled ICO: `ICONDIR` + `ICONDIRENTRY`×N + PNG payloads, width/height byte 0 for the 256 frame).

**Icon-only, logo-first titlebar** ([MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml)). Removed the permanent "EmberTern" wordmark (`AppTitle` `TextBlock`) from the titlebar — matches every IDE/workbench reference (VS, Rider, DataGrip, VS Code, DBeaver show no product name in chrome; the name still lives in the window title, taskbar, Start menu, About, installer). The logo is now the **absolute first element** of the top bar — a brand mark, not a toolbar button — isolated by the brand divider; the sidebar-toggle moved out of the brand zone into the action zone as the first item after that divider: `[LOGO] | [Panel] [+] [Folder] …` (and `[LOGO] | DB name · DEV MODE | …` when connected). Option A (logo alone before the divider) was chosen over `[LOGO] [Panel] | …` because a divider immediately after the logo is the strongest "brand not button" signal. `AppTitle` VM property retained (drives the OS window title). Pixel recovery: the always-on ~70px wordmark is gone, the sidebar toggle regrouped into the tighter spacing-2 toolbar zone (was spacing-8 brand zone), and the brand panel's spacing 8→6 + right margin 4→2 — freed width flows to the central drag region so the toolbar has more room before the window controls. (Tab strip is a separate row — this is horizontal titlebar recovery, not vertical.)

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

126. **A ChatGPT-exported "transparent" PNG may actually be opaque RGB with a baked-in checkerboard — verify the PNG colour type before trusting it as a transparent source.** A first-attempt source looked transparent (gray checkerboard) but was PNG colour type **2 (RGB, no alpha, no `tRNS`)** — the "transparency" was a literal light checkerboard (~`#F2F2F2`/`#FEFEFE`) painted into the pixels (corner pixels `A=255`). Generating icons from it puts a checkerboard square behind the art; keying the background out leaves fringing on the soft orange glow edges (cost a rework). Before using any "transparent" PNG as an asset source, check IHDR colour type (`bytes[25]`: 2=RGB, 6=RGBA) and corner-pixel alpha — don't trust the visual checkerboard. The shipped logo uses a genuine RGBA source (colour type 6, transparent corners); no keying.

127. **When the supplied logo source is already cropped, the icon generator must FIT it whole — never add a bleed/crop.** Fitting = centre the content bbox and scale to the larger dimension with a small (~2%) margin, accepting the vertical gap for a wide mark. An automated "fill the square" bleed crop eats the extremities (here the tail feathers) of an already-tight source. Only apply a bleed crop to a source with generous margins you're deliberately trimming — when in doubt, fit (no clip).

### DDL statement splitter — CASE…END + opaque-run awareness (shipped 2026-06-19)

Bugfix: compiling a valid Firebird procedure containing a `CASE … END` expression failed with `Dynamic SQL Error / SQL error code = -104 / Unexpected end of command`, while IBExpert and the engine accepted it. **1324 → 1330 tests** (+6). Build clean (0/0), all green. Scope is `FirebirdDdlExecutor.SplitStatements` only — no transaction/lane/architecture change.

**Root cause.** The bug was NOT in the PSQL parser or the formatter — it was in `FirebirdDdlExecutor.SplitStatements`, the routine that splits a multi-statement DDL payload on top-level `;` before sending each statement to Firebird (the engine rejects multiple statements in one `FbCommand`). It tracked block nesting by `BEGIN => +1 / END => -1` only. A body containing `(case when … else … end)` decremented the counter on the CASE's `END` with no matching `BEGIN`, so a later `suspend;` was read as a top-level statement separator → the procedure was cut before its closing `end` and a truncated DDL reached the server.

**Fix.** `SplitStatements` now (a) counts **CASE as an opener** alongside BEGIN (both terminate with END, so the nesting stays balanced — `END` closes whichever is innermost), and (b) copies **opaque runs verbatim without inspecting them** — string literals (`'…'` incl. `''` escape), quoted identifiers (`"…"`), line comments (`-- …`), block comments (`/* … */`) — so their `;` / BEGIN / END / CASE tokens never affect splitting or nesting. Word-boundary, case-insensitive keyword matching (same `IsWordBoundary`/`IsWordEndAt`/`Matches` helpers). Same structural insight as formatter gotcha #117 (a `CASE…END` has no `;`, but here the fix is a nesting counter rather than statement-scope collection, because the splitter works token-by-token, not unit-by-unit).

**Tests** ([DdlGeneratorTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/DdlGeneratorTests.cs)): `SplitStatements_ProcedureWithCaseInBody_StaysOneStatement` (the regression pin — `FOR SELECT … (CASE WHEN … END) … INTO … DO SUSPEND;` stays one statement, ends at the closing `end`), `_NestedCaseAndBegin_BalancesNesting` (CASE inside an IF-BEGIN block), `_DeeplyNestedCase_BalancesNesting` (CASE-within-CASE), `_SemicolonInsideStringLiteral_DoesNotSplit`, `_KeywordsInsideStringOrComment_DoNotAffectNesting`, `_DoubledQuoteEscapeWithKeyword_StaysOpaque` (`'select ''begin'' …'` — the `''` escape doesn't terminate the literal early and the inner keyword stays opaque). Existing empty-segment / whitespace / BEGIN-END-nesting tests unchanged.

**Follow-up (out of scope this session).** Sweep every other place that analyses PSQL tokens for the same "counting `END` without distinguishing its context" defect class — the body scanner's subprogram skipping (BEGIN/END nesting) is a documented known-limitation candidate.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

128. **DDL statement splitting cannot rely on BEGIN/END counting alone — `CASE … END` introduces `END` tokens that are not block terminators.** When splitting a multi-statement Firebird PSQL payload on top-level `;`, a naive `BEGIN +1 / END -1` counter is corrupted by `CASE … END` (the CASE's `END` drops the counter with no matching `BEGIN`), so a later in-body `;` is mistaken for a statement separator and the procedure is truncated before its final `END` → `Unexpected end of command`. The robust splitter: count CASE as an opener (END closes whichever opener is innermost), AND skip opaque runs entirely — string literals, quoted identifiers, line comments, block comments — so their `;`/BEGIN/END/CASE tokens are never inspected. This is the same structural class of bug as formatter gotcha #117; audit any code that counts `END` tokens for the same context-blindness. **Generalized + consolidated into a shared scanner — see gotcha #129.**

### CASE-aware block-scanner consolidation (shipped 2026-06-19)

Audit-driven follow-up to #128. An audit of every self-rolled token scanner / nesting tracker found the same "count `END` without counting `CASE`" defect in three more places — one a real, undocumented bug. Consolidated the CASE-aware counting into ONE shared primitive and rerouted all BEGIN/END region scanners through it. **1330 → 1338 tests** (+8). Build clean (0/0), all green. Scope: the shared helper + the three call sites only — no refactoring beyond that.

**Audit verdict (full sweep).** Safe by construction (never count `END`): `SqlScanHelpers.ReadParenBlock`/`ReadUntilSemicolon`/`SplitTopLevelCommas` (paren-depth, string/comment-aware), `ProcedureSignatureParser` + `ViewSignatureParser` (header-only; body taken verbatim), `ProcedureBodySplitter.Split` (body verbatim from `BEGIN` onward, no END-matching), `SqlStatementClassifier` (first keyword only), `SqlAliasResolver` (paren-depth, #18), `FirebirdDdlReader` (emits/reads `RDB$` source verbatim). Safe by design: `SqlFormatter` PSQL path (#117 — statement-scope collection to top-level `;`, so a `CASE…END` is swallowed whole and the BEGIN/END loop never sees its `END`). **Defective (BEGIN/END counter, no CASE):** `FirebirdDdlExecutor.SplitStatements` (already fixed — #128), `ProcedureBodyScanner.FindOuterBodyContent` (**real bug** — Comment Body / Uncomment Body wrapped only up to the first `CASE`'s `END`, leaving the rest of the body dangling; undocumented), `ProcedureBodyScanner.SkipSubprogramBody` + `ProcedureBodySplitter.SkipSubprogramBody` (documented limitation — truncated a local subprogram declaration containing a top-level `CASE`, corrupting the Source→Easy round-trip).

**Fix.** New shared scanner in [SqlScanHelpers.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Sql/SqlScanHelpers.cs): a private `TryScanBeginEndBlock` (counts `BEGIN`+`CASE` as openers — CASE only once inside the outer `BEGIN` — `END` as the closer; skips string literals + comments) feeding two public methods — `FindOuterBeginEndContent` (content range of the outermost `BEGIN…END`) and `SkipToEndOfBlock` (advance the cursor past the next `BEGIN…END` + optional `;`). Rerouted all three call sites to them: `ProcedureBodyScanner.FindOuterBodyContent` → `FindOuterBeginEndContent`; both `SkipSubprogramBody` (in `ProcedureBodyScanner` and `ProcedureBodySplitter`) → `SkipToEndOfBlock`. The two now-obsolete "CASE…END limitation" doc comments were removed. The `SqlFormatter` (#117) was intentionally NOT rerouted — its statement-scope approach is already correct and a different shape; consolidating it would be a refactor beyond scope.

**Tests** ([ProcedureSourceTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ProcedureSourceTests.cs) +6, [ProcedureBodyModelTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ProcedureBodyModelTests.cs) +2): top-level `CASE` in body spans the whole body (FindOuterBodyContent + CommentBody — `suspend` lands inside the `/* … */`, the regression pin); nested `CASE`; BEGIN/END/CASE keywords inside a string literal + line comment ignored; local procedure with `CASE` captures the full body (`END; END`); local function with `CASE`; splitter captures the full subprogram declaration + keeps the executable body separate; function-with-CASE round-trips through `BuildProcedureBody`.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

129. **PSQL block-region scanning is a recurring defect class — never hand-roll a BEGIN/END counter; route through the shared `SqlScanHelpers` CASE-aware scanner (or avoid END-counting entirely).** Any scanner that delimits a `BEGIN…END` region by counting `END` tokens is wrong unless it ALSO counts `CASE` as an opener and skips opaque runs (strings, quoted idents, line/block comments) — because `CASE … END` produces an `END` with no matching `BEGIN` (gotchas #117/#128 were two instances; the audit found two more). Two correct strategies exist in this codebase: **(a)** the shared CASE-aware counter — `SqlScanHelpers.FindOuterBeginEndContent` (region range) / `SqlScanHelpers.SkipToEndOfBlock` (advance past a block); **(b)** avoid END-counting entirely — collect to the top-level `;` (the `SqlFormatter` PSQL path) or take the region verbatim to its terminator (`ProcedureSignatureParser`/`ViewSignatureParser`/`ProcedureBodySplitter.Split`). **Rule:** new code that needs a `BEGIN…END` boundary MUST use (a) or (b) — do not write a fresh `BEGIN +1 / END -1` loop. If you must (some new shape the helpers don't cover), extend `SqlScanHelpers` so there's still ONE implementation, and pin it with a CASE-in-body test.

### Data-loss protection — Increment 1: WorkGuard + ChoiceDialog (shipped 2026-06-19)

First of a two-increment data-loss-prevention model (Increment 2 = auto-draft, separate session). One unified guard replaces the scattered, partial confirmations so the user can't silently lose work on **tab close**, **disconnect**, or **app close**. **1338 → 1356 tests** (+18). Build clean (0/0), smoke OK.

**Two distinct kinds of "work to lose"** (the design's foundation):
- **Unsaved CODE** (lives only in the tab VM): a New Table form, a New View/Procedure source, a modified-but-not-compiled View/Procedure source, or a TableDetail designer with queued `PendingChanges`. Lost on tab/app close. Can only be "saved" by Compile (or, in Increment 2, by an auto-draft).
- **Active transaction** (lives on the server, dies with the session): any data/metadata working tx. **Cannot** survive a restart, so it always needs a conscious Commit/Rollback **now**.

**`ChoiceDialog`** ([ChoiceDialogViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ChoiceDialogViewModel.cs) + [ChoiceDialog.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/ChoiceDialog.axaml)) — the N-button sibling of the binary `ConfirmDialog`. `ChoiceRequest { Title, Message, ChoiceOption[] }` → `Task<string?>` (chosen `ChoiceOption.Id`, or null when dismissed = cancel). Each option carries `IsDefault` (primary style + Enter), `IsCancel` (Esc / window-close maps here), `IsDestructive`. **Additive** — the binary `ConfirmDialog` and its 12 callers are untouched (extending it would have broken their `Task<bool>` contract; gotcha — see #131). Wired via `MainWindowViewModel.ChoiceRequested` (no handler → null = cancel; safe because the branches using it only fire with a live transaction, which tests never have).

**Unified unsaved-work model** ([IUnsavedWorkSource.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/IUnsavedWorkSource.cs)) — `IUnsavedWorkSource { UnsavedWorkItem? GetUnsavedWork(); }` implemented by NewTable / ViewDetail / ProcedureDetail / TableDetail VMs (4 impls → interface justified). `UnsavedWorkItem(UnsavedWorkKind {NewObject, ModifiedSource, PendingStructure}, string Label)` — Label is a ready-to-show summary line (object name included). `WorkspaceTabViewModel.UnsavedWork` switches on `Kind` and delegates to the child. TableDetail reports only **structural** `PendingChanges` (pending DATA edits are transaction work, surfaced by the tx guard — closing one table tab doesn't lose them; the tx keeps them).

**Dirty tracking for View/Procedure** (they had none). An **explicit `IsDirty` flag + `_suppressDirty` guard** (the project's `_suppress*` idiom), NOT a baseline string-compare — the editable source has two representations (Source text vs. the Easy structured model) and a pure Source⇄Easy toggle must NOT read as an edit. Programmatic writes (LoadAsync / RefreshAsync / `OnEasyModeChanged`) are wrapped in `_suppressDirty`; genuine user edits flip it via `MarkDirty` (hooked to `OnSourceTextChanged` / `OnEditableViewName|ProcedureNameChanged` / `OnEditableBody|ExecutableBodyChanged` + the Easy collections' `CollectionChanged` + each added row's `PropertyChanged`). Cleared on load and after a successful compile. The ctor starts suppressed (so ctor field assignments don't mark dirty) and releases at ctor end. A fresh **New** View/Procedure is dirty after the template seed, so `NewView`/`NewProcedure` call `detail.ClearDirty()` after seeding → an untouched new tab is clean and won't prompt; the first real edit flips it back.

**One `WorkGuard`** on [MainWindowViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs), three entry points, one `CollectUnsavedWork()` aggregation over the open tabs:
- **Tab close** (`RequestCloseTabAsync`) — was NewTable-only; now prompts **Discard / Cancel** (binary `ConfirmDialog`) for ANY tab whose `UnsavedWork` is non-null. Clean tabs (and DDL / read-only tabs, reopenable from the tree) close silently. Programmatic closes (post-compile, delete-table cleanup) call `CloseTab` directly and never prompt. Per the user's call: tab close NEVER auto-compiles — Compile is a conscious DB op, Close is a UI op.
- **Disconnect** (`ConfirmDisconnectAsync`) — active transaction → `ChoiceDialog`: **Commit / Roll back / Cancel**, **default Roll back** (settles the chosen lanes via the existing `CommitAllAsync`/`RollbackAllAsync` before disconnecting; "Force" was dropped — disconnect ends the session and Firebird rolls back anyway, so it added only confusion). Uncompiled tab work with no tx → binary Discard / Cancel (Increment 2's drafts will make this survive, at which point disconnect won't lose code).
- **App close** (`TryCloseApplicationAsync`) — `OnWindowClosing` is now `async void`: it sets `e.Cancel = true`, awaits the guard, and re-closes (via a `_forceClose` flag) only if allowed. Active tx or unsaved code → `ChoiceDialog`: **Cancel** (default/Esc) / **Discard and exit** (rolls back active transactions). No "Commit all on exit" — a blind whole-session commit is dangerous; the user commits deliberately per-connection. Nothing unsaved → closes immediately (no dialog).

**Strings** — a `Data-loss WorkGuard` block in [UiStrings.cs](src/EmberTern.App/UiStrings.cs) (Unsaved*Format summary lines, CloseTabUnsavedConfirm*, DisconnectChoice*, DisconnectUnsaved*, ExitUnsaved*). The legacy binary `DisconnectConfirm*` consts are kept but unreferenced (noted in place).

**Tests** ([DataLossGuardTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/DataLossGuardTests.cs), +18): ChoiceDialog Choose/flags; per-VM `GetUnsavedWork` (NewTable content gate, TableDetail PendingChanges, View/Procedure existing-modified → ModifiedSource, new-untouched-after-ClearDirty → clean, new-edited → NewObject, mode-toggle not dirty); `CollectUnsavedWork` lists only dirty tabs; tab-close clean/dirty-cancel/dirty-confirm; app-close nothing/discard/cancel/dismiss; disconnect-with-uncompiled-no-tx prompts. (Disconnect-with-tx and the dialogs' runtime binding need a live FB / display — smoke-deferred.)

**Increment 2 (auto-draft) — NOT YET BUILT (next session).** Approved design + the 5 user corrections: crash/power-loss-safe debounced autosave of uncompiled work to a separate **`drafts.dat`** (reusing `SettingsFileContainer` + DPAPI), `IDraftSource` on the 4 VMs, **TableDetail `PendingChanges` ARE drafted** with on-restore validation against the current schema + a warning for changes that can't be applied (#1), **draft stores the baseline object DDL/version** → warn "draft created on an older version" if the DB object changed since (#4), **draft auto-deleted after a successful Compile** (no historical accumulation, #3), and — explicit (#2) — **NO recovery screen**: silently re-open the drafted tabs on startup, show a one-shot toast **"Recovered N draft(s) from the previous session"**, and mark recovered tabs with a `●` / "Recovered" marker until their first Compile or manual close. Once drafts exist, the app-close guard relaxes so uncompiled code no longer blocks exit (only transactions do); disconnect likewise stops losing code. Hooks already in place: the same `IsDirty`/`MarkDirty` edit points feed both the guard and (next) the autosave debounce.

**Known minor gap** (closed by Increment 2's same hooks): a *rename-only* edit of a View column row / Procedure local row flips dirty via the row `PropertyChanged` subscription — covered; a missed signal would only drop one prompt, never cause silent loss.

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

130. **To prompt asynchronously during window close, make `OnWindowClosing` `async void`, set `e.Cancel = true` BEFORE the first `await`, run the guard, then re-`Close()` behind a `_forceClose` flag.** Avalonia reads `e.Cancel` when the synchronous portion of the handler returns — an `async void` handler runs synchronously up to the first await, so setting `e.Cancel = true` there keeps the window open while the awaited guard/dialog runs. If the guard allows the close, set a `_forceClose` field and call `Close()` again; the re-entrant handler skips the guard and runs the persist path. Never try to await inside a synchronous closing handler without cancelling first — the window tears down underneath you.

131. **For a >2-outcome confirmation, add a parallel `ChoiceDialog`, don't widen the binary `ConfirmDialog`.** `ConfirmDialog` is `Task<bool>` with 12 callers; changing its result type or button model to express Commit/Rollback/Cancel would touch every caller and lose the clean boolean semantics. The N-button `ChoiceDialog` (`ChoiceRequest` → `Task<string?>` of the chosen option Id; null = dismissed = cancel) sits beside it via its own `ChoiceRequested` event, exactly mirroring the `ConfirmationRequested` wiring. Reuse-before-create still holds: only reach for `ChoiceDialog` when the decision genuinely has three+ outcomes (disconnect Commit/Rollback/Cancel; app-close Cancel/Discard) — keep binary confirms on `ConfirmDialog`.

### UI-state persistence tightening — per-tab editor state + globals (shipped 2026-06-20)

Audit-driven sweep of workspace/settings persistence after the reported bug "View set to Easy Mode reopens in Source after restart". The audit found the bug was one instance of a class: `WorkspaceTab` had no slot for any per-tab editor UI state, so Source/Easy mode + active sub-tabs were lost across restart (Procedure papered over it with a *global* preference; View had nothing). Fixed with a **hybrid model** (user-chosen): a global preference seeds a freshly-opened object; a workspace-restored tab restores its own per-tab value. **1338 → 1366 tests**. Build clean (0/0), smoke OK.

**Schema** ([WorkspaceState.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Workspace/WorkspaceState.cs)):
- `WorkspaceTab` gained nullable per-tab UI fields — `EasyMode` (View+Procedure), `ActiveSubTabIndex` (all three detail editors' main tab), `ActiveInnerSubTabIndex` (Procedure's Easy-collection tab Input/Output/Variables/Cursors/Subprograms; Table's Constraints sub-tab PK/FK/Check/Unique), `GridEditMode` (Table Pola edit toggle). Nullable ⇒ a legacy tab falls back to the global default.
- `WorkspaceState` gained globals — `ViewEasyMode` (hybrid default for new views, mirrors the existing `ProcedureEasyMode`), `ResultsMaximized`, `BottomPanelTabIndex` (Results/Messages/Output).

**Hybrid wiring** ([MainWindowViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs)): new `ViewEasyModePreference` + `OnViewDetailPropertyChanged` (mirror of the Procedure preference). `CreateViewDetail` seeds the global preference for freshly-opened views. `SnapshotCurrentTabs` writes each detail tab's live UI state; `LoadWorkspaceFor` re-applies it after creating the VM — **per-tab value wins over the global default**. `CaptureWorkspace`/`RestoreWorkspace` round-trip `ViewEasyMode` + `BottomPanelTabIndex` (`SelectedBottomTabIndex`); `ProcedureEasyMode` + `QueryPanelVisible` already did.

**Results-maximized** ([MainWindow.axaml.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs)): captured in `OnWindowClosing` (`state.ResultsMaximized = _resultsMaximized`), restored in `ApplyLayoutFromPendingRestore` (sets `_resultsMaximized` + `SetResultsMaximized`; row sizing applies via `ApplyResultsRowForActiveTab` when the Query tab activates). Same code-behind-owned-layout pattern as `WindowBounds` / `SidebarWidth` / `ResultsPanelHeight`.

**Dead code removed**: `AppearanceSettings` (the `ThemeVariant`/`AccentColor` stub + `UserSettings.Appearance` + its round-trip test assertions). It was persistable-but-never-read/written (the theme toggle just flips `RequestedThemeVariant` with no persistence); the user opted not to wire theme persistence, so it's gone. `UserSettings` now holds only `GridProfiles`. JSON back-compat is automatic (an old `"Appearance"` key is ignored on read).

**Audit verdict (Capture/Restore symmetry + dead fields):** every `WorkspaceState` field is now both captured and restored. No asymmetry, no dead fields. `WorkspaceTab.ConnectionProfileId` is threaded to the factory but not yet consumed for behavior (V2 source-connection chip) — intentional metadata, not dead.

**Deliberately NOT persisted** (confirmed out of scope with the user): caret / scroll position, Data/Result pagination page+sort (re-fetched), metadata-category expansion inside a connection (eager-loaded), auto-reconnect (user logs in manually), uncompiled New-object / pending-designer content (Increment 2 auto-draft territory).

**Tests** ([WorkspaceUiStatePersistenceTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/WorkspaceUiStatePersistenceTests.cs) + [WorkspaceStoreTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/WorkspaceStoreTests.cs)): store-level round-trip of the per-tab + global fields; VM capture for View/Procedure/Table; VM restore-apply for View/Table; per-tab-beats-global override; global-prefs round-trip; fresh-open-uses-global; and a full **end-to-end disk round-trip** (`EndToEnd_FullScenario_SurvivesDiskRoundTrip`) that builds the user's exact smoke scenario — 2 views (Easy+Source), 2 procedures (Easy on Variables + Source), a table (Constraints→Unique, Grid Edit on), Messages bottom tab, Results maximized — persists through the real `WorkspaceStore`, reloads in a fresh VM, asserts every state came back. Verification caveat: Procedure's restore-apply is covered with the Query tab kept active (the detail tabs' lazy load + procedure domain-load need a live `FbConnection`, so a headless test can't activate them); the true GUI click-through against the live FB is the user's manual check.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

132. **Per-tab editor UI state belongs in `WorkspaceTab`, not in a global preference — use the hybrid rule (global seeds new, per-tab restores reopened).** When an editor surface (View/Procedure/Table detail) has view-state the user expects to survive restart (Source/Easy mode, active sub-tab, grid-edit toggle), add nullable fields to `WorkspaceTab`, write them in `SnapshotCurrentTabs`, and re-apply them in `LoadWorkspaceFor` AFTER the factory applied the global default — so a restored tab keeps its own state while a freshly-opened object uses the latest global preference. A single global preference (the original Procedure approach) cannot represent two open objects in different modes and silently rewrites what every reopened tab restores as. Code-behind-owned *layout* flags (window bounds, sidebar, results height/maximized) stay in `WorkspaceState` globals set in `OnWindowClosing` / read in `ApplyLayoutFromPendingRestore`, separate from VM-owned per-tab state.

### Object Explorer performance — Etap 1: lazy COUNT-only load + debounce + local filter + type-ahead (shipped 2026-06-20)

Audit-first sprint on the metadata tree's lag/scroll/filter behaviour for large schemas (the user's FB5 ERP: ~2356 tables + thousands of indexes/domains/etc.). **Diagnosis came before code** (see the audit in the session): the felt lag was ~80 % self-inflicted (eager full-load + per-keystroke full-tree filter), and only the **scrollbar** problem is a genuine `TreeView` control limitation. Etap 1 fixes the self-inflicted costs without a control migration; the `TreeDataGrid` question is **parked** for a separate architectural sprint (decision: measure after Etap 1, migrate only if scroll still misbehaves on very large expanded categories). **1366 → 1401 tests** (+35). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

**Root causes found (with locations):**
- **Eager full-load on connect** — [ConnectionNodeViewModel.LoadCategoriesAsync](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ConnectionNodeViewModel.cs) loaded ALL 13 categories' full leaf lists sequentially, creating one `MetadataNodeViewModel` per object → ~5–15k VMs at connect before anything was usable; `SqlFor` has no row cap. Plus [LoadGroupAsync](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MetadataExplorerViewModel.cs) called `ApplyFilter()` after EACH category → ~13× full sweeps during connect.
- **Per-keystroke full-tree filter** — `TextBox.Text` (TwoWay) writes on every char → `OnFilterTextChanged → ApplyFilter` with no debounce; `ApplyFilter` iterated all loaded leaves AND auto-expanded every matching loaded group → realize-storm. The eager-load made ALL groups loaded, so filtering expanded everything.
- **Scrollbar jump** — nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` inside `TreeView` (`TreeView.ItemsPanel` + `TreeViewItem.ItemsPanel` styles in [MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml)). Avalonia estimates the outer scroll extent across nested VSPs; a 2000-child expanded category makes the estimate wrong → thumb size/position wrong, jumps. **Control-level, not fixable in our code** — only mitigated by fewer realized rows (which lazy-load delivers).

**What shipped:**
- **COUNT-only on connect, lists on first expand.** New [FirebirdMetadataReader.CountAsync](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdMetadataReader.cs) + `CountSqlFor` (a `SELECT COUNT(*)` mirror of `SqlFor`, no ORDER BY/TRIM). `LoadCategoriesAsync` now loops `metadata.LoadCountAsync(cat)` (one scalar per category) → the user sees `Tables (2356)`, `Views (215)`, … immediately; the leaf list for a category loads on its first expand via the pre-existing `OnIsExpandedChanged → LoadGroupAsync` path. **Domain count must exclude anonymous backing-domains** (`NOT STARTING WITH 'RDB$'`) or it reports thousands more than the displayed user-domain list (which `IsSystemName` strips client-side) — gotcha #133. `RefreshAsync` now reloads loaded/expanded groups in full and just re-fetches the COUNT for the rest (`ResetGroupToPlaceholder` removed).
- **Filter debounce ~350 ms.** `MetadataExplorerViewModel` schedules `ApplyFilter` through a lazily-created `DispatcherTimer`, with a try/catch fallback to synchronous apply when there's no dispatcher loop (unit tests / headless). `ApplyFilter` + `ApplyFilterToGroup` made `internal` for deterministic testing.
- **Local filter, no whole-tree auto-expand.** With lazy-load, on a fresh connect zero categories are loaded, so filtering can't realize a storm; `ApplyFilterToGroup` only auto-expands LOADED groups that have matches (cheap — only user-opened categories) and **never force-loads/expands an unloaded category** (it stays visible so the user can expand it to load+filter). `ApplyFilter` cost is now ∝ loaded leaves, not total objects.
- **IBExpert-style type-ahead.** Filter-independent: with focus on the tree, typed letters accumulate into a transient buffer (~900 ms idle-reset, Escape cancels) and jump to the next node whose name starts with it, searching forward from the current selection and wrapping. Pure logic in the VM ([BuildTypeAheadIndex](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MetadataExplorerViewModel.cs) / `FindTypeAheadIndex` / `NodeSearchText` — unit-tested); the View ([MainWindow.axaml.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs) `OnSidebarTypeAhead`) tunnels `TextInput`/`KeyDown`, expands the match's ancestor path, selects + `BringIntoView` + focus (posted at Background priority so the container is realized). Search scope = every LOADED node (descends into loaded-but-collapsed categories so a leaf there is reachable and its path expands); unloaded categories have no real leaves to find. Fresh single letter searches exclusively (repeating a letter cycles); extending the buffer searches inclusively (refine keeps the current match). **Known limit:** `BringIntoView` on a far-off virtualized item is best-effort (the container may not be realized) — selection still updates.
- **Before/after instrumentation.** New [PerfTrace](src/EmberTern.App/Diagnostics/PerfTrace.cs) (env-gated by `EMBERTERN_PERF_DIAG`, logs to `%TEMP%\EmberTern-debug.log`): `category-load` line = connect→usable-tree time (all COUNTs) + managed-heap KB; `group-load` line = first-expansion cost + leaf count + heap. Procedure to quantify the gain on the live FB schema is in the file header. Can't be run from this environment (needs the live DB); the analytical gain is ~10k leaf-VMs-at-connect → 13 scalar COUNTs, lists realized on demand + virtualized.

**Tests** ([MetadataReaderTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/MetadataReaderTests.cs) +9 `CountSqlFor`/`CountAsync` shape; [TreePerfTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/TreePerfTests.cs) +12 type-ahead find/index/NodeSearchText, local-filter `ApplyFilterToGroup`, lazy `LoadCountAsync` no-op). The type-ahead View wiring + scroll are code-behind (not unit-tested, per precedent).

**Deferred to a future architectural sprint (parked, not abandoned):** if the scrollbar still jumps on very large expanded categories after Etap 1, migrate the sidebar from `TreeView` to **`TreeDataGrid`** (`HierarchicalTreeDataGridSource` flattens visible rows into one virtualized stream → accurate extent, smooth scroll). Cost: NuGet (verify Avalonia 12.0.x compat), rewrite templates (mixed Folder/Connection/Metadata node types), drag&drop (gotcha #40), context-menus, selection, expand persistence. Also a future candidate: a real cross-category **server-side search** (the filter currently only searches loaded categories — type-ahead + lazy-load are the Etap-1 answer to "find an object fast").

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

133. **A `SELECT COUNT(*)` shown before the list must replicate the list's client-side filters, or the count diverges from what the user later sees.** `ListAsync` strips `RDB$`/`MON$`/`SEC$`-prefixed names client-side (`IsSystemName`). For most kinds the `COALESCE(RDB$SYSTEM_FLAG,0)=0` predicate already excludes those, so a plain `COUNT(*)` matches — but **Domain** reads `RDB$FIELDS`, which holds one anonymous `RDB$xxx` backing-domain per inline column type; a plain count there reports thousands more than the displayed user-domain list. The count query must add `AND RDB$FIELD_NAME NOT STARTING WITH 'RDB$'`. `LoadGroupAsync` overwrites `Count` with the real list size on expand, so any residual hair-thin edge (null/empty names) self-corrects — but the *pre-expand* count must still be honest. Rule: when adding a COUNT-before-list, mirror every filter the list applies, and prove the catalog table doesn't carry extra rows the list hides.

134. **Lazy COUNT-only load is what makes the existing filter safe — the filter code was never the real problem, the eager-load was.** The metadata filter already (a) cost ∝ loaded leaves (unloaded groups hold one placeholder child) and (b) only auto-expanded LOADED groups. The "filter expands the whole tree and lags" symptom came entirely from eager-load making ALL categories loaded at connect. Once connect fetches only counts (groups stay unloaded with a placeholder), the same filter touches almost nothing and can't realize a storm. Lesson: before rewriting a hot path, check whether an upstream eager-materialization is what makes it hot — fixing the materialization can fix the symptom without touching the path. (Avalonia `TreeView` + nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` still estimates scroll extent poorly for huge expanded branches — that's the one piece lazy-load only mitigates, not cures; `TreeDataGrid` is the cure if ever needed.) **Superseded in part by the Etap 1 follow-up below** — gotcha #134's "lazy-load makes the filter fine as-is" was incomplete: with lazy-load the filter has *nothing loaded to search*, so it needed a session name cache to show per-category match counts. See #135.

### Object Explorer performance — Etap 1 follow-up: IBExpert filter + incremental type-ahead + expand-bug fix (shipped 2026-06-20)

Four UX corrections after live testing of Etap 1. **1366 → 1402 tests.** Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK. The unifying insight: lazy-load means **nothing is loaded to search**, so both the filter and type-ahead need a **session object-name cache** (object NAMES only — strings, not VMs; cheap memory, no layout cost) built lazily on first filter/search via `ListAsync` per category, cached for the session, invalidated on disconnect / reload / refresh ([MetadataExplorerViewModel.EnsureNameCacheAsync / InvalidateNameCache](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MetadataExplorerViewModel.cs)). Connect itself stays COUNT-only (cache builds only when the user actually filters or types — not at connect).

1. **Filter — IBExpert behaviour (per-category match counts, hide zeros).** `ApplyFilterAsync` ensures the name cache, then per category sets a new `MetadataNodeViewModel.FilterMatchCount` and `IsVisible`: a category with matches shows `Views (1)` and stays visible; a category with **zero matches hides**. Match count for an un-expanded category comes from the cache (no list load); a loaded category also hides its non-matching leaves in place. `DisplayLabel` shows the **match** count while filtering, the **total** when cleared (base `Count` is untouched). So typing `wid` immediately shows e.g. `Views (1)` / `Procedures (3)` with everything else hidden — no manual expanding to discover where matches are. `CountMatches` is the pure, unit-tested counter.
2. **Type-ahead — true incremental search.** Replaced the per-char "find next from current selection" (which read like independent jumps) with **one growing buffer matched from the top of the tree-ordered index every keystroke** (`FindFirstMatch`): `K → KO → KON → KONT → KONTR` keeps converging on `KONTRAHENCI`, never an independent jump. ~1 s idle reset; Escape cancels. (Same model as Explorer/VS incremental search; the "repeat-a-letter-to-cycle" behaviour was intentionally dropped — the user wanted incremental, not cycling.)
3. **Type-ahead searches the WHOLE structure, incl. never-expanded categories.** The searchable index (`BuildFullTypeAheadIndexAsync`) is built from the name cache (all object names) + connection/category labels — so a match in a category that was never expanded is found. `ResolveTypeAheadAsync` then **loads only the owning category** (on-demand `LoadGroupAsync`), and the View expands ONLY the ancestor path (folder/connection/category) + selects + `BringIntoView` — the rest of the tree is untouched. Resolution is async (the View guards with a generation counter so a stale keystroke's result is dropped).
4. **Bug fix — expanding one category no longer expands others (while filtering).** Root cause: `LoadGroupAsync` ended with a **global** `ApplyFilter()`, which auto-expanded every LOADED matching group. So expanding `Functions` re-ran the global filter and re-expanded its already-loaded siblings. Fix: (a) `LoadGroupAsync` now filters ONLY the group it just loaded (`ApplyFilterToGroup(group, …)`), never the whole tree; (b) the filter **never auto-expands** anything — a category opens only by the user's explicit action. Opening one branch can't change another branch's expand state.

**Tests** ([TreePerfTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/TreePerfTests.cs) rewritten): `FindFirstMatch` (incremental convergence, case-insensitive, no-match), `CountMatches`, `NodeSearchText`, `BuildFullTypeAheadIndexAsync` (connection rows when disconnected), `ApplyFilterToGroup` (loaded → match count + hide non-matches + **no auto-expand**; no-match hides; clear restores total label; unloaded never auto-expands), lazy `LoadCountAsync` no-op. The cache-backed filter-of-unloaded-categories and the type-ahead category-load-on-hit need a live FB (the name cache requires a connection) → smoke-tested, consistent with the codebase's DB-path precedent.

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

135. **Lazy tree-load means filter and type-ahead have nothing loaded to search — give them a shared session name cache (names, not VMs).** Deferring leaf-VM creation to first-expand is right for connect speed, but it leaves the search features blind: a filter can't show `Views (1)` for an un-expanded category, and type-ahead can't find an object you never expanded. The fix is a `Dictionary<categoryVM, IReadOnlyList<string>>` of object NAMES, built lazily on first filter/type-ahead (one `ListAsync` per category, strings only — no layout cost), cached for the session, invalidated on disconnect/reload/refresh, deduped via an `EnsureNameCacheAsync()` task (gotcha #23). The filter counts matches from it; type-ahead builds its searchable index from it and loads only the matched category's leaf VMs on a hit. Connect stays COUNT-only — the cache builds only when the user actually searches. This corrects gotcha #134: lazy-load alone did NOT make the filter "fine"; it made it blind, and the cache is what restores cross-category search without re-introducing the eager VM-load.

136. **Contextual type-ahead = match the growing buffer INCLUSIVE-from-anchor with wrap, NOT from the top of the index.** (Supersedes the original "from the top" advice — that was a stepping stone, kept only as the no-anchor fallback.) The incremental growing buffer (`K→KO→KON→KONT→KONTR`, ~1 s idle reset, no repeat-a-letter cycling) is correct and stays. What was wrong was the SEARCH SCOPE: scanning from index 0 every keystroke means the globally-first match wins, so a keystroke flings the user out of the category they're in (Procedures → Tables). Fix = anchor the search on the current position and search forward with wrap: `FindFromAnchor(index, buffer, anchorIndex)` starting AT `anchorIndex` (inclusive) modulo count. Inclusive is mandatory — the first keystroke must check the current selection FIRST and stay if it still matches (IBExpert/VS feel), not advance to the next match (Windows-Explorer feel; explicitly rejected by the user). Two subtleties: (a) map the selected node to an index via a pure `FindAnchorIndex` (structural → reference on `DirectNode`; leaf → `(group, name)` + `Group.Children.Contains` to disambiguate duplicate names; not found → -1 → from-top fallback); (b) a GROWING buffer must anchor on a View-held `_typeAheadAnchor` (the previous match), NOT `tree.SelectedItem`, because selection is applied asynchronously (`Dispatcher.Post`) and a fast refine would otherwise read a stale selection — only a FRESH buffer re-reads `tree.SelectedItem` (to honour a manual click). The search domain is still the FULL flat index (keeps cross-category reach into never-expanded categories via the name cache — #135); only the start point moved from 0 to the anchor.

### Contextual type-ahead search (IBExpert-style anchor + wrap) — shipped 2026-06-20

Replaced the from-top type-ahead with an **anchored** search: typing starts at the user's current selection, searches forward, and wraps — so a keystroke can no longer fling the user from a Procedures node into Tables. Closes the deferred follow-up. **1402 → 1415 tests** (+13). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK. Scope: the type-ahead finder + View wiring only — lazy-load, the name cache, the filter, and `BuildFullTypeAheadIndexAsync` are untouched.

**1. Anchor.** The search domain is still the full flat tree-order index (`BuildFullTypeAheadIndexAsync` over the name cache + structural rows — finds objects in never-expanded categories, loads only the matched category on a hit). What changed is the **start point**: `ResolveTypeAheadAsync(buffer, anchor)` now maps the anchor node to its index position via the new pure `FindAnchorIndex(index, selected)` (structural node → reference match on `DirectNode`; object leaf → `(owning group, name)` with `Group.Children.Contains` disambiguating duplicate names across categories; not found / placeholder / null → -1 → from-top fallback), then searches from there.

**2. Wrap-around.** New pure `FindFromAnchor(index, buffer, anchorIndex)` — first entry (case-insensitive prefix) searching FORWARD from `anchorIndex` **inclusive**, wrapping to the top via `(start + offset) % count`; `anchorIndex < 0` starts at 0 (≡ `FindFirstMatch`, kept as the no-anchor fallback + its existing tests). So from a Procedures selection the search exhausts Procedures-forward, then later categories, then wraps to the top — only ever leaving the current category when there's genuinely no later match.

**3. First keystroke = inclusive (check current FIRST).** Per the user's explicit correction (NOT Windows-Explorer advance): a fresh `K` on a selected `KONTRAHENCI` STAYS on `KONTRAHENCI` because it still matches — it does not jump to the next K-item. Inclusive-from-anchor delivers this for free (the search always begins AT the anchor).

**4. Growing buffer.** `K → KO → KON → KONT → KONTR` stays pinned on `KONTRAHENCI` while it matches; the moment it stops (e.g. `KONTRAHX`) the search advances forward-from-anchor with wrap to the next match (or none). Race-safe: the View anchors a **growing** buffer on its own `_typeAheadAnchor` field (the previous match), not `tree.SelectedItem`, because selection is applied asynchronously (`Dispatcher.Post`) — a fast refine must not read a not-yet-applied selection. A **fresh** buffer (was empty) re-reads `tree.SelectedItem` instead, so a manual click between searches is honoured. `_typeAheadAnchor` is cleared on idle-reset (~1 s) and Escape.

**5. Difference vs. the old global implementation.** Old: `FindFirstMatch` scanned from index 0 every keystroke → the globally-first match won → from a Procedures node, typing jumped to the first matching Table. New: `FindFromAnchor` scans from the selection with wrap → the search is local to where the user is and only crosses categories as a last resort. The incremental growing-buffer semantics and the lazy name cache are preserved exactly; only the start index moved from 0 to the anchor.

**Tests** ([TreePerfTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/TreePerfTests.cs), +13): `FindFromAnchor` (anchor-matches-stays, incremental-stays-pinned, anchor-stops-matching-advances, wrap-to-top, anchor-at-last-wraps, negative-anchor-from-top, no-match, empty) + `FindAnchorIndex` (null, structural by reference, leaf by group+name, duplicate-name disambiguated by group, stray leaf → -1). View wiring (`_typeAheadAnchor`, fresh/grow anchor selection) is code-behind, not unit-tested (precedent).

**Follow-up bugfix — "after clicking an object, typing does nothing" (shipped 2026-06-20).** Reported on the live DB: click a leaf, start typing → nothing. Diagnosed with a **headless Avalonia probe** (gotcha #39/#94 — added to `ConnectionExpandBindingProbe`), NOT by guessing. The probe builds the real `MainWindow`, focuses a real leaf `TreeViewItem`, injects a keystroke, and observes (a) whether the production `OnSidebarTypeAhead` fires (it sets `e.Handled=true`) and (b) whether selection moves. Result: with a **fresh** buffer the whole path works (handler fires for a focused leaf; `AKTYWA` + `k` → selection moves to `KONTRAHENCI`) — so the event layer / focus / anchor were all fine. **Root cause:** the type-ahead buffer was cleared ONLY by the 1 s idle timer + Escape, never by a click — and every keystroke appends to the buffer *regardless of a match*. So clicking a node within ~1 s of any prior typing left a stale buffer; the next keystroke produced a garbage search string anchored on the stale `_typeAheadAnchor` → `result == null` → nothing, and each failed keystroke grew the buffer further (self-perpetuating). **Fix:** `ResetTypeAhead()` (buffer + `_typeAheadAnchor` + stop timer) now also runs on a left-button tree click (`OnSidebarPointerPressed`), so every click starts a fresh search anchored on the clicked node — Escape and the idle timer route through the same helper. Pinned by the probe's regression leg (poison buffer with a non-matching key → click a leaf → a matching key must still resolve; without the reset the final selection would stay on the clicked leaf). Added `MetadataExplorerViewModel.PrimeNameCacheForTest` (internal seam) so the probe can exercise resolution without a live DB. **1402 → 1416 tests.**

137. **A type-ahead / incremental-search buffer MUST be reset on the user's repositioning gesture (a click), not only on an idle timer + Escape.** Each keystroke appends to the buffer regardless of whether it matched, so a buffer that only clears after N seconds of inactivity will carry a stale search string into the user's next interaction: click a node, type, and the keystroke is appended to garbage → no match → "nothing happens", and every failed keystroke makes it worse. A click is "I'm here now, start fresh" — reset the buffer (and the search anchor) on it. The diagnosis discipline that found this: when "typing does nothing", do NOT guess the layer — build a headless probe (gotcha #39) that focuses the real control and injects the real keystroke, and check (a) does the handler fire (`e.Handled`), (b) does the outcome apply. Here the probe proved the handler fires and a *fresh* buffer resolves correctly, which pointed squarely at buffer *state between interactions* rather than events/focus/anchor. (Add the probe to the existing headless class — one session per process, gotcha #94.)

> ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED by "Sidebar type-to-filter" below (2026-06-20).** The whole IBExpert-style type-ahead direction (the two milestones above — anchor + wrap, the click-reset follow-up — plus the `Find*`/`ResolveTypeAheadAsync`/`BuildFullTypeAheadIndexAsync`/`NodeSearchText`/`TypeAheadEntry` code and gotchas #136/#137) was **removed** at the user's request in favour of a much simpler type-to-filter. The session **name cache** (gotcha #135) STAYS — the filter still uses it. Gotchas #136/#137 are retained only as historical lessons; the code they describe no longer exists.

### Sidebar type-to-filter (replaces type-ahead) — shipped 2026-06-20

At the user's explicit request, the IBExpert-style contextual type-ahead was **abandoned** (too much TreeView/focus fiddliness for the value) and replaced with a simpler, robust **type-to-filter**: while the tree has focus and the user starts typing, focus jumps to the sidebar filter box and the first character lands there; subsequent typing is handled natively by the now-focused `TextBox`. Plus **Ctrl+F** focuses the filter and **Escape** clears it and returns focus to the tree. **1416 → 1396 tests** (removed the type-ahead unit tests + probe; added one type-to-filter probe). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK.

**Mechanism** (all View code-behind in [MainWindow.axaml.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs); the filter `TextBox` got `x:Name="SidebarFilterBox"`):
- **Type-to-filter** — a single tunnel `TextInput` handler on `SidebarTree` (`OnSidebarTreeTextInput`). It only fires while the tree (or a tree item) is focused — once the filter box is focused, input no longer routes through the tree, so the redirect is self-limiting. On a printable char it `Focus()`es the filter box, inserts the char at the caret (`box.Text` is TwoWay-bound to `FilterText`, which drives the existing debounced `ApplyFilterAsync`), and marks the event handled.
- **Ctrl+F** — a window-level tunnel `KeyDown` handler (`OnWindowKeyDown`) focuses + selects the filter. Tunnel so it wins before any editor's own Ctrl+F (the user wants the sidebar filter to own Ctrl+F app-wide).
