- **NOT NULL** → `SET/DROP NOT NULL` (FB3+). New `BuildAlterDomainSetNotNull` / `BuildAlterDomainDropNotNull`.
- **Name (rename)** → `ALTER DOMAIN "OLD" TO "NEW"`, emitted LAST (every other ALTER references the old name); on success the VM raises `RenameReopenRequested` → the owner closes + reopens the tab under the new name (reuses the New-domain reopen shape; `OnDomainRenamed` in `MainWindowViewModel`). New `BuildAlterDomainRename`.
- **Default / Check / Description** — already editable, unchanged.
- **Collation — stays READ-ONLY on an existing domain.** Firebird has NO `ALTER DOMAIN … COLLATE` syntax (verified: -104 in every form, even inside `TYPE`), and no bare `SET CHARACTER SET`. Editable only in the New flow (CREATE DOMAIN supports COLLATE).

`DomainDetailTabViewModel`: editability gates replaced `IsDefinitionEditable`(=IsNew) with `IsTypeEditable` (always), `IsCharsetEditable` (`IsCharTypeSelected`), `IsCollationEditable` (`IsNew && IsCharTypeSelected`); Name/NotNull now always editable. `BuildCompileSql` (existing branch) diffs the live form vs. a loaded-`DomainInfo` baseline (`_baselineInfo`) and emits the minimum-set: TYPE / NOT NULL / DEFAULT / CHECK / COMMENT / RENAME (rename last). A unit-constructed VM with no load (`_baselineInfo == null`) skips type/not-null/rename diffs (can't know the prior shape) — preserves the existing default/check/description tests. New `SeedBaselineForTest` seam + extracted `ApplyLoadedInfo` (shared by load + seam). [DomainDetailTabView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/DomainDetailTabView.axaml) rebinds the field gates accordingly.

**Lab DB:** used `Lab/EmberTern_Lab.fdb` (copied to an ASCII temp path per gotcha #149) for verification — no new test database created. The existing 8-domain zoo covered every case (VARCHAR, NUMERIC+default+check, INTEGER+default+check, CHAR, DATE, BLOB), so no lab extension was needed. Every exact EmberTern-generated DDL string (`ALTER DOMAIN "D_NAME" TYPE VARCHAR(100) CHARACTER SET WIN1250`, `TYPE BIGINT`, `SET/DROP NOT NULL`, `SET DEFAULT`, `DROP CONSTRAINT`+`ADD CHECK`, `COMMENT ON DOMAIN`, `TO`) ran clean against FB 5.0.3; Firebird rejects data-unsafe TYPE changes (narrowing a length, a type bound to an index/constraint) server-side, which surface as the editor's error.

**Tests** ([DomainDetailTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/DomainDetailTests.cs)): +new DDL-generator pins (BuildAlterDomainType incl. charset + never-COLLATE, SetNotNull/DropNotNull, Rename, empty-name throws); +BuildCompileSql-against-seeded-baseline (length increase → ALTER TYPE w/ charset, INTEGER→BIGINT, charset change, set/drop NOT NULL, rename emitted last, type+rename ordering, seeded-no-change empty, editing-type-marks-dirty); editability gate tests rewritten for the new model. Generator tests + Domain tests renamed Save→Compile identifiers.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

150. **Firebird `ALTER DOMAIN` supports TYPE, the type's CHARACTER SET (char types), SET/DROP NOT NULL, SET/DROP DEFAULT, ADD CHECK/DROP CONSTRAINT, and TO (rename) — only COLLATION is unchangeable.** Re-verified on FB 5.0.3 (correcting the overly-restrictive gotcha #148). The charset is changed via the `TYPE … CHARACTER SET …` form (there is NO bare `SET CHARACTER SET`); COLLATE is rejected with SQL error -104 in EVERY ALTER form (standalone, in TYPE, with or without CHARACTER SET) — it can only be set at CREATE. Firebird enforces data safety server-side (rejects narrowing a length, or a TYPE change on a domain bound to an index/integrity constraint), so a domain editor should EMIT the ALTER and let the engine's rejection surface as an error rather than pre-blocking. Emit a rename LAST (other ALTERs reference the old name) and reopen the editor under the new name. **Rule applied here and reinforced from the lab-DB rule: never assume an ALTER limitation — verify the exact statement form against the live engine (`Lab/EmberTern_Lab.fdb` on FB5, ASCII-path copy per gotcha #149); the supported *form* (e.g. TYPE … CHARACTER SET vs. SET CHARACTER SET) matters as much as the operation.**

### Package Detail — dedicated 6-tab surface (header + body, shipped 2026-06-29)

Firebird packages open in a dedicated **Package Detail** experience instead of a plain DDL tab: six tabs — **Package** (editable `CREATE OR ALTER PACKAGE` header source + Compile) · **Body** (editable `RECREATE PACKAGE BODY` source) · **Members** (read-only grouped tree, double-click → navigate into the editor) · **Description** (editable `COMMENT ON PACKAGE`) · **Dependencies** (dual tree) · **DDL** (read-only). **1711 → 1764 tests** (+53). Build clean (0/0), 9s smoke OK. The lab `PKG_ORDERS` is the test case (no `setup.sql` change).

**Firebird package storage (verified empirically on a scratch FB5 DB via isql).** A package has **two** source artifacts in `RDB$PACKAGES`: `RDB$PACKAGE_HEADER_SOURCE` (declarations) and `RDB$PACKAGE_BODY_SOURCE` (implementation) — each is the text **after `AS`** (the `BEGIN…END` block, no leading `AS`/no `CREATE PACKAGE name`), exactly the proc-source model (gotcha #114). **Members come straight from the catalog** — `RDB$FUNCTIONS`/`RDB$PROCEDURES` rows carrying `RDB$PACKAGE_NAME = <pkg>` (standalone routines have it NULL) — so the Members tab needs **zero source parsing**. Dependencies use `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` object-type **18** (Package). `COMMENT ON PACKAGE` / `CREATE OR ALTER PACKAGE` / `RECREATE PACKAGE BODY` / `DROP PACKAGE` all confirmed to compile + round-trip.

**Architecture decision — a focused `PackageDetailTabViewModel : ViewModelBase, IUnsavedWorkSource`, NOT a `SourceObjectDetailTabViewModel` subclass** (consistent with gotchas #112/#113; View/Generator/Domain precedent). The source-object base is built around ONE source + a Source⇄Easy structured-model toggle + a Variables grid; a package has **two** editable source artifacts (header + body) and **no** Easy/structured mode, so the base would be all-overrides. The right sibling is **`ViewDetailTabViewModel`** — editable source + Compile + dirty/Revert + Dependencies + DDL + editable Description, all without the base. Package = the same shape with TWO code editors + a Members tab. A package is treated as ONE logical object: a single **Compile** runs the header FIRST, then the body; **if the header fails the body is not attempted** (it can't compile against a header that didn't apply).

**Reuse (the brief's "reuse first" hard requirement):** `SqlEditorBehavior.Attach` (autocomplete + Ctrl/double-click nav on both editors) · shared `SqlFormatter` (Alt+F + toolbar Format) · `ProcedureBodyScanner.CommentBody/UncommentBody` (block `/* */` Comment/Uncomment on the active editor) · `FirebirdDdlReader.ReadBlobAsync` + `StripLeadingAs` + `CharsetCatalog` (source-BLOB fetch) · `TableDetailTabViewModel.BuildDependencyTree` + `MapObjectTypeToKind` + the dep-tree templates + `OpenObjectRequested` routing · `DdlGenerator.BuildRelationComment` (→ `BuildCommentPackage`) · the View dirty/`_suppressDirty`/Revert/WorkGuard pattern · the Generator/Domain routing/factory/dedup/persistence/toolbar/Delete pattern · `Icon.Package` + `IconColor_Package` (already in the metadata tree). The `FirebirdDdlExecutor` PSQL-aware splitter (gotcha #140) **already** handles `CREATE OR ALTER PACKAGE` / `RECREATE PACKAGE BODY` as single statements (header declarations + nested routine bodies have internal `;`) — verified, no executor change.

**New code (and why reuse wasn't possible):**
- `Core/Metadata/PackageMember.cs` (`{Name, PackageMemberKind}`) + `DdlGenerator.BuildCreateOrAlterPackageHeader` / `BuildRecreatePackageBody` / `BuildPackageDdl` / `BuildDropPackage` / `BuildCommentPackage` — package DDL shapes didn't exist. (Uniform `\n` output, not `AppendLine`'s `\r\n`, so the reconstructed source doesn't mix line endings.)
- `FirebirdDdlReader.FetchPackageHeaderSourceAsync` / `FetchPackageBodySourceAsync` + real `BuildPackageDdlAsync` (replaced the placeholder) — package source lives in `RDB$PACKAGES` (two distinct BLOBs); no existing fetcher targets it.
- `FirebirdTableDetailReader.GetPackageMembersAsync` / `GetPackageDescriptionAsync` / `GetPackageDependenciesAsync` + the `Package*Sql` consts — a package isn't a relation (gotcha #113): members + description + type-18 deps need package-scoped catalog queries.
- `PackageDetailTabViewModel` + `PackageDetailTabView.axaml(.cs)` — the two-editor + Members surface; no existing VM/view fits.
- `Core/Sql/PackageSourceScanner.FindMemberOffset` — member-navigation **find** (offset of `FUNCTION name`/`PROCEDURE name`), reusing `SqlScanHelpers` primitives. **Not a new parser** (per "nie tworzyć nowego parsera") — the member *list* is catalog-sourced; this only locates a known member for the editor jump.

**Member navigation.** Double-click a member → `PackageDetailTabViewModel.NavigateToMember` resolves its location (`ResolveMemberLocation`: body first — where you'd edit it — else header), sets `ActiveSubTabIndex` to the right tab, and raises `NavigateToMemberRequested(PackageMemberLocation{InBody,Offset,Length})`; the view posts a `Select`+`ScrollToLine` on the matching editor (Dispatcher.Background, so the tab-switch realizes first).

**Files changed (15).** New: `Core/Metadata/PackageMember.cs`, `Core/Sql/PackageSourceScanner.cs`, `App/ViewModels/PackageDetailTabViewModel.cs`, `App/Views/PackageDetailTabView.axaml(.cs)`, `tests/EmberTern.Tests/PackageDetailTests.cs`. Edited: `Core/Metadata/DdlGenerator.cs`, `Core/Workspace/WorkspaceState.cs` (`PackageDetail` kind), `Firebird/FirebirdDdlReader.cs`, `Firebird/FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs`, `App/ViewModels/WorkspaceTabViewModel.cs` (kind+factory+property+UnsavedWork), `App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs` (OpensAsPackageDetail, CreatePackageDetail, dispatch, dedup, Is/ActivePackageDetail + notify, SelectTab lazy-load, Snapshot/Restore, NewPackage/OnPackageCreated, OnPackageDeleteRequested, CanCreatePackage), `App/Views/MainWindow.axaml` (New Package + Compile/Revert/Delete + Format/Comment/Uncomment buttons gated on `IsPackageDetailTabActive`, sibling view, `Icon.PackagePlus`), `App/Themes/IconGeometries.axaml`, `App/UiStrings.cs`.

**Tests** ([PackageDetailTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/PackageDetailTests.cs), +53): `OpensAsPackageDetail` predicate; factory Compile/Delete capability; direct-open → PackageDetail tab (not DDL) + dedup; native restore + Capture-as-PackageDetail; New command re-notify on connect; DDL builders (header/body wrap, combined DDL with/without body, lowercase quoting, drop, comment cases, empty-name throw); splitter keeps the package header + body as single statements; member grouping (functions/procedures, omit-empty, empty-state); `PackageSourceScanner.FindMemberOffset` (function/procedure located, wrong-kind/not-found/null → -1); `ResolveMemberLocation` (body-first, header-fallback, not-found) + `NavigateToMember` switches to Body sub-tab; `TryParsePackageName` cases; dirty tracking + Revert/GetUnsavedWork gating; no-executor no-op; reader `*Sql` shape pins (RDB$FUNCTIONS/RDB$PROCEDURES by RDB$PACKAGE_NAME, type-18 deps) + `MapObjectType(18)`.

**Known limitations.** Package dependencies use `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` type 18 (the standard "Used by / Depends on" surface), which is sparse for packages — what the member routines reference isn't aggregated up to the package (a deeper V2 query). The live-app GUI flow (double-click `PKG_ORDERS` → 6 tabs → edit → Compile → member double-click navigation) is the user's manual DoD pass, consistent with the codebase's DB-path precedent; the Firebird storage format + every DDL statement the editor emits were verified empirically via isql.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

### Package Detail follow-up — Description labels + PACKAGE BODY formatter fix (shipped 2026-06-29)

Two manual-test findings. **1764 → 1769 tests** (+5). Build clean, lab-verified on FB5.

**1. "Table description" in every source editor's Description tab.** The shared `UiStrings.TableDescriptionEditLabel = "Table description"` was reused as the Description-tab mini-header by 6 editors (Table, View, Procedure, Function, Trigger, Package) — correct only for Table. Added per-object labels (`ViewDescriptionEditLabel` … `PackageDescriptionEditLabel` = "View/Procedure/Function/Trigger/Package description") and pointed each view at its own. Generator/Domain were checked and are clean (they show Description as a `field-label` in their form, not via this label).

**2. CRITICAL — Auto Format corrupted PACKAGE BODY source into non-compilable PSQL.** The PSQL formatter (`SqlFormatter.FormatPsql` → `EmitPsqlUnit`) had no case for a **bare leading `FUNCTION`/`PROCEDURE`** (a packaged subprogram definition). A `PACKAGE BODY`'s top-level items are `FUNCTION name(...) [RETURNS …] AS [DECLARE …;]* BEGIN … END` / `PROCEDURE …` — NOT statements, NOT terminated by `;`. The formatter fell to `CollectPsqlStatement` (collects only to the first inner `;`, e.g. after `RETURN A + B;`), then the routine's own `END` was taken as the package-body `END`, so the body loop exited early and **dropped the trailing `END`s** → invalid PSQL (won't compile). Fix: `EmitPsqlUnit` now detects a packaged subprogram definition (`IsSubprogramDefinition` — a top-level `AS` before any top-level `;`, keyed on `AS` not `BEGIN` so a DECLARE-section `;` doesn't misclassify it) and handles it like the existing local-`DECLARE PROCEDURE/FUNCTION` case: emit the header up to the body `BEGIN`, then recurse for the `BEGIN…END`. The package-body loop now stops only at the package's own `END`. A package-header forward declaration (`FUNCTION/PROCEDURE … ;`, no `AS`) still falls through to the statement path (unchanged). Verified empirically: the formatter's actual output for the PKG_ORDERS-shaped header + body compiled cleanly on FB5 (package created with both members). Standalone procedure/trigger/function formatting is unaffected (the branch only fires on a bare leading FUNCTION/PROCEDURE, which is a syntax error outside a package body). Tests: +5 in `PsqlFormatterTests` (BEGIN/END preserved + balanced + single-statement-to-splitter, idempotent, header forward-decls stay one statement, standalone proc unaffected).

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

152. **The PSQL formatter must treat a bare leading `FUNCTION`/`PROCEDURE` inside a PACKAGE BODY as a subprogram DEFINITION, not a statement.** A packaged routine (`FUNCTION name(...) [RETURNS …] AS BEGIN … END`) has no terminating `;` and its own nested `BEGIN…END`; collecting it as a statement-to-`;` stops at the first inner `;` and then mistakes the routine's `END` for the package-body's `END`, dropping the remaining `END`s → non-compilable PSQL. Detect it by scanning at paren-depth 0 from the keyword: a top-level `AS` ⇒ definition (emit header-to-BEGIN, recurse the BEGIN…END — same shape as a local `DECLARE PROCEDURE/FUNCTION`); a top-level `;` before any `AS` ⇒ a package-header forward declaration (statement path). Key the discriminator on `AS`, NOT `BEGIN`, because a routine body may have a `DECLARE` section whose `;` precedes `BEGIN` at depth 0. This is the same defect family as the DDL splitter's package handling (gotcha #140) — both must understand that a PACKAGE BODY is a sequence of subprogram definitions, not statements. Verify formatter changes by compiling the actual output on a live FB5 engine (a scratch DB with the referenced objects), not just by eyeballing structure.

151. **A Firebird package has TWO source artifacts and is NOT a relation — give it a focused two-editor VM, not the source-object base.** `RDB$PACKAGES.RDB$PACKAGE_HEADER_SOURCE` + `RDB$PACKAGE_BODY_SOURCE` each store the text after `AS` (like `RDB$PROCEDURE_SOURCE`, gotcha #114), so reconstruction is `CREATE OR ALTER PACKAGE name AS <header>` / `RECREATE PACKAGE BODY name AS <body>` (FB has no `CREATE OR ALTER PACKAGE BODY` — RECREATE is the idempotent body form), with `StripLeadingAs` as the defensive guard (gotcha #139). Members are catalog rows (`RDB$FUNCTIONS`/`RDB$PROCEDURES` WHERE `RDB$PACKAGE_NAME`) — never parse the source for the list; only *locate* a known member for navigation. The `SourceObjectDetailTabViewModel` base assumes one source + an Easy toggle, which a package can't satisfy — model it on `ViewDetailTabViewModel` (focused, reuse at the reader/helper level) and treat the package as one logical object: a single Compile that does header-then-body and stops if the header fails.

### Exception Detail — dedicated 4-tab surface (shipped 2026-06-30)

Firebird custom EXCEPTIONs open in a dedicated **Exception Detail** experience instead of a plain DDL tab: four tabs — **Exception** (the editable form: Name + Message), **Description** (editable `COMMENT ON EXCEPTION`), **Dependencies** (the dual dependency tree — an exception is "depended on by" procedures / functions / triggers / packages that raise it), and **DDL** (read-only reconstructed `CREATE EXCEPTION`). Deliberately the simplest editor in the app — an exception has no PSQL body, no parameters. **1769 → 1815 tests** (+46). Build clean (0/0), 8s smoke OK. The lab DB already carries 3 exceptions used by a procedure + trigger (no `setup.sql` change needed).

**Architecture decision — a focused form-based `ExceptionDetailTabViewModel`, NOT a `SourceObjectDetailTabViewModel` subclass.** Closest analogs are Generator + Domain Detail (form-based, no PSQL body); modeled on **Domain's** ctor shape (`(name, reader, ddlExecutor)` — no `_ddlReader`, DDL rebuilt live from the form via `RefreshDdl`). Reuse is total — at the reader / DDL-generator / dependency-tree / dirty-tracking / WorkGuard / Revert / Delete / routing / persistence / toolbar level; **zero new architectural pattern**.

**Reuse (the brief's hard requirement):** the `IUnsavedWorkSource` + dirty-tracking (`_suppressDirty`/`MarkDirty`/`SetDirty`) + `_settingNameUpper` UPPERCASE coercion (gotcha #141) + `ConfirmationRequested`/`RequestConfirmAsync` + `EnsureLoadedAsync`/`RefreshAsync`/`SafeLoadAsync` + `OpenObjectRequested` + the `ObjectCreated`/`DeleteRequested` event shape — all copied verbatim from Generator/Domain; `TableDetailTabViewModel.BuildDependencyTree` + `MapObjectTypeToKind` + the dual dependency-tree templates + double-click navigation; `DdlGenerator.BuildRelationComment` (→ `BuildCommentException` = `COMMENT ON EXCEPTION`); the `Icon.Exception` glyph + `IconColor_Exception` (already in the metadata tree); the Generator/Domain routing/factory/dedup/persistence/toolbar/Delete wiring.

**Verified on live FB 5.0.3 (lab DB, embedded isql — syntax NOT guessed):** `CREATE EXCEPTION "N" 'message'`, `ALTER EXCEPTION "N" 'message'` (changes `RDB$MESSAGE`), `COMMENT ON EXCEPTION "N" IS …` (→ `RDB$DESCRIPTION`), `CREATE OR ALTER`, `IS NULL`, `DROP EXCEPTION "N"` — all succeed; ran EmberTern's exact quoted-identifier output + the reader's catalog queries against a fresh lab copy (load 3 lab exceptions, type-7 dependency lookup returns the raising trigger + procedure, full create/alter/comment/drop round-trip).

**Core** — [ExceptionInfo.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Metadata/ExceptionInfo.cs) (init-only POCO: Name / Message / Description). [DdlGenerator.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Metadata/DdlGenerator.cs): `BuildCreateException` / `BuildAlterException` / `BuildDropException` (always-quote `Quote`; message literal via a new private `QuoteLiteral` — single quotes doubled, null → `''`) + `BuildCommentException`. `WorkspaceTabKind.ExceptionDetail` added to the Core persistence enum.

**Firebird** ([FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs)) — `GetExceptionInfoAsync` (`RDB$MESSAGE` + `RDB$DESCRIPTION` from `RDB$EXCEPTIONS`) + `GetExceptionDependenciesAsync` + `ExceptionDependsOnSql`/`ExceptionDependedOnBySql` (`RDB$DEPENDENCIES` type **7** = Exception, mirror of the generator type-14 SQL). Metadata-lane access, lock captured once (gotcha #98/#120). (`FirebirdDdlReader.BuildExceptionDdlAsync` already reconstructed `CREATE OR ALTER EXCEPTION`; Exception now routes to the detail surface instead of a plain DDL tab.)

**App** — [ExceptionDetailTabViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ExceptionDetailTabViewModel.cs) + [ExceptionDetailTabView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/ExceptionDetailTabView.axaml)(.cs). `CompileCommand` builds the diff DDL (`BuildCompileSql`, pure + internal: New → `CREATE` [+`COMMENT`]; existing → `ALTER` if message changed [+`COMMENT` if description changed]; empty → no-op) and runs it through `FirebirdDdlExecutor` — **autonomous auto-commit (Phase A), so no working transaction and no Commit/Rollback pair on an exception tab** (like Trigger/Generator/Domain). `RevertChangesCommand` (gated `IsDirty && !IsNew`, confirms) reloads; `DeleteCommand` (gated `!IsNew`, confirms) raises `DeleteRequested` → owner runs `DROP EXCEPTION` + closes the tab + refreshes the tree. No rename (Firebird has no `ALTER EXCEPTION … TO`) — name read-only on an existing exception.

**Routing/persistence/toolbar** ([MainWindowViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs), [MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml), [WorkspaceTabViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/WorkspaceTabViewModel.cs)) — strictly parallel to the Generator/Domain path: `OpensAsExceptionDetail` + `CreateExceptionDetail` factory (wires `OpenObjectRequested`/`ConfirmationRequested`/`DeleteRequested`) + `OnOpenDdlRequested` branch + `(Kind,Name)` dedup; `NewException` command (`Icon.ExceptionPlus`, in the New-X toolbar group) + `OnExceptionCreated` (refresh tree → close New tab → reopen real exception); `OnExceptionDeleteRequested`; `IsExceptionDetailTabActive`/`ActiveExceptionDetail` + notify chain + `SelectTab` lazy-load + `IsClosableTabActive`; native `CoreTabKind.ExceptionDetail` persistence (Snapshot skips transient New tabs / Restore lazy-loads + seeds cached DDL); `CanCreateException` re-notify on connection change; Compile/Revert/Delete editor-toolbar buttons gated on `IsExceptionDetailTabActive`; sibling `<views:ExceptionDetailTabView>`. New strings in [UiStrings.cs](src/EmberTern.App/UiStrings.cs); composed `Icon.ExceptionPlus` (warning triangle + bottom-right `+`) in [IconGeometries.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Themes/IconGeometries.axaml).

**Double-click in the metadata tree** opens Exception Detail (not a DDL tab), via the shared `OpensAsExceptionDetail` predicate. Dependency navigation reuses `MapObjectTypeToKind`.

**Tests** ([ExceptionDetailTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/ExceptionDetailTests.cs), +46): `OpensAsExceptionDetail` predicate; factory (Compile/Delete capability); direct-open → ExceptionDetail tab (not DDL) + dedup; native restore; capture persists as ExceptionDetail; New command re-notifies on connect; DDL-generator shape (create/alter incl. quote-escaping/empty-message; drop; comment cases; empty-name throws); `BuildCompileSql` (New CREATE [+COMMENT]; existing no-change → empty / ALTER message / COMMENT description / both); dirty tracking + Compile/Delete/Revert gating; UPPERCASE name; `DeleteCommand` raises `DeleteRequested` after confirm; `GetUnsavedWork` (clean/new/existing); live DDL-preview tracks the form; dependency navigation; reader SQL type-7 shape + `MapObjectType(7)`.

### Security Manager — Users / Roles / Membership / Privileges (shipped 2026-06-30, branch `feat/security-manager`)

Unified **Security Manager** workspace tab for Firebird users, roles, role membership, and object/column privileges — IBExpert-inspired, EmberTern-styled. Decision (user-approved): ONE tool with four sub-tabs, **not** per-object Detail editors and **not** two separate windows. **Not a singleton** (user-requested change) — a tab is keyed by the context object it was opened from `(Kind, Name)`; double-clicking a user/role opens-or-focuses its tab with the matching item auto-selected, and different contexts coexist. All changes apply immediately (each GRANT/REVOKE/CREATE/ALTER executes + auto-commits via the autonomous DDL executor — admin tooling, no buffered Compile). **1815 → 1868 tests** (+53). Build 0/0, smoke OK.

**Firebird 5 verified empirically** (embedded `isql`, throwaway DBs at ASCII temp paths — gotcha #149): CREATE/DROP ROLE; GRANT/REVOKE object privileges (S/I/U/D/R/X/USAGE); **column-level** `GRANT UPDATE(col)/REFERENCES(col)`; `WITH GRANT OPTION`; `GRANT role TO user/role [WITH ADMIN OPTION]`; `RDB$USER_PRIVILEGES` decoding (privilege letters S/I/U/D/R/X/G/M, grant-option 0/1/2, object-type 0 relation / 5 proc / 7 exception / 13 role / 14 sequence / 15 func / 18 package, user-type 8 user / 13 role); `SEC$USERS` columns (USER_NAME/FIRST/MIDDLE/LAST_NAME/ACTIVE/ADMIN/DESCRIPTION/PLUGIN — **no FB2.5 UID/GID in FB5**). **`COMMENT ON ROLE`** runs cleanly (→ `RDB$ROLES.RDB$DESCRIPTION`); **`COMMENT ON USER`** is syntactically accepted (failed only with SQLSTATE 28000 security-DB write perms, not -104 — works in-app as SYSDBA, → `SEC$DESCRIPTION`) — so **both keep Description fields** (per the user's conditional). User-management DDL writes the server security DB (couldn't run embedded; grammar is stable FB3→5, smoke-verified live). Users are **server-global**; roles/membership/privileges are **per-database**.

**Layers (reuse-first):** Core — [SecurityModels.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Security/SecurityModels.cs) (UserInfo/RoleInfo/PrivilegeInfo/MembershipInfo/GranteeRef + `SecurityCatalog` decoders) + [SecurityDdlGenerator.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Metadata/SecurityDdlGenerator.cs) (pure DDL; reuses `DdlGenerator.QuoteLight` — promoted private→internal — so SHOUTY names stay bare, matching the catalog). Firebird — [FirebirdSecurityReader.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdSecurityReader.cs) (metadata-lane, capture-once lock #98/#120: `ListUsersAsync`/`ListRolesAsync`/`ListPrivilegesAsync`/`ListMembershipAsync`). App — [SecurityManagerTabViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/SecurityManagerTabViewModel.cs) (orchestrator, per-step-isolated load #24) + [SecurityPaneViewModels.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/SecurityPaneViewModels.cs) (Users/Roles/Membership/Privileges panes + PrivilegeRow/ColumnPrivilegeRow/MembershipRow/GranteeOption VMs) + [UserEditDialogViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/UserEditDialogViewModel.cs)/[NewRoleDialogViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/NewRoleDialogViewModel.cs) + [SecurityManagerTabView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/SecurityManagerTabView.axaml)/[UserEditDialog](src/EmberTern.App/Views/UserEditDialog.axaml)/[NewRoleDialog](src/EmberTern.App/Views/NewRoleDialog.axaml). Reuses the existing DataGrid + `BoolToCheckmarkConverter` + filter + `SvgIcon`/`IconBrushConverter`, `FirebirdDdlExecutor` (autonomous auto-commit), `FirebirdMetadataReader.ListAsync`/`ListColumnsAsync` (grantable objects + column panel), the tab factory/routing/dedup pattern, and the `ConfirmDialog`. No new abstraction layer, no `ISecurity*` interfaces.

**UX.** **Privileges** tab: grantee picker (user/role) + object-category picker (Tables/Views/Procedures/Functions/Packages/Sequences/Exceptions) + name filter + "Grant with GRANT OPTION" toggle → matrix grid (object rows × S/I/U/D/R/X/USAGE checkboxes, per-row visibility by kind, + All) with optimistic toggle (revert on failure); a **Columns panel** (tables only) for `GRANT UPDATE(col)/REFERENCES(col)`. **Membership**: grantee picker + role list with Member + Admin-option checkboxes. **Users**: grid + Add/Edit/Delete (UserEditDialog: name/password/confirm/active/admin/first/middle/last/description) + Refresh. **Roles**: grid + Add (name dialog) / Drop + editable Description. **Tree**: Users/Roles category → "Add user…"/"Add role…" (standalone dialogs → CREATE + tree refresh, nothing opens — IBExpert behaviour); user/role leaf double-click or "Open in Security Manager" → the manager. **Persistence**: deliberately NOT persisted (live admin tool, reopened from the tree; skipped in `SnapshotCurrentTabs`).

**Lab** ([setup.sql](Lab/setup.sql)): seeded role grants + role-to-role membership + `COMMENT ON ROLE` on the existing `LAB_READER/EDITOR/ADMIN` (per-DB, safe; users can't be seeded — server-global). Applied to the committed `Lab/EmberTern_Lab.fdb` via embedded isql (copy-to-ASCII → apply → copy back).

**Tests** ([SecurityManagerTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/SecurityManagerTests.cs), +60): DDL-generator shape (user/role CRUD, COMMENT, object/column/membership grants, keywords, grant/admin option, quoting, validation throws); `SecurityCatalog` decoders; reader SQL pins; `OpensAsSecurityManager` routing; orchestrator context→initial-sub-tab; PrivilegeRow tri-state + Cycle; bulk picker per-category + column-header commands; column-panel visibility; Membership direction switch + both-direction tri-state + Cycle; GranteeOption display; UserEdit/NewRole dialog VM validation + UPPERCASE coercion. The live GUI flow is the user's manual DoD pass, per codebase precedent.

**Post-live-test polish (after the user smoke-tested on the FK ERP schema).** Privileges: **tri-state cells** replace the per-privilege checkbox + the "Grant with grant option" toggle — each cell cycles **none → granted (`✓` green) → granted WITH GRANT OPTION (`✓+` blue) → none** on click (theme-aware [PrivilegeStateConverters](src/EmberTern.App/Converters/PrivilegeStateConverters.cs) + legend). **Bulk ops (A+B):** kept the "Bulk (visible)" toolbar (privilege picker `All`+each + Grant/Revoke over the filtered set) AND added a **▾ dropdown in every privilege column header** (grant/revoke that privilege for all visible rows); per-row right-click → Grant/Revoke all. All bulk = one autonomous transaction, skip-already-matching. **Column-level panel** now shows for the whole Tables category (hint until a table row is picked) and clicking a cell selects the row (tunneled `PointerPressed`, gotcha #16/#99). **Headers:** full names (NOT short codes); fix was `CanUserSortColumns=False` (frees the sort-arrow space) + Object column `MaxWidth=440` so it stops dominating. **Toolbar:** the Security Manager button sits **between the Connection/Database and Create-Object sections**, separated both sides (shield `Icon.Role`, opens a context-less Users tab). **Membership rewrite (A+B+C):** a **direction switch** — "Member of" (a grantee → roles it belongs to) and "Members" (a role → users/roles that belong to it); a **single tri-state Membership cell** (none → member → member + admin option) replacing the two checkboxes (reuses the privilege converters); the **combo picker** kept (roles-only in "Members"). All apply optimistically with revert-on-failure; bulk/admin transitions are `GRANT` / `GRANT … WITH {GRANT|ADMIN} OPTION` / `REVOKE`.

**Future UX enhancement (noted, NOT implemented — user's call):** the IBExpert-style set of quick **row / column / all** grant-revoke buttons (the green/red dot affordances). The current reach is per-cell (cycle), per-row (right-click), per-column (header ▾), per-filter (bulk toolbar) — a dedicated always-visible quick-button cluster like IBExpert's is deferred. — **DELIVERED by the milestone below.**

### Security Manager — Privileges bulk-ops redesign (contextual one-click trio, shipped 2026-06-30)

The "future UX enhancement" above, done. Brings IBExpert's bulk-grant *convenience* to the Privileges tab (the 9 operations: row × {grant / grant+option / revoke}, column × same, all-visible × same) without copying its cryptic dot icons and **without touching single-cell editing or adding any grid column**. **1875 tests green, build 0/0, app smoke-launches clean.** The filtering/one-click flow against the live FB is the user's manual pass.

**The model — one vocabulary, three locations; *location* encodes scope.** The same three glyphs appear everywhere, matching the tri-state cell/legend colors: **✓ grant** (`SuccessIconBrush`, = granted cell), **✓+ grant + grant option** (`AccentBrush`, = with-option cell), **✕ revoke** (`DangerIconBrush`).
- **① All visible** → toolbar above the grid: `✓ Grant all` / `✓+ Grant all + option` / `✕ Revoke all` (replaces the removed `Bulk (visible)` combobox + 2 generic buttons — the disliked extra step).
- **② Column** (one privilege, all visible) → an inline trio in each privilege column header (replaces the removed per-header `▾` `MenuFlyout`). Header is now a 2-line vertical stack (label + trio), fits the 84–122 px widths.
- **③ Row** (one object, all privileges) → a hover-revealed trio docked right in the existing **Object** cell (`StackPanel.row-bulk`, `IsVisible` toggled by `DataGridRow:pointerover`/`:selected` styles — the gotcha #35 pattern), **plus** the existing right-click row menu enriched from 2→3 items. No new column; the grid is clean at rest.

**What changed.** Removed: `BulkPrivileges`/`SelectedBulkPrivilege`/`BulkPrivilegeOption` + the picker, the per-header `▾` menus, and the dead strings (`SecurityBulk{AllPrivileges,Label,Grant,Revoke}`, `SecurityColMenuTooltip`, `SecurityCol{Grant,Revoke}AllVisible`). Added/kept: 9 `[RelayCommand]`s — pane `BulkGrant`/`BulkGrantWithOption`/`BulkRevoke` (all-visible) + `GrantColumn`/`GrantColumnWithOption`/`RevokeColumn` (param = privilege letter), row VM `GrantAll`/`GrantAllWithOption`/`RevokeAll`. All route through one pure helper `SecurityPrivilegesPaneViewModel.StatementFor(category, object, priv, grantee, state, BulkAction)` → builds the GRANT/REVOKE or returns null when the cell already satisfies the action (predicates: Grant⇔state 0, GrantWithOption⇔state<2 so plain→option upgrades, Revoke⇔state>0). Same downstream as before: collect statements over the **visible (filtered)** rows → one autonomous-tx batch via `RunBatchAndReloadAsync` → reload the privilege grid; skip-already-matching makes re-runs cheap. New `internal enum BulkAction { Grant, GrantWithOption, Revoke }`. Light confirm (`_owner.ConfirmAsync`, `IsDestructive`) **only** on the broadest op (global Revoke all); grant + row/column ops are frictionless (trivially reversible, IBExpert-like). The single-cell tri-state click, grantee/category pickers, filter, legend, and the table column-level panel are **unchanged**.

**Reuse / style.** New `Button.bulk-glyph` + the `StackPanel.row-bulk` hover styles live in the view's local `<UserControl.Styles>` next to `priv-cell` (view-specific, not duplicated). Glyphs are text (`UiStrings.SecurityGrantGlyph`/`…GrantOptionGlyph`/`…RevokeGlyph` = ✓ / ✓+ / ✕) to match the existing tri-state cell converter vocabulary — NOT `SvgIcon` (this grid's established local convention; ✓+ has no clean Lucide glyph). Colors via theme tokens only.

**Files** (5): [SecurityPaneViewModels.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/SecurityPaneViewModels.cs) (enum + `StatementFor` + 9 commands + confirm), [SecurityManagerTabView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/SecurityManagerTabView.axaml) (toolbar, 7 header trios, Object-cell hover trio, 3-item context menu, 2 styles), [UiStrings.cs](src/EmberTern.App/UiStrings.cs) (glyph/label/tip/confirm strings; removed dead ones), [SecurityManagerTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/SecurityManagerTests.cs) (rewrote the picker test → `BulkCommands_ThreeActionsPerScope_NoOpWithoutGrantee` + `RowBulkCommands_ThreeActions_Exist_AndNoOpWithoutGrantee`). The toolbar `<UserControl.Styles>` `bulk-glyph` reuses the `priv-cell` hover idiom (`/template/ ContentPresenter#PART_ContentPresenter`).

### Index Detail — dedicated 2-tab read-mostly surface (shipped 2026-06-30)

Indexes open in a dedicated **Index Detail** experience instead of a plain DDL tab: two tabs — **Index** (the properties form: Name / Table / Constraint type / Fields-or-Expression / Unique / Sort direction / Statistics / Active / Description) and **DDL** (read-only reconstructed `CREATE INDEX`). Deliberately the **most read-only** detail editor in the app — empirically, the only mutable index properties in Firebird are Active/Inactive and the description. **1875 → 1924 tests** (+49). Build clean (0/0), 12s smoke OK. Lab DB extended with standalone indexes (no new database).

**Empirical Firebird 5.0.3 verification (embedded isql, scratch + lab DBs — NOT guessed):**

| Operation | Result |
|---|---|
| `ALTER INDEX ix ACTIVE` / `INACTIVE` on a **plain** index | ✅ toggles `RDB$INDEX_INACTIVE` |
| `ALTER INDEX` on a **PK / UNIQUE / FK** backing index | ❌ "Cannot deactivate index used by a … constraint" |
| `ALTER INDEX ix TO newname` (rename) | ❌ "Token unknown — TO" (no index rename in Firebird) |
| `COMMENT ON INDEX ix IS …` (plain **and** constraint-backed) | ✅ writes/clears `RDB$INDICES.RDB$DESCRIPTION` on both |
| `SET STATISTICS INDEX ix` (plain **and** constraint-backed) | ✅ works on both |
| Index in `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` | only **expression** indexes (type 6, on their referenced columns); nothing depends *on* an index → **no Dependencies tab** (justified) |

**Architecture decision — a focused form-based `IndexDetailTabViewModel`, NOT a `SourceObjectDetailTabViewModel` subclass.** An index has no PSQL body, no Source/Easy mode, no New flow. Modeled on `GeneratorDetailTabViewModel` (dirty tracking + `IUnsavedWorkSource` + `ConfirmationRequested` + `EnsureLoadedAsync`/`RefreshAsync` + Compile/Revert/Delete), minus the New/IsNew machinery, plus constraint-backed gating + a Recompute-statistics action. **No standalone New-Index flow** (per the user's call — index creation stays exclusively in Table Detail → Indeksy; no second creation path to maintain).

**Editable scope:** **Active** (plain indexes only — `IsActiveEditable => !IsConstraintBacked`; the engine rejects deactivating a constraint index) and **Description** (`COMMENT ON INDEX`, allowed for any index). Everything else is read-only (no ALTER, no rename). **Constraint-backed indexes are clearly identified in the UI** (the user's added requirement): the Constraint type field shows PRIMARY KEY/UNIQUE/FOREIGN KEY, and a `WarningBrush` note ("Backs a … constraint — Firebird manages it through the constraint, so it can't be deactivated or dropped directly. Use Table Detail → Constraints.") appears, making it immediately obvious why the Active toggle is disabled and Drop greys out (`CanDelete => !IsConstraintBacked`).

**Reuse (the brief's hard requirement):** DDL generator `BuildCreateIndex`/`BuildDropIndex`/`BuildSetIndexStatistics` (existed) + new `BuildAlterIndexActive`/`BuildAlterIndexInactive`/`BuildCommentIndex` (→ `BuildRelationComment("INDEX", …)`)/`BuildIndexDdl` (reconstruction). The Generator detail VM/view shape, the routing/factory/dedup/persistence/lazy-load/toolbar pattern, the `Icon.Index` + `IconColor_Index` (already in the metadata tree), `Icon.RefreshCw` (recompute), `Icon.Hammer`/`Icon.Undo`/`Icon.Trash`.

**Core** — [IndexDetailInfo.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Metadata/IndexDetailInfo.cs) (single-index read: Table / Fields / Unique / Descending / Active / Statistics / Expression / ConstraintType / Description / IsSystem; `IsConstraintBacked => ConstraintType != ""`). [DdlGenerator.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Metadata/DdlGenerator.cs): the new Alter/Comment/Ddl methods (`BuildIndexDdl` emits the expression source as-is when already parenthesized to avoid double-parens; appends `/* INACTIVE */` + a `COMMENT ON INDEX` line). `WorkspaceTabKind.IndexDetail` added to the Core persistence enum.

**Firebird** ([FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs)) — `GetIndexDetailAsync(indexName)` + `IndexDetailSql` (keyed by index name, discovers the owning table; constraint subquery keeps **UNIQUE** — unlike the grid's `IndexesSql` which narrows to PK/FK — so a UNIQUE-constraint backing index is recognized) + `NormalizeConstraintIndexType`. Metadata-lane, capture-lock-once (gotcha #98/#120). The `FirebirdDdlReader` Index placeholder is left untouched (unreachable — `OpensAsIndexDetail` routes every index open to the detail surface, whose DDL tab is built from `DdlGenerator.BuildIndexDdl`; same pattern as Domain).

**App** — [IndexDetailTabViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/IndexDetailTabViewModel.cs) (`CompileCommand` emits the minimum-set via `BuildCompileSql`: ALTER INDEX when Active changed [plain only] + COMMENT ON INDEX when description changed; `RecomputeStatisticsCommand` → `SET STATISTICS INDEX` then refresh; `RevertChangesCommand`; `DeleteCommand` gated on `!IsConstraintBacked`; DDL rebuilt live from the loaded facts + current Active/Description). [IndexDetailTabView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/IndexDetailTabView.axaml)(.cs) — the form (read-only TextBoxes + disabled Unique CheckBox + editable Active CheckBox + editable Description + the constraint-backed note) + read-only DDL editor. All DDL auto-commits via the autonomous path (no Commit/Rollback pair on an index tab, like Trigger/Generator/Domain/Exception).

**Routing/persistence/toolbar** ([MainWindowViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs), [MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml), [WorkspaceTabViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/WorkspaceTabViewModel.cs)) — parallel to the Generator path: `OpensAsIndexDetail` + `CreateIndexDetail` factory (wires `ConfirmationRequested`/`DeleteRequested`) + `OnOpenDdlRequested` branch + `(Kind,Name)` dedup; `OnIndexDeleteRequested` (DROP INDEX → close tab → refresh tree); `IsIndexDetailTabActive`/`ActiveIndexDetail` + notify chain + `SelectTab` lazy-load + `IsClosableTabActive`; native `CoreTabKind.IndexDetail` Snapshot/Restore (lazy-load + seed cached DDL); Compile/Revert/Recompute/Delete editor-toolbar buttons gated on `IsIndexDetailTabActive`; sibling `<views:IndexDetailTabView>`. New strings in [UiStrings.cs](src/EmberTern.App/UiStrings.cs). **No New-Index toolbar button.**

**Double-click in the metadata tree** (the connection-level Index category — which lists constraint-backed indexes too, all `RDB$SYSTEM_FLAG=0`) opens Index Detail, via the shared `OpensAsIndexDetail` predicate.

**Lab** ([setup.sql](Lab/setup.sql)) — added standalone indexes on existing tables: plain (`IDX_ORDERS_DATE` + a COMMENT), descending (`IDX_ORDERS_AMT`), composite (`IDX_ITEMS_PROD_QTY`), standalone unique (`IDX_CUST_EMAIL_U` — a plain unique index, distinct from the constraint-backed `UQ_CUSTOMERS_MAIL`), expression (`IDX_CUST_NAME_UPPER`), and inactive (`IDX_ORDERS_STATUS`). Recreated the committed `.fdb` via build-at-ASCII-then-copy (gotcha #149); `IndexDetailSql` verified against the rebuilt lab DB (every variant returns the expected shape; the standalone unique index correctly shows `CT=null` while the constraint one shows UNIQUE).

**Tests** ([IndexDetailTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/IndexDetailTests.cs), +49): `OpensAsIndexDetail` predicate; factory (Compile/Delete/Recompute capability); direct-open → IndexDetail tab (not DDL) + dedup; native restore; capture persists as IndexDetail; DDL builders (alter active/inactive incl. quote-escaping, comment cases, reconstruction plain/unique+descending/expression-no-double-paren/inactive/with-description, empty throws); `BuildCompileSql` (no-op / deactivate plain / describe / constraint-backed emits no ALTER); constraint-backed gating (Active+Delete disabled + note); plain-index allows; sort-direction text; dirty tracking; Revert gating; Delete raises after confirm + constraint-backed no-op; GetUnsavedWork; `IndexDetailSql` shape pin + `NormalizeConstraintIndexType` theory.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

153. **A Firebird index is almost entirely read-only — the ONLY mutable properties are Active/Inactive (plain indexes only) and the description (COMMENT ON INDEX); there is no rename and no structural ALTER.** Verified on FB 5.0.3: `ALTER INDEX … ACTIVE/INACTIVE` works on a plain index but is rejected for PRIMARY/UNIQUE/FOREIGN KEY backing indexes ("Cannot deactivate index used by a … constraint"); `ALTER INDEX … TO` (rename) is a syntax error; `COMMENT ON INDEX` and `SET STATISTICS INDEX` work on every index (incl. constraint-backed); changing fields/UNIQUE/sort-direction/expression requires DROP + CREATE. So an index editor is a read-mostly form, NOT a designer: gate the Active toggle + Drop on `IsConstraintBacked` (PK/FK/UNIQUE), allow description + recompute universally, and surface a clear note explaining why constraint-backed indexes are locked. A constraint-backed index is distinguished by a matching `RDB$RELATION_CONSTRAINTS` row (PRIMARY KEY/UNIQUE/FOREIGN KEY) — note the single-index query must keep UNIQUE in that subquery (the table-grid query narrows to PK/FK, surfacing UNIQUE via `IsUnique` instead). Index *creation* belongs in the table designer (it needs the table context), not a standalone editor — don't build a second creation path. Indexes are NOT meaningfully present in `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` (only expression indexes, on their own columns), so a dependency tab isn't warranted.

### Metadata Tree & Context Menu sprint (shipped 2026-07-01)

Made the sidebar tree the primary navigation + management surface, and fixed three UX defects first. **1924 → 1973 tests** (+49). Build 0/0, 8s smoke clean. Design-first: only triggers/indexes get real activate/deactivate (Firebird has no inactive state for proc/func/pkg — those get **Recompile**); bulk trigger ops act on the **visible (filtered) set** or **all** (NO TreeView multi-select — the filter IS the selection, mirroring the Security Manager bulk pattern); one dedicated **batch-results dialog** for every bulk op.

**Part 1 — fixes.**
- **Refresh gate** — `MetadataExplorerViewModel.RefreshCommand` was `[RelayCommand]` with no `CanExecute` (always enabled). Added `CanRefresh => _connectionService.IsConnected` + re-notify via `ActiveConnectionChanged` (marshalled to the UI thread, gotcha #11).
- **Toolbar collapse** — the editor-toolbar `<Border Grid.Row="1">` had no `IsVisible`, so an empty command strip reserved space above the tabs. New computed `MainWindowViewModel.ShowEditorToolbar` (OR of the per-tab-kind flags; false for no-tab and for the command-less Security Manager tab) bound to the Border; the `Auto` row collapses to 0.
- **Filter/scroll lag — root cause + real fix (gotcha #154).** Filtering flipped `MetadataNodeViewModel.IsVisible=false` on non-matching leaves but LEFT them in `group.Children` (the VSP's ItemsSource) → the `VirtualizingStackPanel` still realized/measured every hidden zero-height row, corrupting the scroll extent on 1000+-row categories. Fix: filter the **collection**, not `IsVisible` — a per-group `_allLeaves` master list + `SetLeaves`/`ApplyLeafFilter` rebuild `Children` to matches only. `EnumerateLoadedObjects` (autocomplete/name-resolution) now reads `AllLeaves` so a filtered tree doesn't shrink its set.

**Part 2 — context menus.** One shared conditional `ContextMenu` on the metadata-node template, driven by generic gates/labels on `MetadataNodeViewModel` (`SupportsNew`/`ContextNewLabel`, `IsRecompilableGroup`, `IsTriggerGroup`, `IsProcedureLeaf`, `ShowActivate`/`ShowDeactivate`, `CanDeleteLeaf`, `ContextEditLabel`, `IsInactive`). Per category: New (all except Index/SystemTable) · Edit=OpenDdl (existing `OpensAsXxxDetail` dispatcher — no new open logic) · generic Delete · Procedure Execute · Trigger activate/deactivate + group bulk · proc/func/trigger/pkg "Recompile all" · SystemTable read-only (Open + Copy Name) · Role/User → Security Manager. The node fires owner-dispatch events (`NewObjectRequested`/`DeleteObjectRequested`/`ExecuteProcedureRequested`/`RecompileGroupRequested`/`SetObjectActiveRequested`/`BulkSetActiveRequested`) consolidated from the old table-specific ones. New `DdlGenerator.BuildDropView/Procedure/Trigger/Function` + `BuildDrop(kind,name)` dispatcher + `BuildAlterTriggerActive/Inactive`. Generic delete = confirm → `BuildDrop` → `_ddlExecutor.ExecuteAsync` → `CloseTabsForObject` → refresh. **Inactive triggers/indexes** carry `MetadataObject.IsActive` (new optional init prop; trigger/index `SqlFor` gained the `RDB$*_INACTIVE` column) and render dimmed + "(inactive)" in the tree.

**Part 3 — connection node + bulk pipeline.** Connection-node menu gained **Refresh metadata**, **Recompute statistics (all indexes)** (`SET STATISTICS INDEX` per index), **Recompile all objects** (re-run each proc/func/trigger/pkg's stored `CREATE OR ALTER`/`RECREATE` source). One reusable pipeline `MainWindowViewModel.RunBatchWithReportAsync` over the existing `FirebirdConnectionService.ExecuteAdminBatchAsync` (per-statement autonomous autocommit, continues past failures, null=ok/error per statement) → new [BatchResultsDialog](src/EmberTern.App/Views/BatchResultsDialog.axaml) (Object / Operation / Result / Error + Total/Success/Failed/Duration footer), reused by recompile, recompute, and trigger activate/deactivate. Bulk trigger ops skip objects already in the target state (via `IsActive`) to avoid needless commits (gotcha #109). Recompile per-object source is sent as ONE command (no `;`-split), so PSQL bodies stay intact.

**Part 4 — multi-select: not built** (documented decision). The filter is the selection mechanism (visible vs all), so no `TreeView` multi-select was introduced — avoids collisions with the collection-rebuild filter, lazy load, mixed node types, and drag&drop.

**Known limitations.** Recompile runs on a transient admin attachment, so a proc currently in use by the data connection fails "object is in use" (cross-attachment, gotcha #122) — reported as a Failed row (IBExpert-parity). Tree "Execute Procedure" opens the proc tab then fires its Execute command (guarded by CanExecute). Live-DB flow is the user's manual pass; the DDL builders, batch report, node gates/commands/events, and the SqlFor inactive-column shape are unit-pinned ([MetadataTreeContextMenuTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/MetadataTreeContextMenuTests.cs), +49).

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

154. **Filtering a virtualized `TreeView`/`ListBox` by toggling item `IsVisible=false` does NOT fix scroll performance — the hidden items stay in the `ItemsSource`, so the `VirtualizingStackPanel` still allocates/measures a layout slot for each one and computes the scroll extent across all of them.** On a 1500-row category filtered to a handful, ~1495 zero-height containers make scrolling jerky, the thumb wrong, and selection/hit-testing laggy. The fix is to filter the **collection** the panel binds to (keep an unfiltered master list; rebuild the displayed `Children` to matches only), so the VSP only ever sees rows that are actually shown. Corollary: any consumer that needs the FULL set (autocomplete, name resolution, bulk-"all") must read the master list, not the now-filtered displayed collection.

### Batch UX (live results) + TreeView scroll investigation (2026-07-01, in progress)

Follow-up on the Metadata sprint. **1973 → 1975 tests.** Build clean, smoke OK. Uncommitted.

**Batch results dialog is now LIVE (P2 — done).** The `BatchResultsDialog` opens **immediately** when a bulk op starts and streams in as it runs, instead of appearing after completion:
- `FirebirdConnectionService.ExecuteAdminBatchAsync` gained `IProgress<(int Index, string? Error)>? progress` (reported after each statement) + honors the `CancellationToken` (checks `IsCancellationRequested` before each statement → stops the remainder; already-run statements are autonomously committed). Params added AFTER `ct` so the positional callers (`FirebirdDdlExecutor`, `TableDetailTabViewModel` recompute) are unaffected. `FirebirdDdlExecutor.ExecuteAutonomousBatchAsync` forwards the progress.
- [BatchResultsViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/BatchResultsViewModel.cs) rewritten from a static report to a **live** VM: `Begin(total)` / `AddResult` / `Complete`, observable `Total`/`Processed`/`SuccessCount`/`FailedCount`/`DurationText` (a `DispatcherTimer` ticks the duration) + a `StatusSummary` footer line, All/Success/Failed filter (`SelectedFilterIndex` → rebuild `VisibleRows` from the `_all` master), `CancelCommand` (cancels a `CancellationTokenSource` the VM owns; `CancellationToken` exposed to the executor), and `CopyAll`/`CopyFailed` → `CopyRequested` event carrying TSV (the dialog window writes the clipboard). The Core `BatchResultsReport` record was removed (streaming replaced it).
- `MainWindowViewModel.RunBatchWithReportAsync` now: builds the VM, seeds pre-failures, opens the dialog (`BatchResultsRequested` → `Func<BatchResultsViewModel,Task>`), runs the executor concurrently with a UI-thread `Progress<T>` feeding `AddResult`, and on dialog-close calls `RequestCancel()` then awaits the exec. [BatchResultsDialog.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/BatchResultsDialog.axaml) got the filter ComboBox + Copy All/Copy Failed toolbar + live footer (summary + Cancel[while running] + Close).

**TreeView scroll-jump (P1 — investigation, NOT yet fixed).** Confirmed it is NOT the filter (the no-filter path was never touched) and NOT the cheaper suspects: headless probes (real `MainWindow`, real templates, 2- and 3-level nesting, selection + `AutoScrollToSelectedItem`, container `IsExpanded`/`IsVisible` bindings) all showed a **perfectly stable** `ScrollViewer` extent + offset (extent constant, offset held at every position). So it's not measurable layout math — it only manifests in the **live render + real scrollbar-thumb drag**, which headless masks because `RunJobs()` fully resolves every measure pass. Leading hypothesis: Avalonia `VirtualizingStackPanel` **estimates** the scroll extent from the realized window and refines it mid-drag → the thumb (a function of that estimate) fights/snaps. Per the user's "prove the cause, no workaround": added **`EMBERTERN_SCROLL_DIAG`** live instrumentation ([ScrollTrace](src/EmberTern.App/Diagnostics/ScrollTrace.cs) + `MainWindow.HookSidebarScrollDiagnostics`) that logs `offsetY`/`extentH`/`viewportH`/`dOffset`/`dExtent` on every scroll change (flags `<-- EXTENT RE-ESTIMATED DURING SCROLL` when `extentH` shifts) AND logs every tree-collection rebuild (`[rebuild] ReloadConnections`/`ApplyFilter`/`LoadGroup`). **Awaiting the user's live log** to decide: extent-refine-during-drag → migrate the sidebar to **TreeDataGrid** (single flat VSP); a `[rebuild]` line mid-drag → an EmberTern code bug to fix directly. Multi-select stays out (the filter is the selection surface).

### Batch Operations UX polish — instant feedback via a preparing phase (shipped 2026-07-01)

Root-caused + fixed the "invoke a bulk op → 10–15 s before any feedback" delay on large schemas. **1975 → 1983 tests** (+8). Build 0/0 (only the 2 pre-existing IndexDetailTabView Watermark warnings), 8 s smoke OK. No redesign of `BatchResultsDialog` or the execution engine — Option A of the sprint brief.

**Root cause (from code, then instrumented — NOT the dialog).** The dialog already opens up front for *execution* (prior "live results" milestone). The delay was entirely **before** the dialog opened: each handler built its full `steps` list first, and for the recompile ops that build is the cost — `OnRecompileGroupRequested`/`RecompileAllObjectsAsync` did `ListAsync` (1 round-trip) then a loop of **one `FetchXxxSourceAsync` DB round-trip per object** (procedure/function/trigger/package source), THEN called `RunBatchWithReportAsync` (which opened the dialog). On the user's ~1965-procedure schema that's ~1965 sequential source fetches with nothing on screen. `RecomputeAllIndexStatistics` / bulk trigger activate-deactivate were already fast (1 `ListAsync` + in-memory SQL) — no delay there.

**Fix — a preparation phase, dialog opens FIRST.** [BatchResultsViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/BatchResultsViewModel.cs) gained an `IsPreparing` phase (default true) with `PreparationStatus` / `PreparationValue` / `PreparationTotal` / `PreparationIsIndeterminate` / `PreparationFailed` + a `CanCancel` computed (`(IsPreparing && !PreparationFailed) || IsRunning` — preparation is cancellable too). `ReportPreparation(status)` (indeterminate) / `ReportPreparation(value,total,status)` (determinate, e.g. "Loading procedures 143 / 1965") / `FailPreparation(msg)`; `Begin(total)` flips preparing→execution. [BatchResultsDialog.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/BatchResultsDialog.axaml) shows a centered progress panel (bar + status, red on prep-failure) over Row 2 while `IsPreparing`, hides the filter/copy toolbar + footer summary until execution, and Cancel is bound to `CanCancel`. The existing live execution view (rows, counters, filters, Copy) is unchanged.

**Pipeline restructure.** `MainWindowViewModel.RunBatchWithReportAsync` now takes a `Func<BatchResultsViewModel, CancellationToken, Task<BatchPlan>> prepareAsync` instead of pre-built steps. It constructs the VM, **starts `RunAsync()` (which runs prepare → `Begin` → execute) then awaits `show(vm)`** — so the dialog opens in the preparing view immediately while prepare streams progress into it on the UI thread (`ConfigureAwait(true)` throughout → `ReportPreparation` is UI-thread-safe with no marshalling). A prep failure (object-list read error) → `FailPreparation` in the dialog + the Messages log entry (unchanged). Each of the 4 bulk entry points now supplies a prepare delegate (`BuildTriggerBulkPlanAsync` / `BuildRecomputeStatsPlanAsync` / `BuildRecompileAllPlanAsync` + `BuildRecompileStepsAsync(kind, vm, ct)`), which reports "Loading …" / "Building operation list…" / per-object progress and threads the `CancellationToken` into `ListAsync`/`FetchXxxSourceAsync` + `ct.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` between objects (so Cancel stops the *fetch loop* too). `record struct BatchPlan(Steps, PreResults)`. Behavior deltas: (a) a no-op (e.g. "Activate visible triggers" when all already active) now opens a dialog that immediately shows a completed 0/0 result **plus** the existing quiet "Nothing to do" Info message — deliberate for consistency + instant feedback; (b) `RecompileAll` records a per-kind list failure as a failed row and continues the other kinds (same resilience as before, now surfaced).

**Instrumentation (measure, don't guess).** New env-gated [BatchTrace](src/EmberTern.App/Diagnostics/BatchTrace.cs) (`EMBERTERN_BATCH_DIAG`, writes to the shared `%TEMP%\EmberTern-debug.log`, modeled on `PerfTrace`) logs `list-enumerate` (kind, count, ms), `source-fetch` (kind, fetched/failed, ms — the dominant cost), `prepare-done` (steps, total prep ms), `exec-done` (steps, ms, cancelled). Procedure to capture the real breakdown on the live schema is in the file header. **The pre-dialog wait is now zero** structurally (dialog opens before the fetch loop); the `BATCH [source-fetch]` line quantifies how long the preparing view is shown. Large-object-set GUI verification is the user's manual pass (the pipeline can't connect to the ~1965-object production DB from CI; the state machine is unit-pinned).

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

155. **"Open the results dialog immediately" isn't enough — the delay can be entirely in building the operation list BEFORE the dialog opens.** The batch dialog already opened up front for *execution*, yet the user still waited 10–15 s because the caller enumerated + fetched one source-per-object to build `steps` before invoking the dialog at all. The fix is a **preparation phase inside the dialog**: pass a `prepare(vm, ct)` delegate, open the dialog in an `IsPreparing` state, run the enumerate/fetch/SQL-build with the dialog already on screen (reporting `ReportPreparation` progress), then `Begin()` into the execution view. Start the prepare+execute task BEFORE awaiting the modal `ShowDialog` (same pattern as the execution task) so the dialog and the prepare loop cooperate on the UI thread. Any "show progress immediately" work must cover *preparation*, not just execution — audit where the pre-work actually happens.

### TreeView scroll-jump — IsVisible/layout-cycle bug fixed, but scroll symptom PERSISTS (investigation ongoing, 2026-07-01)

> ⚠️ **CORRECTION (later same day).** The heading below originally read "ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED." That was an **overclaim** — user-verified. The IsVisible fix DID eliminate the `IsVisible…ConnectionNodeViewModel` binding-error flood AND the `[Layout]…VirtualizingStackPanel` layout-cycle warning (both confirmed gone in the post-fix VS Output), but the **scroll symptom is unchanged** (thumb still fights / snaps back / unstable). So the IsVisible/layout-cycle bug (gotcha #156) was a **real contributor, not the root cause**. The root cause is still open — see "Round 2" below. Lesson: do not declare a UI root cause fixed without the user's live confirmation of the *symptom* (the layout-cycle warning disappearing is not the same as the scroll being smooth).

The IsVisible fix (still valid, still shipped) —

**Evidence chain (all from the logs + code, no guessing):**
1. **VS Output, dozens of times** (lines 136–235): `An error occurred binding 'IsVisible' to 'IsVisible' … 'Could not find a matching property accessor for 'IsVisible' on 'EmberTern.App.ViewModels.ConnectionNodeViewModel''` (and `FolderNodeViewModel`), each on a `TreeViewItem`.
2. **VS Output line 236**, sandwiched *inside* that error burst (235 → **236** → 237): `[Layout]Layout cycle detected. Item 'Avalonia.Controls.VirtualizingStackPanel' was enqueued '10' times.` — Avalonia's 10× re-enqueue safety cap tripping.
3. **[MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml):345–347** — ONE global `<Style Selector="TreeViewItem">` sets `IsVisible` on **every** container via `{ReflectionBinding IsVisible, FallbackValue=True}` (the single-style-with-ReflectionBinding pattern from gotcha #38).
4. **Code fact**: `MetadataNodeViewModel` has `[ObservableProperty] _isVisible` (live — used for the zero-match category hide at [MetadataExplorerViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MetadataExplorerViewModel.cs):400,429), but `ConnectionNodeViewModel` and `FolderNodeViewModel` had **no** `IsVisible`. `IsExpanded` is on all three — which is exactly why the same style's `IsExpanded` ReflectionBinding never errored and `IsVisible` did.

**Mechanism.** Every time the `VirtualizingStackPanel` realizes/recycles a connection or folder container during a scroll, the `IsVisible` ReflectionBinding fails (no accessor), resolves to `FallbackValue=True`, and **re-asserts the container's `IsVisible` mid-measure** → the panel re-invalidates measure → re-enqueues → the 10× cycle cap trips → the scroll extent never settles → the ScrollViewer's committed `Offset` reverts → the thumb "snaps back". The `IsVisible` setter is a **leftover of the IsVisible→collection filtering migration** (gotcha #154): filtering no longer hides leaves via `IsVisible`, and only the metadata *group* node still toggles `IsVisible` — but the container-level setter was left applying to all three node types, two of which never had the property.

**Why the scroll diagnostics captured nothing** (debug log: only `SCROLL [rebuild] scroll diagnostics hooked`, zero `SCROLL offsetY=` lines). The hook subscribed **only to `ScrollChanged`**. A thumb drag whose offset never *commits* (the layout cycle reverts it — the very symptom) raises **no net `ScrollChanged`**, so there was nothing to log. This is itself corroboration: the offset was churning inside layout but never settling. (Secondary: `FirstOrDefault()` could pick a nested ScrollViewer.) The diagnostic is now hardened — targets `PART_ScrollViewer` by name + observes `Offset`/`Extent` **property changes** directly (captures churn even when no committed `ScrollChanged`) — so the post-fix state can be re-measured.

**Fix.** Give `ConnectionNodeViewModel` + `FolderNodeViewModel` a stable `public bool IsVisible => true;` — making all three node types satisfy the shared container style's binding contract, exactly as they already all satisfy `IsExpanded`. This eliminates the per-realization binding failure and the mid-measure visibility re-assertion, breaking the feedback loop that fed the VSP layout cycle. `MetadataNodeViewModel` keeps its real observable `IsVisible` (zero-match category hide unaffected). Connection/folder rows are never filter-hidden, so always-true is correct.

**Verification.** Build 0/0, 1983/1983 tests. Live GUI confirmation is the user's pass: after the fix the VS Output must show **no** `IsVisible … ConnectionNodeViewModel` binding errors and **no** `[Layout]Layout cycle detected … VirtualizingStackPanel` while scrolling a large expanded category. If the layout-cycle warning somehow persists after the binding errors are gone, THEN (and only then) is the Avalonia nested-VSP extent limitation proven and TreeDataGrid justified — but the evidence says it will be gone.

**Files:** [ConnectionNodeViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ConnectionNodeViewModel.cs), [FolderNodeViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/FolderNodeViewModel.cs) (the fix), [MainWindow.axaml.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs) `HookSidebarScrollDiagnostics` (hardened diagnostic).

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

156. **A global `<Style Selector="TreeViewItem">` (or any container style) that binds a property via `ReflectionBinding` requires EVERY node-type in that TreeView to expose that property — a missing accessor doesn't just log noise, it feeds a `VirtualizingStackPanel` layout cycle.** The shared style binds `IsExpanded` AND `IsVisible` across three node types (Connection/Folder/Metadata). `IsExpanded` is on all three → fine. `IsVisible` was on only one → on the other two the ReflectionBinding failed per realized container, resolved to `FallbackValue`, and re-asserted the container's `IsVisible` during the panel's measure pass → measure re-invalidated → VSP re-enqueued to the 10× cap → scroll extent never settled → the scrollbar thumb fought/snapped back. The failure surfaces as (a) a flood of `Could not find a matching property accessor for 'X'` binding errors in the debug output and (b) `[Layout]Layout cycle detected … VirtualizingStackPanel was enqueued '10' times`. **Rule:** when a container-level ReflectionBinding is added for one node type, add the bound property (a stable literal is fine for types that never change it) to ALL node types the style matches — `x:DataType` does NOT scope a container style at runtime (gotcha #38), so there's no way to bind it for only one type. Corollary for diagnostics: a scroll issue whose offset never *commits* raises no net `ScrollChanged`; observe `ScrollViewer.Offset`/`Extent` property changes to see the churn. And: a filtering-mechanism migration (gotcha #154, IsVisible→collection) must also remove the now-orphaned container bindings it leaves behind.

**Round 2 — IsVisible fixed, symptom persists; every EmberTern-side cause now ruled out by code (2026-07-01).** After the IsVisible fix the user re-tested: the binding errors + layout-cycle warning are **gone** (clean post-fix VS Output) but the scroll still fights / snaps back. So the layout cycle was one contributor; the scroll jump has another cause. **The post-fix `EmberTern-debug.log` contained NO scroll telemetry** — `EMBERTERN_SCROLL_DIAG` was never set that session, so the hardened diagnostic silently no-op'd (its whole `Enabled` gate was env-only). Fix for *that*: `ScrollTrace` is now **default-ON** (silence with `EMBERTERN_SCROLL_DIAG=0`) and each `SCROLL` line now carries `realized=<N>` (count of realized `TreeViewItem` containers) so extent churn can be correlated with realization in one ordinary run.

EmberTern-side causes ruled out **by reading the code** (not assumed): (a) filter — untouched path, symptom present with no filter; (b) IsVisible/layout-cycle — fixed, symptom persists; (c) auto-scroll-to-selection / `BringIntoView` / `ScrollIntoView` — **none** on `SidebarTree` (only in `SearchableComboBox`); (d) tree rebuild during scroll — `ReloadConnections` only runs on connect/edit, would log `[rebuild]`; (e) drag-drop pointer handlers ([MainWindow.axaml.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs):589+) — `OnSidebarPointerMoved` early-returns unless `_dragSource` is set, and `_dragSource` is set only when the press lands on a Folder/Connection **row**, so a scrollbar-thumb drag never engages them; (f) double/wrapping ScrollViewer — the tree is directly in a `DockPanel`, single `PART_ScrollViewer`.

**Remaining suspect (now the only one left): the nested `VirtualizingStackPanel`** — [MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml):302–314 puts a VSP on BOTH `TreeView.ItemsPanel` and the per-item `TreeViewItem.ItemsPanel`. Avalonia's VSP computes `Extent` from the realized window (average realized height × remaining count); in a TreeView with a 2000+-leaf expanded category the extent is mostly *estimate*, and each new realization during a thumb drag corrects the estimate → `Extent` changes → the ScrollViewer recomputes the thumb → jump/snap-back. This is the documented Avalonia TreeView limitation CLAUDE.md already flagged, and it's consistent with the prior headless probes showing a *stable* extent (headless `RunJobs()` fully settles layout, so it can't reproduce a live realize-on-scroll effect). **This remains a hypothesis until the live telemetry confirms it** — do NOT migrate yet.

**Round 3 — extent instability PROVEN by telemetry; uniform-height fix shipped (Step 1, 2026-07-01).** With the default-ON diagnostic, the user dragged the thumb in a large category and captured the decisive signature:
```
offsetY=113971  extentH=521589 → 80690   dExtent=-440899   realized=14   <-- EXTENT RE-ESTIMATED DURING SCROLL
then extentH walks 80690 → 74522 → 68352 → 62183 → 56013 → 49844 → 43674 → 37504 → 31335 → 25165
     each step dExtent≈-6169 while realized creeps 3→11
```
Extent swings 6–20× (viewport is only 709, so the thumb is ~0.1% of the track) and collapses below the current offset near the end → the ScrollViewer clamps → **empty tree**. The VS Output shows the matching `[Layout]Layout cycle detected` on `VirtualizingStackPanel`, `Grid`, AND `Track` (the scrollbar reacting to the churning `Maximum`). **Case B confirmed: Avalonia's nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` re-estimates `Extent` = averageRealizedHeight × count, and because rows are NON-uniform height the average — hence the extent — swings as the realized window changes during a drag.** (The extent DOES reflect the full count, so virtualization works; it's the *average* that's unstable, not a broken realize.)

**Step 1 fix (shipped): uniform row height.** [ControlStyles.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Themes/ControlStyles.axaml) pins every TreeViewItem HEADER (`ContentPresenter#PART_HeaderPresenter` + `Grid#PART_Header`) to a fixed 24 px — never the `TreeViewItem` itself (that would clip the child subtree). With every realized row the same height, `averageRealizedHeight` is CONSTANT → `Extent = 24 × count` is exact and stable at both the nested and outer level, so the thumb stops re-estimating — while virtualization is retained (no freeze on 8000+ rows) and the TreeView keeps drag-drop / context-menus / selection / expand-persistence / type-to-filter untouched. Build 0/0, 1983/1983 tests. **The diagnostic is left default-ON so the user's next run confirms `dExtent≈0` during the drag.**

**Step 2 fallback (only if Step 1's telemetry still shows `dExtent≠0`): migrate the sidebar to `TreeDataGrid`** (`HierarchicalTreeDataGridSource` → one flattened, single-level virtualized row stream → exact extent by construction, scales to 100k+). Cost (own sprint): NuGet `Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` (verify 12.0.x compat), rewrite the mixed Folder/Connection/Metadata templates as columns/cell templates, reimplement pointer drag-drop (gotcha #40), context menus, selection, expand-state persistence, type-to-filter, per-kind icons. High regression surface — do it ONLY if uniform height proves insufficient.

**Round 4 — uniform height DISPROVEN + reverted; the collapse is Avalonia re-realization, not an average-height drift (2026-07-01).** User re-captured after the height fix: the symptom is unchanged AND the telemetry signature *changed shape* — instead of a gradual estimate drift, the extent now hard-**collapses to `extentH=709.6` (== viewport) with `realized=4`, `offsetY` clamped to 0, then rebuilds to 36k/136k**, cycling every drag frame (`dExtent` swings of 30k–135k). When extent == viewport the ScrollViewer MUST clamp offset to 0 — so the extent-collapse is the primary event, and it is a **re-realization/reset**, not a smooth average change. The uniform-height style was **reverted** (ineffective, and it may have worsened the pattern by constraining a header part). Investigation of every EmberTern data path (asked by the user) found **nothing being recreated during the drag**: `Children.Clear`/`SetLeaves` only run inside `LoadGroupAsync` (guarded by `IsLoaded`, fires once on first expand — the single `LoadGroup` line, NOT per cycle); `OnIsExpandedChanged` no-ops on collapse and only reloads when `!IsLoaded`; no code toggles `IsExpanded` during scroll; `RootNodes` is only rebuilt by `ReloadConnections` (connect/edit — no line during the drag). **Conclusion: the realized set collapsing to ~4 and the extent to viewport is Avalonia's nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` derealizing the expanded subtree on a random-access thumb drag and mis-estimating the extent — a control-level limitation with no XAML/data fix.** Both cheap EmberTern-side hypotheses (IsVisible #156, uniform height) are now exhausted. **The remaining path is the structural `TreeDataGrid` migration** (single flattened virtualized row stream → stable extent + correct random-access thumb scrolling). Recommended, pending user go-ahead — it's a multi-file sprint (see Step 2 scope above; verify the `Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` NuGet is 12.0.x-compatible first). NOT started blind.

**Round 5 — TreeDataGrid gating FAILED: it's a COMMERCIAL/licensed control on Avalonia 12 (2026-07-01).** Ran the user's gating step on branch `feat/treedatagrid-poc`. Part 1 (version): `Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` **12.0.3** exists (official `avaloniaui` owner) — version-compatible. Part 2 (build the POC): **hard blocker** — the build fails with `AvaloniaUI.Licensing error AVLIC0001: No valid AvaloniaUI license keys found for required commercial products: "Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid"`. Avalonia introduced commercial licensing ("Accelerate") in v12 and TreeDataGrid is a licensed control — it **cannot build or run without an AvaloniaUI license key** from the Avalonia Portal. The POC could not even compile, so per the gating instruction we **STOP and reassess** before touching the production sidebar. POC wiring (package + theme include + code-behind swap) was reverted; branch is back to a green baseline (build 0/0). Reassessed options for the flattened-single-VSP fix (all still target the same principle — one flat virtualized row stream = stable extent + correct random-access thumb scroll): **(1) Official TreeDataGrid + AvaloniaUI license** (obtain a key from the Avalonia Portal — may have a free community tier for individuals/OSS, user must check terms + accept the dependency/licensing). **(2) Community fork** — `Caly.Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` (12.0.1.3) or `Beardmouse.Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` (12.0.0), free Avalonia-12 forks of the pre-commercial TreeDataGrid (trust + maintenance-lag risk). **(3) Hand-rolled flat virtualized list** — a `ListBox` (single VSP) bound to a flattened `ObservableCollection<FlatNode>` (visible rows + indent level), expand/collapse splices children in/out; no NuGet, no license, fully owned; more code but reuses our DataTemplates/commands and is the license-free path. **Recommendation: (3)** — the official control is now paid and a random fork carries risk, whereas a hand-rolled flat list is free, owned, and (unlike a throwaway POC) IS the actual fix. Awaiting the user's choice. See gotcha #158.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

157. **Avalonia's `VirtualizingStackPanel` estimates scroll `Extent` from the AVERAGE height of *realized* rows (`≈ averageRealizedHeight × itemCount`) — so NON-uniform row heights make the extent (and the scrollbar thumb) swing wildly as you scroll, even with virtualization working correctly.** In the sidebar TreeView (nested VSP: outer + per-item) with an 8000-row expanded category, telemetry showed `extentH` lurching 521589 → 80690 → … → 25165 (`dExtent≈-6169` per realization) as the realized window changed from mixed node types (connections/folders/category-headers ~tall) to pure leaves (~short) — the thumb was ~0.1% of the track, re-sized every frame, and collapsed below the offset near the end → blank list. This is NOT a rebuild bug and NOT broken realization (the extent reflects the full count); it's the estimator reacting to a variable average. **Fix without abandoning the control: pin every row to ONE height** so the average is constant → `Extent = rowHeight × count` is exact and stable. Pin the TreeViewItem HEADER parts (`ContentPresenter#PART_HeaderPresenter` + `Grid#PART_Header`), NEVER the `TreeViewItem` itself (that clips the child subtree). Avalonia 12.0.0 has no "assume fixed item size" hint on VSP, so uniform actual heights are the only lever short of migrating to `TreeDataGrid` (single flattened VSP = exact extent by construction). Diagnostic fingerprint to confirm: `SCROLL … dExtent≠0` flagged `EXTENT RE-ESTIMATED DURING SCROLL` correlated with a changing `realized` count, plus `[Layout]Layout cycle detected … Track/VirtualizingStackPanel`. Corollary: an env-gated diagnostic that "captured nothing" may simply not have been enabled — default it ON during an active investigation (gotcha in ScrollTrace) so evidence isn't lost to a missed env var. **⚠️ CORRECTION (Round 4): the "pin every row height" fix was tried and DISPROVEN for EmberTern's sidebar — and reverted.** It addresses only a gentle average-height *drift*; the actual failure is far more severe: on a random-access thumb drag the nested VSP **derealizes the whole expanded subtree**, collapsing `Extent` to == viewport (`realized`≈4) and clamping the offset to 0, then rebuilds — a re-realization cycle uniform heights can't touch. When you see the extent hard-collapse to viewport (not a smooth walk), stop trying style/height fixes: it's the nested-VSP-in-TreeView random-access limitation, and the only real fix is a single flattened virtualized list (`TreeDataGrid`). Verify no EmberTern data recreation first (`Children`/`ItemsSource`/`IsExpanded` mutations during scroll — a `[rebuild]` line) to rule out a genuine app bug, then migrate.

158. **`Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` is a COMMERCIAL/licensed control on Avalonia 12 — it won't build without an AvaloniaUI license key.** Avalonia introduced commercial licensing ("Accelerate") in v12; adding the (version-matched) `Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` 12.0.3 package fails the build with `AvaloniaUI.Licensing error AVLIC0001: No valid AvaloniaUI license keys found for required commercial products`. A NuGet version match does NOT imply it's usable — check licensing before planning any migration onto an Avalonia "Accelerate" control (TreeDataGrid and others). Free alternatives for a flattened-single-VSP tree on Avalonia 12: a community fork (`Caly.Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid`, `Beardmouse.Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` — trust/maintenance risk) or a hand-rolled flat `ListBox` over a flattened `ObservableCollection` (no dependency/license, fully owned). **Rule: gate every NuGet control choice on licensing, not just target-framework/version compatibility — the Avalonia 12 line splits free vs. paid.**

### FlatTree Sidebar Migration — Object Explorer on a single-VSP ListBox (shipped 2026-07-01, branch `feat/flattree-sidebar`)

Self-contained handoff for the Object Explorer sidebar rewrite. If you know nothing about this work, read this whole section. **All 5 phases + review fixes are complete on `feat/flattree-sidebar`; build 0/0; 1996 tests green.** Awaiting the user's final visual/DnD smoke before merge to `master`.

#### Problem
