- **Original issue:** the metadata sidebar was an Avalonia `TreeView` with a **nested `VirtualizingStackPanel`** (a VSP on `TreeView.ItemsPanel` AND on each `TreeViewItem.ItemsPanel`). On a large schema (the user's FB5 ERP: ~2400 tables, ~8200 indexes, thousands of procedures/triggers/etc.) scrolling was broken.
- **Symptoms:** dragging the scrollbar thumb "fought" the user, the thumb jumped back to the top, scrolling felt unstable, and dragging near the end of a big expanded category produced an **empty tree**.
- **Investigation history (the dead ends matter — don't repeat them):**
  - **Hypothesis 1 — the filter left hidden items in the collection (IsVisible=false rows still measured).** Fixed separately (collection-based filtering, gotcha #154) — improved responsiveness but the scroll symptom persisted with no filter. Not the cause.
  - **Hypothesis 2 — a dead `IsVisible` ReflectionBinding on the shared `TreeViewItem` container style feeding a `VirtualizingStackPanel` layout cycle (gotcha #156).** REAL bug, fixed (added `IsVisible => true` to `ConnectionNodeViewModel`/`FolderNodeViewModel`); the binding-error flood + `[Layout]Layout cycle detected` warnings went away — **but the scroll symptom was unchanged.** A contributor, not the root cause.
  - **Hypothesis 3 — non-uniform row heights making the VSP's average-height extent estimate swing (gotcha #157).** Tried pinning a uniform row height; **DISPROVEN and reverted** — telemetry showed a far more severe pattern than a gentle drift.
  - **Diagnostics:** an env-gated `ScrollTrace` (`EMBERTERN_SCROLL_DIAG`, now opt-in again) logging `offsetY / extentH / viewportH / dExtent / realized` per scroll change to `%TEMP%\EmberTern-debug.log`. First run "captured nothing" because the env var wasn't set (lesson: default-ON during an active investigation).
- **Final proven root cause (from telemetry, not assumption):** Avalonia's **nested `VirtualizingStackPanel` inside a `TreeView` cannot do stable random-access thumb scrolling** over a large expanded subtree. On a thumb drag the extent **hard-collapses to `== viewport` (`realized`≈4), the offset clamps to 0, then rebuilds to tens of thousands of px** — a re-realization/reset cycle (`dExtent` swings 30k–135k), with matching `[Layout]Layout cycle detected … Track/Grid/VirtualizingStackPanel`. Verified in code that **no EmberTern collection / expansion / itemsource is recreated during the drag** — it's the control derealizing the subtree. Control-level limitation, no XAML/data fix.

#### Solution
- **Why not `TreeDataGrid`** (the obvious "flattened virtualization" control): `Avalonia.Controls.TreeDataGrid` 12.0.3 is version-compatible **but a COMMERCIAL/licensed Avalonia "Accelerate" control** — the POC build failed `AvaloniaUI.Licensing error AVLIC0001` (no license key). Rejected (gotcha #158). Community forks (Caly/Beardmouse) exist but carry trust/maintenance risk.
- **Why the hand-rolled flat single-VSP `ListBox`:** free, owned, no dependency, and it IS the actual fix (a single-level VSP over a flat list has an exact, stable extent and correct random-access scrolling). Validated first by a throwaway POC (branch `feat/flattree-poc`, kept until accepted) against the production DB — scroll stable, no collapse.
- **Architecture — two new types (both in `src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/`):**
  - **`SidebarRow`** — a thin wrapper around one existing node VM (`Node` = `ConnectionNodeViewModel` / `FolderNodeViewModel` / `MetadataNodeViewModel`) + `Depth` (indent) + observable `IsExpandable` (chevron) + observable `IsExpanded` (chevron glyph). The node stays the source of truth for icons, colors, labels, context menus, expansion, children, persistence.
  - **`SidebarFlatController`** — projects the node hierarchy into a flat `ObservableCollection<SidebarRow> Rows` (the ListBox `ItemsSource`). Delegate-based (childrenSelector / isContainer / hasChildren / isExpanded / setExpanded / isVisible) so the projection logic is unit-testable without real VMs or a DB (13 tests). Owned by `MetadataExplorerViewModel` (created once in its ctor; survives `ReloadConnections` by tracking `RootNodes.CollectionChanged`). Exposed as `Metadata.SidebarRows` + `Metadata.ToggleSidebarRow(row)`.
  - **Two distinct node notions (important):** `isContainer` (structural — can host children; drives subscriptions + recursion; a category is a container even while empty) vs `hasChildren` (currently has children; drives the chevron). Conflating them leaves empty categories unsubscribed.
- **Expand/collapse:** driven by each node's own `IsExpanded` — user chevron clicks (`Toggle` → flips `Node.IsExpanded`), double-click a category, and programmatic expansion (auto-expand-on-connect, restore-expand-state) all flow through the same path. The controller observes `IsExpanded` on container nodes only (never leaves) and **splices incrementally** (insert the node's visible subtree / remove the contiguous deeper block) — the hot path never does a full rebuild.
- **Filtering:** unchanged engine (`ApplyFilterAsync` → per-group `ApplyFilterToGroup`: sets `group.IsVisible`, `FilterMatchCount`, rebuilds `Children` to matches; name cache for un-expanded categories; **no auto-expand**). The flat projection respects `IsVisible` (zero-match categories excluded) and shows the (filtered) children; match counts render via the node template's `DisplayLabel`. The filter wraps its group loop in `SidebarFlatController.BeginUpdate()/EndUpdate()` (nesting-safe suspend + one re-projection) so `ApplyLeafFilter`'s item-by-item `Children` rebuild doesn't splice per item (O(n²) when clearing a filter with a big category expanded).
- **Lazy loading / prefetch:** **prefetch on connect** — `ConnectionNodeViewModel.LoadCategoriesAsync` loads the FULL object list for every category (was count-only) via `LoadGroupAsync`, sequentially (single `FbConnection`). "Lazy rendering, not lazy loading": data is ready up front so expand is instant; UI virtualization means only visible rows realize (safe now — the old nested-VSP TreeView froze because it realized ~2k TreeViewItems per category). `LoadGroupAsync` marks the category loaded, so expand never re-queries. Bulk leaf-populate doesn't thrash the controller (leaf `Add`s carry non-container items → skip the subscription re-walk; a collapsed category's populate only refreshes its chevron).
- **Drag & drop:** the existing pointer-based DnD (gotcha #40) retargeted from `TreeView`/`TreeViewItem` to `ListBox`/`ListBoxItem`, unwrapping `SidebarRow.Node`. `ExecuteDrop`, folder state, `IsDragging`/`IsDropTarget`, and the before/after/into computation are unchanged. Only Folder/Connection rows are drag sources/targets.
- **Persistence:** UNCHANGED — expand-state persistence (`FolderState.ExpandedNodeIds` via `Node.IsExpanded` → `MainWindowViewModel.OnNodeExpansionChanged` → `RestoreExpandState`, gotchas #36/#37) works as before because the flat controller is driven by `Node.IsExpanded`; restoring expansion projects into the flat list automatically.

#### Migration status (all phases complete)
| Phase | What | Commit |
|---|---|---|
| POC | throwaway runnable toggle (TreeView⇄flat), validated on prod DB | `feat/flattree-poc` @ `6fc8543` (kept until accepted) |
| 1 | `SidebarRow` + `SidebarFlatController` + 13 unit tests (no UI) | `1e99132` |
| 2 | flat `ListBox` re-hosting node templates verbatim (visual/menu parity) | `f02724d` |
| review fixes | bigger 20px chevron; chevron only when children exist (`isContainer` vs `hasChildren`); prefetch on connect | `c7b7a50` |
| 3 | drag&drop + type-to-filter retargeted to the ListBox | `2617c16` |
| 4 | filter parity + `BeginUpdate`/`EndUpdate` bulk-update perf guard | `3a4b15b` |
| 5 | cleanup: removed dead `OnSidebarTreeSelectionChanged`; `ScrollTrace` back to opt-in; stale comments; this doc | (this commit) |

- **What was removed from the old TreeView implementation:** the `<TreeView>` + nested `<VirtualizingStackPanel>` item panels; the shared `<Style Selector="TreeViewItem">` container style with the `IsExpanded`/`IsVisible` ReflectionBinding setters (gotcha #38 — the whole class of container-binding problems is gone with the control); the `OnSidebarTreeSelectionChanged` handler; the uniform-row-height experiment (gotcha #157). The three per-type `TreeDataTemplate`s were converted to plain `DataTemplate`s (dropped `ItemsSource`) and re-hosted verbatim via a `ContentControl` in the `SidebarRow` row template. **Gotchas #156/#157 are now historical** — they described TreeView-container/ nested-VSP behavior that no longer exists; #158 (TreeDataGrid licensing) stays relevant for any future control choice.

#### Remaining Work
Everything below is intentionally deferred, not blocking merge:
- **Merge:** `feat/flattree-sidebar` is functionally complete and green; needs the user's final **visual smoke** (chevron feel, no-chevron-on-empty, instant expand) + **hands-on drag & drop test** (can't be verified headless) before merge to `master`. Then `feat/flattree-poc` can be deleted.
- **Prefetch cost on connect:** loading all objects for all 13 categories up front (~15k leaf VMs on the big schema) lengthens connect (one-time; the user chose this tradeoff for instant expand). No progress indicator during prefetch yet — **a subtle "loading…" affordance on the connection node during prefetch is a candidate** if connect feels slow.
- **Big-category expand cost:** expanding a category inserts N rows one-by-one into `Rows` (N `CollectionChanged`); the POC proved this acceptable in practice, but an `AddRange`/reset-style bulk insert would cut the churn if a very large category ever feels slow on expand.
- **UX polish deferred:** no drop-insertion-line indicator (drop feedback is a row-background tint, as before); type-ahead-to-node (jump to a node by typing, distinct from the filter) was removed earlier and not reintroduced (type-to-filter is the current model).
- **Known limitations:** the flat list is single-select (as the tree was); the `OnSidebarTreeTextInput` handler + `FocusSidebarTree` method keep their "Tree" names though they now target the ListBox (cosmetic); `ScrollTrace` + the `HookSidebarScrollDiagnostics` hook remain in the code (opt-in, zero-cost) as a regression instrument.
- **Tech debt to review later:** consider whether prefetch should be capped/streamed for pathologically large schemas; consider extracting the row-template + chevron into a small `UserControl` if the sidebar grows more controls.

### Trigger context menu — scoped submenus + multi-select analysis (shipped 2026-07-01, branch `feat/flattree-sidebar`)

The trigger-category context menu was reorganized from four flat items (Activate visible / Deactivate visible / Activate all / Deactivate all) into two **action → scope** submenus in [MainWindow.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/MainWindow.axaml): **Activate → {Visible, All}** and **Deactivate → {Visible, All}**. Functionality is unchanged — the four leaf items bind the same `ActivateVisibleCommand`/`DeactivateVisibleCommand`/`ActivateAllCommand`/`DeactivateAllCommand`; the parent `MenuItem`s are command-less headers gated on `IsTriggerGroup`, reusing the existing `MetadataContextActivate`/`Deactivate` strings (also used by the single-trigger leaf items). Two new scope strings `MetadataContextScopeVisible`/`ScopeAll` replaced the retired `MetadataContext{Activate,Deactivate}{Visible,All}` strings (dead after the reorg; only ever referenced by this menu). The submenu shape is deliberately left open for a **Selected** scope to drop between Visible and All. 1996/1996 tests green, build 0/0.

**Multi-select (Ctrl/Shift) feasibility — analyzed, NOT implemented (verdict: MEDIUM risk, deferred to a separate task).** The sidebar `ListBox` is `SelectionMode="Single"`; selection has two single-item consumers — `OnSidebarListSelectionChanged` → `Metadata.SelectedConnection` (titlebar Edit/Copy/Delete/Connect), and `DetectFolderContext` → the "+" add-connection folder. Sidebar selection is NOT persisted. Drag & drop is pointer-based and **decoupled** from selection (reads the pointed row via `FindRowVmAtPointer`, never `SelectedItems`; only Folder/Connection rows drag — trigger leaves don't), so DnD is largely unaffected: the only effect of `Multiple` mode is the Avalonia default press-collapse (can't drag a *group* of connections — not a requested feature). The real sharp edge is **selection volatility on full re-projection**: `SidebarFlatController.RebuildRows()` (fired by `ApplyFilter`'s `EndUpdate` / root reload) does `Rows.Clear()` + recreates `SidebarRow` instances → the ListBox drops `SelectedItems`, so a multi-selection evaporates on a filter keystroke or reconnect (incremental expand/collapse splices *should* preserve it — unverified, needs a headless probe). "Selected trigger operations" are cheap once selection exists — the bulk pipeline is already name-list-shaped (`TriggerBulkRequest(Kind, activate, visibleOnly, names)`), so "Selected" is just a third name source + gating. Implementing multi-select needs: `SelectionMode="Multiple"`, `SelectedItems`→`SelectedConnection` reconciliation, a "Selected" command + `TriggerBulkRequest` variant + owner plumbing + gating + unit tests + a **headless probe** characterizing selection survival across splice vs. full rebuild. Not high-risk, but above the do-it-inline bar — recommended as its own scoped follow-up.

### SQL Templates & Drag & Drop (shipped 2026-07-02, merged to `master`)

IBExpert-style SQL generation, redesigned around **Drag & Drop** as the single workflow (no modal, no "generate into a guessed active editor"). Drag a metadata object from the sidebar onto any SQL/PSQL editor → a flyout of applicable templates appears **at the drop point** → pick inserts the generated SQL exactly at the drop offset, first placeholder selected. **2049 tests** (engine + builder + preview fully unit-covered), build 0/0.

**Workflow: `drag → drop → click`.** The drag source is the sidebar (metadata leaves; folders/connections still reorder — no conflict, different row kinds). The drop target is every editable editor.

**Editors (drop targets):** SQL Editor · Procedure Editor · Trigger Editor · Function Editor · Package Editor · View Editor (source + body + cursor/subprogram editors where present; read-only DDL editors excluded).

**Objects (draggable):** Tables · Views · Procedures · Functions · Generators · Exceptions.

**Templates:**
- **Tables:** SELECT * · SELECT Columns · Field List · Parameter List · INSERT (skips computed + identity) · UPDATE (SET skips PK, PK-aware WHERE) · DELETE · UPDATE OR INSERT · **FOR SELECT** (cursor loop from columns) · **DECLARE VARIABLES** (`TYPE OF COLUMN` per column)
- **Views:** SELECT * · SELECT Columns
- **Procedures:** EXECUTE PROCEDURE · SELECT FROM PROCEDURE (selectable) · **FOR SELECT FROM PROCEDURE** (cursor loop; inputs in the call, outputs in SELECT/INTO)
- **Functions:** Function Call (`SELECT f(:args) FROM RDB$DATABASE`)
- **Generators:** NEXT VALUE FOR · GEN_ID
- **Exceptions:** EXCEPTION · EXCEPTION with message (`'message'` tab-stop; FB 2.0+)

**PlainSql vs PsqlBody context.** Every drop target carries a `SnippetInsertionContext` derived from the editor: the main SQL editor + View editors are `PlainSql`; procedure/trigger/function/package body editors are `PsqlBody`. Each template declares the contexts it's valid in (`SqlTemplateDescriptor.Contexts` / `SnippetContexts.Any` vs `PsqlOnly`). The PSQL scaffolds (FOR SELECT, DECLARE VARIABLES, FOR SELECT FROM PROCEDURE, EXCEPTION / EXCEPTION with message) are `PsqlOnly` → they appear only in body editors, never in a plain SQL editor. DML/call/sequence templates are `Any`.

**Tooltip preview.** Hovering a flyout item shows a lightweight monospace tooltip of the exact SQL to be inserted, truncated to 12 lines / 500 chars with an ellipsis, anchored to the **right** of the flyout (`PlacementMode.Right`) so it never covers the list. Deliberately not a preview pane / not a window.

**Lazy metadata loading.** The flyout is built from the object **kind only** — instant, zero catalog reads. Detailed metadata (columns / PK from the PRIMARY KEY constraint / procedure params / function signature) is loaded **once, on the first hover**, via `SnippetContextBuilder`, cached, and shared by every preview AND the final insertion — so the whole interaction costs a single catalog read (and none at all if the user drops-and-clicks without hovering, since the pick triggers the same one load).

**Shared template engine** ([EmberTern.Core/Sql/Templates/](src/EmberTern.Core/Sql/Templates/), zero Avalonia): `ISqlTemplate` (many small impls, no switch) + `SqlTemplateRegistry` + `SqlSnippet`/`SqlPlaceholder` (tab-stop offsets) + `SnippetContext`/`SnippetOptions`, composed by `SqlTemplateCatalog.CreateRegistry()`. Identifier quoting reuses `DdlGenerator.QuoteLight`. `DescriptorsForKind(kind, insertion)` powers the instant menu; `HasTemplatesForKind(kind)` is the drag-start gate; `AppliesTo(ctx)` (kind + context + data) gates generation. App side: [SnippetContextBuilder](src/EmberTern.App/Sql/SnippetContextBuilder.cs) (delegate-seam metadata loaders → the `FirebirdTableDetailReader`, testable without a live FB; PK from `GetConstraintsAsync` per gotcha #103) + [SqlSnippetDropTarget](src/EmberTern.App/Sql/SqlSnippetDropTarget.cs) (`DragDrop`/`DataTransfer` new Avalonia 12.0.3 API, `DataFormat.CreateInProcessFormat<MetadataObject>` in-process payload, flyout + truncated tooltip + insert). VM ([MainWindowViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs)) exposes `SnippetTemplatesFor(kind, insertion)` / `HasSnippetTemplates(kind)` / `BuildSnippetContextAsync` / `GenerateSnippet` (returns Core types only — no Avalonia in the VM contract).

**Full test coverage** ([SqlTemplateTests](tests/EmberTern.Tests/SqlTemplateTests.cs), [SnippetContextBuilderTests](tests/EmberTern.Tests/SnippetContextBuilderTests.cs), [SqlSnippetPreviewTests](tests/EmberTern.Tests/SqlSnippetPreviewTests.cs)): exact SQL per template, PK-aware / computed-identity-excluding / no-PK-placeholder edge cases, quoting, context filtering (PSQL hidden in PlainSql), registry applicability + drag-start gate, context-builder metadata loading per kind + PK-from-constraint, and preview truncation. The drag gesture / flyout / insert are view code-behind — manual smoke (DnD precedent).

**Gotcha promoted to architecture lore.**

159. **Avalonia 12.0.3 replaced the drag/drop data API — `DataObject`/`DragDrop.DoDragDrop`/`DragEventArgs.Data` are gone/obsolete; use `DataTransfer` + `DragDrop.DoDragDropAsync(PointerPressedEventArgs, IDataTransfer, …)` + `DragEventArgs.DataTransfer`.** For an intra-app drag that carries a live object (not OS-serialized), use `DataFormat.CreateInProcessFormat<T>(name)` + `DataTransferItem.Create(format, value)` / `dataTransfer.TryGetValue(format)` — the object travels by reference. `DoDragDropAsync` requires the originating `PointerPressedEventArgs`, so capture it at PointerPressed and start the drag from PointerMoved once past the movement threshold. Cross-control drag (sidebar → a single non-virtualized `TextEditor`) is the built-in API's happy path — distinct from the virtualized-`TreeView`-drop-target unreliability of gotcha #40; the sidebar's own reorder stays pointer-based.

### Data Grid UX — Etap 0 (localization) + Etap 1 (Record N of M) (shipped 2026-07-02)

First slice of the approved "Data Grid Filtering, Aggregations & Navigation" MVP (analysis + phased plan agreed with the user; **filters/aggregations for the materialized grids = Etap 2, next session**; tables/views push-down = a separate later etap the user deferred). **2049 → 2054 tests** (+5). Build 0/0, 8s smoke clean.

**The load-bearing analysis finding (drives every later etap):** the 5 data grids split into two data models — **materialized** (SQL Editor Results, Procedure Result, Function Execute Result: full result in memory, client-side paged/sorted) vs **server-paged** (Table Detail Data, View Detail Data: only the current 200-row page in memory, `GetRowCountAsync` capped at 50 000, sort pushed to SQL as `ORDER BY`). Client-side filter/aggregate on a server-paged grid would silently operate on one page → wrong; those must be pushed to SQL (`WHERE` + separate `SELECT agg()`), which is why the user split them into their own later etap. `QueryColumn.ClrType` is present → type-aware aggregation is feasible client-side. All 5 grids are 5 separate implementations (no shared component yet) — a future `DataGridPanel` + `IGridDataSource` (2 impls) unification is the eventual dedup, staged last.

**Etap 0 — localization.** The 6 Table Detail main-tab labels were Polish string *values* in `UiStrings` (`TableDetailTabFields="Pola"`, `Constraints="Ograniczenia"`, `Indexes="Indeksy"`, `Dependencies="Zależności"`, `Data="Dane"`, `Description="Opis"`) → English. **This reverses the earlier "keep tab names Polish" instruction** (the app is English). Audit (diacritics + Polish-word grep across App `.cs`/`.axaml`) found the only other user-visible Polish was the Folder/Connection/Query context-menu cluster in `UiStrings` (`FolderNewTooltip`/`FolderDialogTitle`/`FolderDialogNameLabel`/`FolderDialogCreate`/`FolderContextRename`/`FolderContextDelete`/`FolderDeleteConfirm*`/`FolderDefaultName`, `ConnectionContextSort`/`SortAscending`/`SortDescending`, `FolderContextAddConnection`, `QueryContextRename`/`QueryContextDelete`/`QueryRenameTooltip`) → all translated to English. `.axaml` files carry no hardcoded text (everything is `x:Static app:UiStrings.*`); remaining Polish is only in code comments (left as-is — not user-visible). Metadata/field/dependency/constraint-sub-tab strings were already English. No test asserted the old Polish values.

**Etap 1 — "Record N of M".** IBExpert-style record-position indicator on the three **materialized** grids (SQL / Procedure / Function). New `UiStrings.RecordPositionFormat = "Record {0} of {1}"` + `RecordCountFormat = "{0} rows"`. Each VM (`MainWindowViewModel`, `ProcedureDetailTabViewModel`, `FunctionDetailTabViewModel`) got a `…RecordInfo` getter + a `Set…SelectedRow(indexInPage)` sink + a `-1` reset in the page-rebuild path: absolute position = `(page-1)*pageSize + indexInPage + 1` over the full (sorted) row count; when rows exist but nothing is selected → `"{total} rows"`; empty when no rows. Views bind a subtle `TextBlock` next to the pagination hint and wire the grid's `SelectionChanged` → `Set…SelectedRow(grid.SelectedIndex)` (SelectedIndex is within the current page; the VM adds the page offset). Re-slicing a page drops grid selection, so the pointer resets to `-1` (falls back to the count). Pinned by 5 tests in `ResultPagingSortTests` (no-result → empty, rows-no-selection → count, first-page absolute position, second-page page-offset math, page-change clears selection).

**Record N of M for the two server-paged grids (Table Data / View Data) is intentionally deferred** into the tables/views etap — it ties into the same paging + cap (`≥50 000`) + selection-within-page code that etap reworks, so doing it there avoids editing that code twice.

### Data Grid Filtering & Aggregations — Faza 1–2 foundation (WIP, branch `feat/data-grid-filtering`, 2026-07-02)

Scope expanded by the user: full filter + aggregation + Record-N-of-M system across ALL 5 grids in one iteration; materialized (SQL/Procedure/Function) run client-side, server-paged (Table/View) run SQL push-down (parameterized WHERE + separate aggregate queries on the FULL dataset — never "filter only the 200 visible rows"). No shared-`DataGridPanel` refactor yet (functionality first). **Fazy 1–2 = the correctness-critical foundation, fully unit-tested (build 0/0, 2054 → 2100 tests, +46).**

**Faza 1 — Core engine (zero Avalonia/DB)** — [GridDataQuery.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Query/GridDataQuery.cs) + [GridDataEngine.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Query/GridDataEngine.cs):
- `GridFilter` (conditions + `GridFilterCombine` AND/OR, `Empty`) · `GridFilterCondition` (record: ColumnIndex/ColumnName/Operator/Value) · `GridFilterOperator` (Equals/NotEquals/Less/LessOrEqual/Greater/GreaterOrEqual/Contains/StartsWith/EndsWith/IsNull/IsNotNull) · `GridAggregate` (Sum/Avg/Count/CountDistinct/Min/Max).
- `GridColumnClassifier` — `Classify(Type)` → `GridColumnCategory` (Numeric/Temporal/Text/Boolean/Other) + `OperatorsFor(cat)` / `AggregatesFor(cat)` (type-aware menus) + `OperatorTakesValue`.
- `GridValueConverter` — `TryConvert(text, cat)` (CurrentCulture→Invariant fallback; numeric→decimal, temporal→DateTime, bool incl. 1/0, text) + `Canonicalize(cell, cat)`.
- `GridFilterEvaluator.Matches(row, filter, columns)` + `GridAggregator.Compute(rows, colIndex, agg, clrType)` — **Firebird-matching semantics** (Contains=case-insensitive; StartsWith/EndsWith/text `=`/ordering=case-sensitive; comparison vs NULL=false). COUNT counts non-null; SUM/AVG decimal (double for float/double cols); returns raw value (VM formats), null when agg N/A or empty.

**Faza 2 — Firebird push-down** — [FirebirdGridSqlBuilder.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdGridSqlBuilder.cs) + [FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdTableDetailReader.cs):
- `BuildWhere(filter, columns)` → `GridSqlFilter { WhereClause (no leading WHERE), Parameters }` — **parameterized `@p0..@pN`, never inlined** (injection-safe). Operators → `= <> < <= > >=`, `CONTAINING`, `STARTING WITH`, `LIKE @p ESCAPE '\'` (EndsWith, `%`-prefix + `%`/`_`/`\` escaped), `IS [NOT] NULL`. Comparison operands bound as typed values (decimal/DateTime/bool/string); an unconvertible value operand is **skipped** (UI validates). `AggregateExpression(agg, col)` → `SUM("c")`…`COUNT(DISTINCT "c")`.
- Reader: `GetDataPreviewAsync(..., GridSqlFilter? filter, ...)` (WHERE between FROM and ORDER BY) + `GetRowCountAsync(..., filter, ...)` (WHERE inside the capped subquery) + new `GetAggregateAsync(table, col, agg, filter)` (`SELECT <agg> FROM "T" [WHERE ...]` over the whole set). `AddFilterParameters` binds via `AddWithValue`. Old overloads preserved (delegate with null filter). `BuildDataPreviewSql`/`BuildRowCountSql` gained optional `where`; new `BuildAggregateSql`.

**Tests (+46):** [GridDataEngineTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/GridDataEngineTests.cs) (classify/operators/aggregates per category, value conversion, evaluate incl. AND/OR/NULL/case-sensitivity, aggregate SUM/AVG/MIN/MAX/COUNT/DISTINCT + temporal + text + N/A→null + empty→null) + [FirebirdGridSqlBuilderTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/FirebirdGridSqlBuilderTests.cs) (WHERE shape, typed params, LIKE escaping, AND/OR combine, skip-unconvertible, identifier quoting, aggregate expressions, preview/count/aggregate SQL shape with WHERE).

**Extensibility design decision (per user, design-only — NOT implemented):** the model must not block future IN/NOT IN, BETWEEN/NOT BETWEEN, date ranges, Save filter, Recent filters. How it's kept open: (a) `GridFilterCondition` is a `record` — future multi-value operators (IN list, BETWEEN lo/hi) add an OPTIONAL init-only property (e.g. `ExtraValues`) without breaking the positional ctor; (b) new operators are additive `GridFilterOperator` members + `OperatorsFor` entries + one evaluator + one builder case (localized change); (c) the Faza-3 condition-row VM will expose a computed `ValueEditorKind` (None/Single today; +List/Range/DateRange later) so the value-editor UI is operator-driven — adding an editor kind won't rebuild the panel; (d) `GridFilter` is a clean serializable DTO → Save/Recent later = persist/restore the DTO to `UserSettings` + a dropdown. The one thing that WOULD need a model change (documented, not needed by the listed extensions) is **nested/grouped `(A OR B) AND C`** — current model is a single flat combine mode.

**Faza 3a — shared VM layer (DONE, +15 tests).** Host-agnostic VMs in `EmberTern.App.ViewModels`, reused by every grid without unifying the grids themselves (no `DataGridPanel`): [GridColumnRef.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/GridColumnRef.cs) (`GridColumnRef(Index,Name,ClrType)` + `Category`; `ValueEditorKind` enum None/Single, reserved List/Range/DateRange), [GridFilterCatalog.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/GridFilterCatalog.cs) (`OperatorOption`/`AggregateOption` + labels + type-aware option lists), [FilterConditionRowViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/FilterConditionRowViewModel.cs) (column+operator+value row; operators follow the column category; `ValueEditorKind` operator-driven; `TryBuild()`→`GridFilterCondition?`), [FilterPanelViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/FilterPanelViewModel.cs) (`Conditions` + `MatchAny` AND/OR + `IsPanelOpen`/`IsFilterActive`; `SetColumns`/`BuildFilter`/`ApplyFromCellAsync`; applies via `Func<GridFilter,Task> ApplyRequested` — host decides client vs server), [AggregationLineViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/AggregationLineViewModel.cs) + [AggregationBarViewModel.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/AggregationBarViewModel.cs) (lines compute through `Func<GridColumnRef,GridAggregate,Task<object?>>`; `RecomputeAllAsync` sequential per gotcha #31; result formatted CurrentCulture, null→"∅"). Extensibility seams: operator-driven `ValueEditorKind` (add List/Range/DateRange editor w/o panel rebuild), `GridFilter` DTO round-trips for future Save/Recent, new operators are additive. Strings in `UiStrings` (`FilterOp*`/`Aggregate*`/`Filter*`/`Aggregation*`). Tests: [GridFilterPanelVmTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/GridFilterPanelVmTests.cs).

**REMAINING:** Faza 3b — the two UserControls (`FilterPanelView` + `AggregationBarView`, bound to the Faza-3a VMs; reused in all 5 views) + filter-from-cell context menu (resolve clicked cell via `CellPointerPressed`, gotcha #99). Faza 4 — wire the 3 materialized grids: host `FilterPanelViewModel`/`AggregationBarViewModel`, `ApplyRequested` filters the in-memory set (`GridFilterEvaluator`) feeding the existing paging pipeline, compute callback = `GridAggregator` over the filtered rows, recompute on apply. Faza 5 — wire Table/View: `ApplyRequested` builds `FirebirdGridSqlBuilder.BuildWhere` → re-query page 1 + re-count with the filter; compute callback = `GetAggregateAsync`; + Record-N-of-M for Table/View. Faza 6 — build + tests + smoke. No merge to master until the user approves.

### Data Grid Filtering & Aggregations — Faza 3b–6 (UI wiring, all 5 grids) — shipped on branch 2026-07-02

Completes the full-scope filtering/aggregation/Record-N-of-M system: one identical filter panel + aggregation bar + record indicator across all 5 grids (SQL Results, Procedure Results, Function Results, Table Data, View Data). Materialized grids run client-side; the two server-paged grids push filter + aggregates to SQL. UX is identical; the client-vs-server split lives entirely inside each host's callbacks. **2113 → 2125 tests** (+12). Build 0/0, 8s smoke OK. Still on `feat/data-grid-filtering` — NOT merged.

**Faza 3b — two reusable UserControls + shared cell helper.** [FilterPanelView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/FilterPanelView.axaml) (bound to `FilterPanelViewModel`, self-hides via `IsPanelOpen`) — header (title · Match-any CheckBox · Apply/Clear) + `ItemsControl` of condition rows (column ComboBox · operator ComboBox · value TextBox gated on `ShowValueEditor` · remove) + empty hint + Add-condition. [AggregationBarView.axaml](src/EmberTern.App/Views/AggregationBarView.axaml) (bound to `AggregationBarViewModel`, self-hides via `IsBarOpen`) — Add-aggregate + `ItemsControl` of lines (column · function · `= result` · remove). New shared [GridCellFilter.cs](src/EmberTern.App/Views/GridCellFilter.cs): `GridCellFilterContext` + `Resolve(grid, DataGridCellPointerPressedEventArgs, columns)` (gotcha #99; prefers the boxed data index in `Column.Tag`, reorder-proof) + the three verb mappers (`FilterByValue`/`ExcludeValue`/`Contains` → op+value, null-cell → IS [NOT] NULL) + `SupportsContains` (text cells only). Each host appends its own 3 `MenuItem`s (grids already carry Copy / Set NULL menus, so we append rather than own the menu). New icons `Icon.Filter` (Lucide funnel) + `Icon.Sigma` for the toggles. `AggregationLineViewModel` now **auto-recomputes on function change** (fire the `ComputeCommand` in `OnSelectedFunctionChanged`) so a line's result never goes stale without a manual apply. `FilterPanelViewModel.Columns` exposed for the cell resolver. New string `AggregationEmptyHint`.

**Faza 4 — 3 materialized grids (client-side).** Each owning VM hosts a `FilterPanelViewModel` + `AggregationBarViewModel`; on a new result the columns are (re)set via `GridColumnRef.From(result.Columns)` (new shared static factory) and the filter reset. `ApplyRequested` filters the materialized rows through `GridFilterEvaluator` (→ feeds the existing paging pipeline: filter → sort → page), then `RecomputeAllAsync`; the aggregate callback runs `GridAggregator` over the filtered set (all pages, not just the current page). Wired: SQL Results in [MainWindowViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/MainWindowViewModel.cs) (`ResultFilterPanel`/`ResultAggregationBar`; `RebuildResultView` filters first, handles an empty filtered set); Procedure Results in [ProcedureDetailTabViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ProcedureDetailTabViewModel.cs) + Function Results in [FunctionDetailTabViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/FunctionDetailTabViewModel.cs) (`ExecFilterPanel`/`ExecAggregationBar`; `_execAllRows` = full, `_execRows` = filtered/displayed). Each view got toggle buttons in the pagination row, the two panels as new grid rows, `Tag`-stamped columns, `CellPointerPressed` capture, and the 3 filter menu items.

**Faza 5 — Table/View (SQL push-down) + Record N of M.** [TableDetailTabViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/TableDetailTabViewModel.cs) (Dane) + [ViewDetailTabViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ViewDetailTabViewModel.cs) (Data) host `DataFilterPanel`/`DataAggregationBar`. `ApplyRequested` builds a `FirebirdGridSqlBuilder.GridSqlFilter` (parameterized WHERE, from `DataFilterPanel.Columns` → `QueryColumn`), re-fetches page 1 + re-probes the bounded row count + recomputes; `_dataSqlFilter` is threaded into `GetDataPreviewAsync` / `GetRowCountAsync` (sort/last-page paths too); the aggregate callback is `GetAggregateAsync` (server-side `SELECT agg` over the WHOLE filtered table/view). `SyncDataFilterColumns` re-points the panels ONLY when the column structure changes (a same-column filter reload keeps the conditions — SetColumns clears them). **Record N of M** for the server-paged grids: `DataRecordInfo` uses `LastKnownRowCount` (a bounded COUNT, re-probed on initial load / sort / filter — NOT on plain page nav) as M, with a `"+"` suffix when the count hit `RowCountCap`; `SetDataSelectedRow(indexInPage)` + the page offset give N. Each view: filter/agg toggles, the two panels, `DataRecordInfo` text, `SelectionChanged`, `Tag`-stamped columns, and the 3 filter menu items (Dane keeps Set NULL; the menu is no longer gated on `CanEditData` so filtering works on read-only/system tables).

**Faza 6 — tests + the hang fix.** New [GridFilterHostWiringTests.cs](tests/EmberTern.Tests/GridFilterHostWiringTests.cs) (+12): SQL/Procedure/Function filter narrows rows + Record-N-of-M; aggregate computes over the FILTERED set; new-result resets the filter; Table/View column-sync, filter-active state, Record-N-of-M formatting (incl. capped `"50000+"`), and same-column-reload keeps conditions. Server-paged SQL is unit-covered by the Faza-2 `FirebirdGridSqlBuilderTests`; the live filter/aggregate against a real FB is the user's manual smoke.

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

160. **A `RadioButton` with a static `GroupName` inside a REUSED `UserControl` deadlocks the UI when >1 instance renders — a shared group across independent VMs fights the group manager in an infinite binding loop.** The first cut of `FilterPanelView` had two `RadioButton`s (`GroupName="filterCombine"`) for AND/OR with `IsChecked="{Binding !MatchAny}"` (one-way) + `IsChecked="{Binding MatchAny, Mode=TwoWay}"`. Avalonia scopes `GroupName` to the whole window, so when the same UserControl renders in several places (SQL Results + Table Data + View Data …) ALL the "AND" radios land in ONE group. The group manager permits only one checked member; it unchecks the others; each other's one-way `{Binding !MatchAny}` immediately re-asserts "checked"; the group unchecks again → an infinite check/uncheck loop that never lets `Dispatcher.UIThread.RunJobs()` settle → the app freezes the moment a detail view renders (and the headless probe hangs forever). A SINGLE instance is fine (self-consistent), which is why it slipped a solo build. **Fix:** don't put a grouped `RadioButton` in a shared UserControl — use a single `CheckBox` (unchecked = AND / checked = OR), which has no group and no cross-instance coupling. **Rule:** any control with window-scoped grouping (`RadioButton.GroupName`) is unsafe inside a control that can be instantiated more than once; prefer a `CheckBox`/`ToggleButton`/`ComboBox`, or generate a per-instance unique group name in code-behind. Diagnose a "renders once, hangs when reused / hangs in the headless probe that builds the real window" symptom by looking for shared window-scoped state in the reused view first.

### Data Grid Filtering & Aggregations — COMPLETE (shipped 2026-07-02, merged to `master`)

Consolidates + finalizes the whole feature (the earlier Faza 1–6 milestones above) after a live-testing bugfix/UX pass. One consistent filtering + aggregation + record-navigation experience across **all 5 data grids** — SQL Results, Procedure Results, Function Results, Table Data, View Data. **2113 → 2135 tests**, build 0/0, smoke clean.

**Supported grids + architecture.** Materialized grids (SQL / Procedure / Function results — the full result is in memory) filter + aggregate **client-side** (`GridFilterEvaluator` / `GridAggregator` over the materialized rows). Server-paged grids (Table Data / View Data — only the current 200-row page in memory) **push down to Firebird SQL** (`FirebirdGridSqlBuilder`: parameterized `WHERE` + a separate `SELECT agg()` over the whole filtered set). The UX is byte-identical; the client-vs-server split lives entirely inside each host's `ApplyRequested` / compute callbacks. Shared, host-agnostic VMs: `FilterPanelViewModel`, `AggregationBarViewModel` (+ `AggregationLineViewModel` chip), `GridColumnRef` / `GridFilterCatalog` / `GridColumnClassifier` / `GridValueConverter`. Shared views: `FilterPanelView`, `AggregationBarView`; shared cell helper `GridCellFilter`. No big `DataGridPanel` unification (functionality-first; the 5 grids stay separate hosts wired to the shared VMs).

**Filtering.** Filter panel (per grid, toggled by a filter `ToggleButton`): add/remove conditions, per-column type-aware operators, **AND / OR** combine (a single `CheckBox` "Match any (OR)" — never grouped RadioButtons, gotcha #160), **Apply / Clear**. Cell context-menu verbs via `GridCellFilter`: **Filter by value** (`=`), **Exclude value** (`≠`), **Contains** (text only). Operators: `= ≠ < ≤ > ≥`, CONTAINING / STARTING WITH / LIKE-suffix, IS [NOT] NULL. **Active-filter marker:** a small accent **dot** overlaid top-right of the filter icon (shared `Border.filter-active-dot` style) bound to the panel's `IsFilterActive` — visible whenever a non-empty filter is applied, **independent of whether the panel is open** (the `ToggleButton :checked` state = panel-open; the dot = filter-applied). No extra toolbar text (DataGrip/IBExpert convention). **Timestamp precision fix:** `GridCellFilter` formats a `DateTime` cell with a round-trippable invariant form (`yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.FFFFFFF`, fraction only when non-zero) instead of `Convert.ToString` "G" — the old format dropped sub-second precision so an exact `=` on a Firebird `TIMESTAMP` with a fraction found 0 rows (gotcha #161).

**Aggregations (chips, not a footer).** Deliberately NOT the IBExpert footer and NOT results-in-column-headers — a compact **chip** strip under the grid toolbar (evaluated: a true aligned footer needs internal-scrollbar sync + width mirroring in Avalonia 12.0.0 → fragile, rejected; column-header results also rejected by the user). **One-gesture add:** pick a column (persistent context) → pick a **function** → the chip appears + computes immediately (no separate "+ Add" button); the function picker resets to its "Add aggregate…" placeholder so the same function can be re-picked. Removal via **✕** on each chip. Functions (type-aware): **SUM, AVG, COUNT, COUNT DISTINCT, MIN, MAX**. `RecomputeAllAsync` re-runs every chip after a filter change (client) / re-queries (server).

**UX consistency.** All 5 grids share ONE layout: a self-contained grid toolbar row `[⏮ ◀ ▶ ⏭] · page hint · Record N of M · [Filter⧩] [Σ] [↻ refresh where applicable]`, then the filter panel, then the aggregation strip, then the grid. **Record N of M** on every grid (materialized: absolute position over the full/filtered set; Table/View: over the bounded `LastKnownRowCount`). Table Data's pagination + Refresh were moved out of the main app toolbar into the Dane sub-tab's own grid toolbar (previously the lone split grid); Refresh sits flush at the end of the icon group (it's a data-reload action, not navigation). **Filters and aggregations persist when their panels are closed** — the panels are pure UI; closing a panel never clears the filter or the chips (only the user does, via Clear / the chip ✕). New result set → filter + chips reset + panels re-pointed at the new columns.

**Extensibility seams kept (design-only, not built):** the condition model is a serializable DTO + operator-driven `ValueEditorKind`, ready for future IN/NOT IN, BETWEEN, date ranges, and Save/Recent filters without a panel rewrite.

**Bugfix/UX pass (this session, the finishing work):** (1) Timestamp filter 0-rows → precision round-trip (gotcha #161, +5 regression tests); (2) aggregation "no result" investigated via a real-MainWindow headless probe (`AggregationAddRow_ProducesComputedChip`) that proved the add→chip→compute chain sound across every ordering — the one plausible live-only cause (ComboBox `SelectedItem`-before-`ItemsSource` clobber, gotcha #71) was hardened in `SetColumns`; (3) orphaned toolbar separators after moving Table pagination out — `ShowHelperSection` no longer counts `IsDataTabActive` (gotcha #162); (4) Refresh repositioned + regrouped; (5) toggle buttons are real `ToggleButton`s bound to `IsPanelOpen`/`IsBarOpen` so the "on" look tracks panel visibility, not focus; (6) the active-filter dot marker; (7) one-gesture aggregation add.

**Tests.** `GridDataEngineTests`, `FirebirdGridSqlBuilderTests`, `GridFilterPanelVmTests`, `GridFilterHostWiringTests`, `SnippetContextBuilderTests` (unaffected), new `GridCellFilterTests` (Timestamp round-trip + the old-truncation regression pin), plus two headless probes in `ConnectionExpandBindingProbe` (aggregation add-row through the real window; the `filter-active-dot` style resolves). The live filter/aggregate/Record-N-of-M against a real Firebird is the user's manual smoke; the engine, SQL builder, VM wiring, and binding/style resolution are unit- + probe-pinned.

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

161. **A cell-derived "filter by value" string must ROUND-TRIP through the value converter, or an exact `=` misses its own row.** `GridCellFilter` builds the filter operand as text (later parsed back by `GridValueConverter.TryConvert` for the client comparison AND the SQL `FbParameter`). `Convert.ToString(dateTime, culture)` uses the "G" format, which **drops sub-second precision** — so a Firebird `TIMESTAMP` stored as `…:00.1234567` became `…:00`, and the exact comparison (client `DateTime.Compare` / server bound param) never matched → "Filter by value" on a timestamp returned 0 rows. Fix: format `DateTime` cells with an invariant, sub-second-preserving form (`yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.FFFFFFF`, fraction only when non-zero so whole-second values stay clean). **Rule:** any value you stringify for later re-parse (filter operands, persisted keys) must survive the format→parse round-trip at full precision; verify with a round-trips-exactly test AND a negative test that documents the lossy form.

162. **When you relocate the only content of a toolbar "section", update that section's visibility predicate — or its separator orphans.** The unified toolbar draws a `|` between two non-empty adjacent sections (`ToolbarSepNVisible = ShowSectionN && HasFromN+1`). Moving Table-Dane's Refresh + pagination out of the main toolbar's Helper section into the sub-tab left `ShowHelperSection` still returning true for `IsDataTabActive`, so the helper separator rendered with nothing beside it (a lone `|`). Fix: drop `IsDataTabActive` from `ShowHelperSection`. **Rule:** a section-visibility flag must reflect whether that section actually has visible content *now*; when content moves between the main toolbar and a tab (or between sections), re-derive the flag, not just the buttons.

### Execute Procedure / Function parameter dialog — TIMESTAMP default, text time entry, persistent history (shipped 2026-07-02)

Three UX fixes to the shared Execute-parameters dialog ([ExecuteProcedureDialog](src/EmberTern.App/Views/ExecuteProcedureDialog.axaml) + [ExecuteProcedureDialogViewModel](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/ExecuteProcedureDialogViewModel.cs)), used by BOTH the Procedure and Function editors' Execute (Data lane). **2135 → 2177 tests** (+42). Build 0/0, smoke clean.

**1. TIMESTAMP defaults to today 00:00:00** (was today + current wall-clock time). The only init site is the `ExecuteProcedureParamRowViewModel` ctor: `Timestamp` → `DateValue = now.Date; TimeValue = TimeSpan.Zero`. `DATE` (already `now.Date`) and `TIME` (`now.TimeOfDay`) unchanged. Rationale: TIMESTAMP columns in ERP schemas are overwhelmingly date-range bounds (DATAOD/DATADO), not "now" — the current-time default was the reported annoyance. History (when present) overrides the default.

**2. Free-text time entry** — the `TimePicker` (scrolling flyout, slow for keyboard) replaced with a plain `TextBox` for the TIME component (and TIME-typed params); the date component keeps `CalendarDatePicker`. Pure, unit-tested `ExecuteProcedureParamRowViewModel.TryParseTime` accepts `8` / `8:30` / `8:30:15` (H / H:mm / H:mm:ss; empty = 00:00:00), enforces 0–23 h / 0–59 m/s, rejects garbage. `TimeText` (string, bound to the TextBox) is committed to the canonical `TimeValue` by `CommitTime()` — called on **focus-loss** (code-behind `OnTimeBoxLostFocus`) and again in `Accept`. Focus selects the whole value (`OnTimeBoxGotFocus` → `Dispatcher.Post(SelectAll)`) for fast retyping. An unparseable, non-null time sets `HasTimeError` (red border via a `TextBox.time-error` style over `ErrorBrush`) + the dialog's `HasValidationError` message line, and **blocks Execute** (`Accept` re-validates all rows first; on any error it doesn't set `Result`/fire `RequestClose`).

**3. Persistent parameter history, per (Connection, ObjectKind, ObjectName)** — replaces the old process-lifetime, name-only `ExecuteProcedureHistory` static dict (deleted). Persisted in the encrypted `settings.dat` via a new `UserSettings.ParameterHistory` section + section-facade [ParameterHistoryStore](src/EmberTern.Core/Settings/ParameterHistoryStore.cs) (read-modify-write, mirrors `GridProfileStore`/`FolderStore` — never clobbers other sections). Models [ParameterHistory.cs](src/EmberTern.Core/Settings/ParameterHistory.cs): `ParameterHistoryEntry { ConnectionId, ObjectKind, ObjectName, Executions[] }` / `ParameterSet { ExecutedAt, Values[] }` / `ParameterValue { Name, IsNull, Text }`. `Record` dedups against the newest set (identical values → refresh its timestamp, no dup) and caps at `MaxSets = 20` FIFO; keys match connection Id exactly + kind/name case-insensitive. **UX (variant C, user-approved):** a "History" `ComboBox` in the dialog header (hidden until ≥1 saved set) with a **two-line item** — `TimestampText` (subtle) + a compact `PreviewText` (`DATAOD=2026-01-01, DATADO=2026-12-31`, truncated) — so the user recognises the set before loading. Selecting an entry loads its values into the existing parameter grid (still editable before Run). **The newest set auto-loads on open** (ctor sets `SelectedHistory = History[0]`) — the "re-run last" case needs zero clicks. `MainWindowViewModel.ParameterHistory` is built from `store.FilePath` dir + `store.Protector` (gotcha #88); the two detail views pass `Service.ActiveProfile?.Id` + `"Procedure"`/`"Function"` at dialog-open.

**Value serialization.** Dialog values are typed (`decimal`/`DateTime`/`TimeSpan`/`bool`/`string`); history stores each as a **canonical invariant-culture string** (`ToHistoryValue`) and re-hydrates per Firebird type (`ApplyHistoryValue`) — never boxed `object?` in JSON. TIMESTAMP uses `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.FFFFFFF` (sub-second-lossless, same discipline as gotcha #161). Unparseable stored values are ignored on restore so a schema/type change can't crash the load.

**Files** (9 changed, 4 new): Core — `ParameterHistory.cs` (new), `ParameterHistoryStore.cs` (new), `UserSettings.cs` (+ParameterHistory). App — `ExecuteProcedureDialogViewModel.cs` (row VM time/history/round-trip + dialog VM history + validation; `ExecuteProcedureHistory` removed), `ExecuteProcedureDialog.axaml`(+.cs) (history ComboBox, time TextBox, error style/line, focus handlers), `MainWindowViewModel.cs` (internal `ParameterHistoryStore`), `ProcedureDetailTabView.axaml.cs` / `FunctionDetailTabView.axaml.cs` (pass connId+kind+store), `UiStrings.cs` (history/time strings). Tests — `ParameterHistoryStoreTests.cs` (new, 11), `ExecuteProcedureDialogTests.cs` (new, ~29), `ProcedureDetailTests.cs` (the in-memory history test rewritten to the persistent store).

**Tests** cover: `TryParseTime` accept/reject/empty, TIMESTAMP-default, `CommitTime` normalize/error/null, per-kind + sub-second-TIMESTAMP + null value round-trip, store dedup/cap/isolation/case-insensitivity/persistence/other-sections-preserved, dialog auto-load-newest + select-applies + preview + invalid-time-blocks-Accept. The dialog UI itself (TextBox/ComboBox rendering) is manual smoke, per the codebase's headless-flakiness precedent (the `ConnectionExpandBindingProbe` headless class must be run as a separate `dotnet test` partition — it intermittently hangs when run alongside the rest, gotcha #94; both partitions pass: 2168 + 9).

**Gotchas — promote to architecture lore.**

163. **Persist typed dialog/input values as canonical invariant strings + re-hydrate per type — never boxed `object?` in JSON.** The Execute-params dialog holds typed values (`decimal`/`DateTime`/`TimeSpan`/`bool`/`string`); serializing `object?` through System.Text.Json is lossy/ambiguous and couples the on-disk shape to CLR boxing. Store `{ IsNull, Text }` where `Text` is invariant-culture (`yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.FFFFFFF` for TIMESTAMP so sub-seconds survive — gotcha #161; `hh\:mm\:ss` for TIME; invariant `decimal`), and parse back keyed on the known type. Ignore unparseable values on restore (a schema/type change must degrade, not crash). Per-object history is a new `UserSettings` section behind a `ParameterHistoryStore` facade (the `GridProfileStore` pattern — read-modify-write over the shared encrypted `settings.dat`, never clobbering sibling sections); key by `ConnectionId` (exact) + `ObjectKind` + `ObjectName` (case-insensitive), dedup the newest set, cap FIFO, and auto-select the newest in the VM ctor for "re-run last".

164. **Avalonia 12 `GotFocus`/`LostFocus` args are `Avalonia.Input.FocusChangedEventArgs` (base `RoutedEventArgs`) — there is no `GotFocusEventArgs`.** A code-behind XAML handler typed `(object?, RoutedEventArgs)` binds to `GotFocus="..."` / `LostFocus="..."` fine (the base type is delegate-compatible with how Avalonia wires XAML events — same as the existing rename `LostFocus` handlers). Don't reach for a `GotFocusEventArgs` type (it doesn't exist → CS0246); use `RoutedEventArgs`. For "select all on focus", post the `SelectAll()` at `DispatcherPriority.Background` so it wins over the click's caret placement.

## Performance Analysis (APPROVED FEATURE — design is source of truth; Phase 1 on `feat/performance-analysis-phase1`)

A query/PSQL **performance analysis experience** for EmberTern. Status: **design approved and frozen** (UX, wireframes, MVP, domain model, roadmap). Future sessions **extend this design, do not redesign it.** **Phase 1 is COMPLETE + merged to `master` (2026-07-03)** — plan capture + parser + plan-tree + self-measured stats + `PerformanceReport`, auto-available after execution (Option B), with a plain-language summary and demoted plan (see the Current-state bullet for details). **Phase 2 MEASUREMENT SLICE is COMPLETE + merged to `master` (2026-07-03)** — MON$ per-table reads, sequential-vs-indexed, read amplification, measured findings, Table Access bars, and the Summary↔Verdict↔Findings consistency fix. The **advisor** (rule engine, predicate parser, sargability, R1–R5, **missing-index detection**, **recommendations**) and **"% of table scanned"** (catalog cardinality reader) are **deferred to a later advisor phase** — see the "Phase 2 measurement slice" milestone below.

### Vision
Every existing Firebird tool (IBExpert et al.) is information-rich but guidance-poor — it shows `NATURAL READS = 72`, a plan, and fetch counts, but never says *what's wrong* or *what to change*. EmberTern's differentiator is a **deterministic, evidence-backed advisor** that turns raw optimizer output into a ranked list of findings, each with a fix — while keeping every raw number one scroll away for experts. Two load-bearing ideas: (1) the answer is at the top, raw data at the bottom, nothing between them is required reading; (2) EmberTern **owns the connection and the cursor**, so it can capture per-statement per-table read stats that `isql` structurally cannot (isql destroys the statement before its stats can be read).

### Approved UX direction (frozen)
One **Performance surface** — the identical component hosted in the SQL Editor (a 4th bottom-panel sub-tab beside Results/Messages/Output) **and** in Procedure/Function/View Detail (a sub-tab). The only difference between hosts is how many statements feed it (editor = one; procedure/function = N internal cursors). **Progressive disclosure, four stacked zones** (NOT five sibling tabs — that repeats IBExpert's "which tab?" failure):
- **① Verdict bar** (always visible): severity dot + one-word grade (`Fast`/`Acceptable`/`Needs attention`/`Slow`) + headline metrics (time · rows returned, rows read, **amplification factor**) + one plain-language sentence = the beginner's whole answer. Right-side: `Re-run & profile`, `Export`, `Capture ▾`.
- **② Findings** (ranked advisor list): each an accordion → Explanation → Evidence → Fix → action buttons (`Test this index`, `Copy DDL`, `Show in plan`). Collapses to one green `✓ No issues` row when clean.
- **③ Access & Plan** (linked, splitter-resizable): left = **Table Access bars** (red=sequential/Natural, blue=index, **sorted by Natural desc**, offending table pinned, each bar annotated with absolute reads + **% of table scanned** so magnitude is never shown without scale — the fix for IBExpert's "72 looks scary" flaw); right = **Plan tree** cost-heat tinted, `Full Scan`/NATURAL flagged. Clicking a finding highlights the table in both panes (one selection model across ①②③).
- **④ Details** (expert drawer, collapsed): timings (prepare/execute/fetch, cold-cache badge), I/O counters (with accuracy label), full per-table counters, raw plan text (selectable/copyable), transaction/TPB context, capture method. Everything selectable + exportable.

**Redesigned Execution Log** (replaces `285 rows in 1020 ms`): a structured, **selectable/copyable/exportable** list of collapsible per-run records — timestamp · severity dot · statement (elided) · headline metric, expanding to Rows / Timing / I/O / Access / Tx / Finding, with honesty chips (cold-cache, counter-accuracy) and a `↦ Open in Performance` deep-link. Metadata-lane/internal reader statements suppressed by default. No memory/buffer noise.

**Procedure panel:** ② becomes a **Cursor Breakdown grid** (# · Statement · Access badge · Time · Reads · Finding dot), sorted by Time desc, NATURAL pinned; ③ adds a **cursor cost ranking** bar ("which of the N statements to fix first" — the thing IBExpert never ranks). **Function panel:** adds a **Call Summary strip** (Deterministic yes/no, per-call time, and a **per-row → per-query projection**: "0.9 ms/call ≈ 90 s in a 100k-row query" — the function blind spot) + `Execute ×N` for a stable average.

Wireframes for all four surfaces (SQL Editor Performance Panel, Procedure Performance Panel, Function Performance Panel, Execution Log) are the approved layout. Cross-screen UX principles: one reading order everywhere; scale before alarm; one selection model; every finding ends in an action with before/after proof; honesty chips (cold-cache, accuracy, capture method, confidence/"candidate") surfaced not hidden; same component across all object types.

### Supported engines & verified feasibility facts (FB3/FB4/FB5)
Verified on the live FB **5.0.3** lab engine this design cycle:
- **Explained plan carries NO `[cardinality=/cost=]` annotations in FB3–5** — those are **FB6 only.** ⇒ the cost heatmap in MVP is **derived from per-table reads**, honestly labelled a *reads heatmap*, not a true cost heatmap. True cost/cardinality + estimate-vs-actual = V3/FB6.
- **`RDB$INDICES` has NO statistics-timestamp column** ⇒ no timestamp-based stale-stats detection; MVP uses the cheap `RDB$STATISTICS≈0` signal, computed divergence deferred.
- `MON$STATEMENTS.MON$EXPLAINED_PLAN` present FB3+; `MON$COMPILED_STATEMENTS` (per-cursor PSQL plans) **FB5 only**; `MON$RECORD_STATS.MON$RECORD_SEQ_READS`/`IDX_READS` present FB3+; `RDB$INDICES.RDB$STATISTICS` + `RDB$INDEX_SEGMENTS.RDB$STATISTICS` present; `RDB$CONDITION_SOURCE` (partial index) FB5.
- **Per-table reads without SYSDBA/trace:** read the Data connection's **own** MON$ attachment-level per-table counters **before/after** a profiled run and diff (a user sees their own attachments in MON$ with no privilege; commit+restart the metadata read-tx between snapshots per the MON$ snapshot rule). Under SuperServer the page-level reads/fetches are shared/noisy, but **record-level SEQ/IDX at attachment scope are accurate.**
- Driver plan API: `FbCommand` legacy plan (all versions) + explained plan (FB3+) from the prepared command — **verify exact method names on FirebirdClient 10.3.4 at Phase-1 build start**; legacy plan is the guaranteed fallback.

### MVP scope (no SYSDBA, no trace, FB3+ — the founding experience)
Plan Viewer · self-measured timings + rows-read/returned amplification · per-table reads via MON$ own-attachment delta → Table Access bars with scale context · Advisor rules R1–R5 · missing-index DDL generation (user-initiated create, not auto-test) · reads-derived heatmap · redesigned Execution Log · Procedure/Function **aggregate** table-access (per-cursor plans only where FB5 gives them free via `MON$COMPILED_STATEMENTS`). **Advisor MVP rules:** R1 costly full scan (scale-gated), R2 missing index candidate, R3 non-sargable predicate (rewrite, not index), R4 low-selectivity index, R5 uninitialized statistics (`RDB$STATISTICS≈0`). Rationale: rides entirely on EmberTern's own prepared statement + own MON$ rows + catalog ⇒ a non-SYSDBA ERP dev login uses 100% of it, uniformly on FB3+.

### Approved domain model (source of truth — extend, don't redesign)
Layering obeys the existing rules (Core = pure logic zero deps; Firebird = acquisition, returns Core DTOs, holds all `Fb*` internally; App = UI). Data flow: `PerformanceProfiler`(Firebird) → `PerformanceCapture`+`CatalogCapture`(Core DTOs) → `PerformanceAnalyzer`(Core façade: ContextBuilder→RuleEngine→ReportBuilder) → `PerformanceReport`(aggregate) → App view-models. Key Core types: **value objects** `PlanTree`/`PlanNode`/`PlanNodeMetrics`, `QueryPredicate`/`SargabilityVerdict`, `IndexModel`/`TableCatalogInfo`/`CatalogModel`, `TableAccessStat`/`TableAccessProfile`, `ExecutionTimings`/`IoCounters`/`StatementIdentity`, `Finding`/`FindingEvidence`/`Recommendation`/`IndexCandidate`; **DTOs** `RawPlanCapture`/`PerTableReadRow`/`PerformanceCapture`/`CatalogCapture`/`PerformanceReport`/`ExecutionRecord`; **services** `PlanParser`(+`ExplainPlanParser`/`LegacyPlanParser`), `PredicateExtractor`, `SargabilityClassifier`, `MissingIndexAdvisor`, `PerformanceContextBuilder`, `PerformanceRuleEngine`+`IPerformanceRule`(+`PerformanceRuleCatalog`), `PerformanceReportBuilder`, `PerformanceAnalyzer`; **report model** `PerformanceReport` aggregate with `PerformanceVerdict`/`ExecutionDetails`/`StatementPerformance`/`FunctionCallProfile`. Firebird: `FirebirdPlanReader`, `FirebirdPerfStatsReader`, `FirebirdCatalogReader`, `PerformanceProfiler`, `PerformanceCaptureException`. App: `PerformancePanelViewModel` + `Verdict`/`Finding`/`PlanNode`/`TableAccessBar`/`CursorPerformance`/`ExecutionDetails`/`ExecutionLogEntry` view-models + `PerformancePanelView`. `IPerformanceRule` is the one justified interface (many impls, the `ISqlTemplate` precedent); everything else is a direct class (no interface without two impls). Parsing rides on `SqlScanHelpers` (gotcha #129); readers use the metadata-lane + capture-lock-once pattern (gotchas #98/#120).

### Implementation phases (roadmap)
- **Phase 1 (this branch): plan-only foundation.** Performance tab shell · Explain Plan capture (driver, prepared command) · Plan parser (Explain + Legacy → `PlanTree`) · Plan tree visualization · basic self-measured execution statistics · `PerformanceReport` generation. **No** MON$, Trace, Advisor, missing-index, rule engine, predicate parser, recommendations, procedure/function breakdown.
- **Phase 2: per-table reads (MON$ own-attachment delta).** Table Access bars + scale context · upgrade findings to "confirmed" · reads-derived heatmap · amplification · FB5 per-cursor PSQL plans via `MON$COMPILED_STATEMENTS`. Predicate parser + `SargabilityClassifier` + advisor rules R1–R5 + missing-index DDL land here (they need the plan foundation + reads).
- **Phase 3: trace upgrade + Test Index (privileged/guarded).** Services trace (privilege-detected, graceful fallback) · accurate I/O under load · per-cursor PSQL plans on FB3/4 · guarded Test-Index (cardinality-gated, confirm, auto-drop, before/after) · V2 advisor rules (large-IN, cartesian, external sort, composite misuse, MVCC thrash) · computed stale-stats divergence.
- **V3/FB6:** true cost/cardinality heatmap · estimate-vs-actual blowup · per-query performance history + regression detection · plan comparison · database-wide advisor · optional LLM explanation/rewrite layer (always seeded + validated by the deterministic engine; never the source of truth).

### Phase 1 goals (the ONLY current scope)
Deliver the smallest slice that already beats IBExpert on *clarity*: run a query with profiling → a **Performance sub-tab** shows the **Explain plan as a readable, cost-neutral tree** (Full Scan/NATURAL nodes flagged) + a **basic stats header** (self-measured prepare/execute/fetch time + rows returned), assembled into a real `PerformanceReport`. Everything is deterministic, needs **no privileges and no MON$/trace**, and works FB3+. The zones ②(Findings) and ③-left(Access bars) render as empty/placeholder states in Phase 1 (their data arrives in Phase 2) — the panel scaffold is built to the approved 4-zone layout so later phases only fill zones, never restructure.

### Deferred features (explicitly NOT Phase 1)
MON$ per-table reads · Trace API · the Advisor / rule engine (`IPerformanceRule` + rules) · missing-index detection · SQL predicate parser + sargability classifier · recommendations / `Test this index` · procedure cursor breakdown · function breakdown / call-summary projection · reads-derived heatmap · accurate I/O counters · true cost heatmap · performance history. These belong to Phases 2/3/V3 above and must extend the frozen design, not alter it.

### Phase 2 — investigation findings (VERIFIED on live FB 5.0.3, 2026-07-03)
The per-table-reads acquisition (the biggest Phase-2 unknown) is **proven against the engine** — no SYSDBA, no trace:
- **Per-table Natural/Index reads come from `MON$ATTACHMENTS ⋈ MON$TABLE_STATS ⋈ MON$RECORD_STATS`** joined by `MON$ATTACHMENTS.MON$STAT_ID = MON$TABLE_STATS.MON$STAT_ID` and `MON$RECORD_STATS.MON$STAT_ID = MON$TABLE_STATS.MON$RECORD_STAT_ID`, filtered `MON$ATTACHMENTS.MON$ATTACHMENT_ID = <data attachment id>`. These are **connection-level (group 1)** counters. Probe result: `SEQ_BEFORE=0` → `SELECT COUNT(*)` full scan of 5 rows → `SEQ_AFTER=5, IDX=0`. **The before/after delta is exact and attributes reads to the specific table.**
- **Full scan → `MON$RECORD_SEQ_READS`; index access → `MON$RECORD_IDX_READS`** (verified: `PLAN (P2T INDEX …)` gave `IDX=1, SEQ=0`). This is the Natural-vs-Index signal the Table Access bars + R1/R2 need.
- **MON$ snapshot rule confirmed:** the snapshot freezes at first MON$ access within a transaction — so the two reads MUST come from a transaction we can COMMIT between (COMMIT+restart refreshes). **Design consequence:** read the stats from the **metadata connection (#2)** in its own short txs (before-read → commit → data query runs on #1 → after-read), filtering to connection #1's attachment id. This never touches the user's data working tx (which we must not commit — manual-tx rule) and reuses the existing C2 metadata lane. Cache the data attachment id (`SELECT CURRENT_CONNECTION FROM RDB$DATABASE` on #1) at connect.
- **Index selectivity** `RDB$INDICES.RDB$STATISTICS` reads fine (probe: 0.2 = 1/5 distinct after `SET STATISTICS INDEX`); `RDB$INDEX_SEGMENTS.RDB$STATISTICS` gives per-segment. `SET STATISTICS INDEX` works (already the app's Recompute-statistics path).
- **Privilege:** a user sees their **own** attachments in MON$ with no special right (SYSDBA/`MONITOR_ANY_ATTACHMENT` only needed to see *others*). Embedded connects as the OS user, so the lab-DB user tables aren't readable by it — the probe used a fresh owned DB; production connects as the user's (privileged) profile, so this is a probe-only artifact.

**Phase 2 build plan (reduce the frozen domain model to concrete steps):** (1) Core value objects `TableAccessStat`/`TableAccessProfile` + DTO `PerTableReadRow` + `TableStatsDiffer` (pure delta, unit-tested). (2) `FirebirdPerfStatsReader` (metadata lane, the MON$ join, snapshot-per-short-tx) + wire before/after around the data execution in the profiler path; upgrade `PerformanceCapture` with `TableReads` (additive) + `CaptureMethod.MonAttachmentDelta`. (3) Core catalog: `IndexModel`/`TableCatalogInfo`/`CatalogModel` + `FirebirdCatalogReader` (indexes/segments/selectivity/capped cardinality — reuse `FirebirdTableDetailReader` reads) + `CatalogCapture`; add the optional `CatalogCapture` param to `PerformanceAnalyzer.Analyze` (additive — Phase 1 call site keeps working). (4) `PredicateExtractor` (reuse `SqlScanHelpers`+`SqlAliasResolver`) + `SargabilityClassifier` + `PerformanceContextBuilder`. (5) `IPerformanceRule` + `PerformanceRuleEngine` + `PerformanceRuleCatalog` + rules R1 (costly full scan, scale-gated by reads×cardinality), R2 (missing index via `MissingIndexAdvisor`), R3 (non-sargable), R4 (low-selectivity index), R5 (uninitialized stats `RDB$STATISTICS≈0`). (6) App: fill the Findings zone (`FindingViewModel`) + Table Access bars (`TableAccessBarViewModel`, red/blue + %-scanned) + reads-derived heat tint on plan nodes + amplification in the verdict. All slots into the existing 4-zone panel + the `Analyze` façade with no restructuring. Trace/Test-Index/procedure-cursor/FB5-`MON$COMPILED_STATEMENTS` breakdown remain Phase 3.

### Performance Analysis — Phase 2 measurement slice (shipped 2026-07-03, branch `feat/performance-analysis-phase2`)

The measurement half of Phase 2: **answers "is the detected full scan actually a problem?" with MEASURED per-table reads — no advisor, no recommendations.** Verified live on FB 5.0.3 against the user's Szkoleniowa ERP schema (2387 tables). The empty Phase-2 zones of the Performance panel now show real measured data; the Phase-1 plan/summary/timings surface is unchanged.

**Core** ([EmberTern.Core/Performance/](src/EmberTern.Core/Performance/)): `PerTableReadRow` (raw MON$ row/delta DTO), `TableAccessStat` (seq/idx/total per table, `IsSequential`), `TableAccessProfile` (ordered most-sequential-first, `TotalRowsRead`), `TableStatsDiffer.Diff` (pure before/after delta, clamps negatives, case-insensitive, drops unchanged). `Finding`/`FindingEvidence`/`FindingSeverity`/`FindingKind` + `PerformanceFindings.Build` — measured-reads observations only (`CostlyFullScan`; a scan below the Low reads floor is deliberately NOT flagged — the measured form of the "72 looks scary" fix; severity from absolute seq reads + amplification). `PerformanceCapture.TableReads` + `ReadsAvailable` + `CaptureMethod.MonAttachmentDelta`; `PerformanceReport.Access` + `.Findings`; `PerformanceVerdict.RowsRead` + `.Amplification`. `PerformanceReportBuilder` builds the access profile + findings + amplification and lets a **High measured finding raise** the time-based grade (never lowers it — time stays an honest signal).

**Firebird** ([FirebirdPerfStatsReader.cs](src/EmberTern.Firebird/FirebirdPerfStatsReader.cs)): two-lane MON$ acquisition, no SYSDBA / no trace. `GetDataAttachmentIdAsync` reads `CURRENT_CONNECTION` on the **data** lane (cached once). `SnapshotAsync` reads `MON$ATTACHMENTS ⋈ MON$TABLE_STATS ⋈ MON$RECORD_STATS` (SEQ/IDX record reads) filtered to that attachment id on the **metadata** lane — each call a fresh implicit-tx MON$ snapshot, so it never touches the user's data working tx (manual-tx rule) and the before/after delta around one execution is that run's per-table reads. System tables filtered; lock captured once (gotcha #120). Returns Core DTOs only.

**App**: `FindingViewModel` (severity chip + evidence, no action buttons — measurement-only), `TableAccessBarViewModel` (red seq / blue idx, widths normalized to the widest bar in the profile — the scale context). `PerformancePanelViewModel` fills the `Findings` + `TableAccess` zones, `ShowReadsNudge` (full scan present but this run wasn't profiled with reads) and `FindingsNoneVisible` (reads measured, nothing costly). `VerdictViewModel` surfaces rows-read + amplification. `MainWindowViewModel` **arms capture only while the Performance tab is active** — a data-lane execute is bracketed by before/after snapshots → `TableStatsDiffer.Diff` → capture with `MonAttachmentDelta`; any MON$ failure degrades gracefully to plan+timings (zero cost on runs the user isn't profiling).

**Consistency fix (Summary ↔ Verdict ↔ Findings).** The Phase-1 summary lead was a *plan* heuristic ("It reads table NAGL in full — often why a query is slow") that fired on any full-scan node and could directly contradict the measured Findings ("no costly full scans found") when the scan was actually cheap (NAGL: 285 seq). Fix ([PerformanceInsight.cs](src/EmberTern.App/ViewModels/PerformanceInsight.cs)): when per-table reads are available the lead is **derived from the measured findings** — it names the costly scan(s) the findings identified, or states "No costly full table scans were measured — it read {N} rows to return {M}." (the `{N}` matches the verdict's "read" metric). With no measured reads it keeps the exact Phase-1 plan lead. The **grade mechanism is unchanged** (time-based + High-finding bump) — a 1.2 s query legitimately reads "Needs attention"; once the lead agrees with the findings the three lines cohere (slow + 72× amplification, but *not* a costly scan). Verified live: the verdict now reads "Needs attention … 1,22 s" + "No costly full table scans were measured — it read 20 552 rows to return 285." + the green findings note, with no contradiction.

**Tests**: `PerformanceReadsTests` (differ, findings incl. the "72 isn't scary" case, report-with-reads), `FirebirdPerfStatsReaderTests` (MON$ SQL shape pins), `PerformanceInsightTests` (the consistency contract — reads-measured-no-costly → measured lead not the plan heuristic; costly → names the table; no-reads → Phase-1 fallback). Build 0/0 (only the 2 pre-existing IndexDetailTabView Watermark warnings); **2209 main + 9 headless probe green** (gotcha #94 — run the probe as a separate partition).

**Deferred to a later ADVISOR phase (explicitly NOT in this slice, per the frozen roadmap + the approved narrower scope):**
- **Advisor / rule engine** (`IPerformanceRule`, `PerformanceRuleEngine`, `PerformanceRuleCatalog`, rules R1–R5).
- **Predicate parser + sargability** (`PredicateExtractor`, `SargabilityClassifier`, `PerformanceContextBuilder`).
- **Missing-index detection** (`MissingIndexAdvisor`) + **CREATE INDEX / fix recommendations** + finding action buttons.
- **"% of table scanned"** scale context — needs `FirebirdCatalogReader`/`CatalogModel`/`CatalogCapture` (index/segment/selectivity/capped cardinality). Bars today show absolute reads + normalized width; the % annotation is the follow-up.
- **Reads-derived heat tint** on plan nodes; **procedure/function cursor breakdown** (FB5 `MON$COMPILED_STATEMENTS`); Trace / Test-Index / performance history (Phase 3+).

**Known limitations in this slice (documented, not blockers):** amplification uses the executor-capped rows-returned (5000) — a truncated result could overstate it; if a metadata working tx is open across both snapshots (only after a raw F5 DDL in the SQL editor) MON$ freezes and that run degrades to plan-only; `FindingKind.HighReadAmplification` is defined but not yet emitted (only `CostlyFullScan`).

**Gotcha — promote to architecture lore.**

165. **Once measured data exists, the plain-language summary MUST be derived from the measurement, never from a plan heuristic that can contradict it.** The Phase-1 lead asserted a plan full scan was "often why a query is slow"; measured per-table reads proved that scan cheap (285 rows) while the Findings zone correctly said "no costly full scans" — so the same panel showed an amber "Needs attention", a plan-blaming lead, and a green "nothing costly" note simultaneously. The fix is layering, not new logic: the verdict **grade** stays an honest coarse signal (time + a finding-driven bump — never suppressed, a slow query is slow), but the **narrative** (`PerformanceInsight` lead) switches to the measured findings the moment reads are available, so grade/summary/findings describe the same reality from different angles instead of fighting. **Rule:** when a richer measured signal supersedes an earlier heuristic, route the *user-facing explanation* through the measured signal and keep the heuristic only as the no-measurement fallback — don't leave two independent narrators that can disagree.

### Performance Analysis — Phase 3 (Advisor) — OFFICIAL PLAN (approved 2026-07-03, branch `feat/performance-analysis-phase3-advisor`)

The advisor phase. Goal: explain **what likely causes the cost, why it's a problem, and what to investigate** — **measured-data-first** (Phase 2 reads/amplification gate every rule; the plan/SQL only explains *why*). **NOT** in Phase 3: `CREATE INDEX` generation, recommendation DDL, one-click fixes, UI redesign, plan cost heatmap (FB6), trace, performance history, procedure/function cursor breakdown. Phase 3 is about *understanding causes*; recommendations/fix actions are a later phase. Extends the frozen design (do not redesign).

**Headline shift Phase 2 forces:** the frozen R1–R5 were plan-first heuristics; Phase 2 inverts this — **a rule fires on MEASURED cost, then predicate/sargability/catalog analysis supplies the explanation.** A plan full scan is no longer evidence of a problem (the NAGL case: full scan, 285 reads, cheap). Consequence: the **catalog reader** (frozen Phase-2 step 3, deferred) is the Phase-3 prerequisite — R2/R4/R5 + "% scanned" are blind without indexes/selectivity/cardinality.

**Architecture (Core pure; `IPerformanceRule` is the one justified interface — the `ISqlTemplate` precedent):**
```
PerformanceCapture ─┐
CatalogCapture ─────┤→ PerformanceContextBuilder → PerformanceContext → PerformanceRuleEngine → IReadOnlyList<Finding>
QueryPredicate[] ───┤        (Core, pure)          (measured+plan+          (runs IPerformanceRule[],
SargabilityVerdict[]┘                               predicate+catalog)        composed by PerformanceRuleCatalog)
```
- `IPerformanceRule.Evaluate(PerformanceContext) → IReadOnlyList<Finding>` (Core). `PerformanceRuleEngine` runs the ordered set; `PerformanceRuleCatalog` composes it.
- `PerformanceContext` = measured `TableAccessProfile` + verdict metrics + `PlanTree` + `QueryPredicate[]` + `SargabilityVerdict[]` + `CatalogModel`, assembled by `PerformanceContextBuilder`.
- `FirebirdCatalogReader → CatalogModel/TableCatalogInfo/IndexModel` (Firebird, metadata lane, capture-lock-once — gotchas #98/#120; returns Core DTOs). `PerformanceAnalyzer.Analyze(capture, catalog?)` — **additive** overload; the existing single-arg call keeps working.
- **DECISION 1 (locked):** extend `Finding` with **`RuleId` + `Confidence`** (enum `High`/`Medium`/`Low`); rules return a flat `IReadOnlyList<Finding>`. **Do NOT introduce `PerformanceRuleResult`** — findings already flow through the report model + UI; no extra abstraction without a consumer.
- **Migration:** the existing `PerformanceFindings.CostlyFullScan` static builder becomes **R1** behind the engine — `PerformanceReport.Findings` shape unchanged (its Core comment anticipates this), zero UI change.
- The plan parser already gives structured `PlanNode.{Method(AccessMethod), TableName, Alias, IndexName, Detail}` — so a plan index-access node maps to (table, index) for catalog/predicate cross-check with no new plan work. `PlanNodeMetrics` (Cardinality/Cost) is null on FB3–5 (FB6 only) → **measured reads remain the cost proxy.**

**Predicate analysis** — `PredicateExtractor` (Core, reuse `SqlScanHelpers` + `SqlAliasResolver`, gotchas #18/#129) → `QueryPredicate { Table, Alias, Column, Operator, Rhs, Kind(Where|JoinOn) }` over top-level WHERE conjuncts + JOIN ON. Lightweight scanner, NOT a grammar: subquery/CASE/computed/OR-tree/view predicates are out; **unparseable ⇒ emit nothing** (never a false finding) ⇒ predicate-derived findings are ≤ Medium confidence, investigation-phrased.

**Sargability** — `SargabilityClassifier` (Core, pure) → `SargabilityVerdict` per predicate. Patterns: function/expression on the indexed column (`UPPER(col)`, `CAST(col…)`, `col+0`, `SUBSTRING`), leading-wildcard `LIKE '%x'`, implicit conversion, non-leading-segment use. Value = structural + measured joined (non-sargable on an indexed, NATURAL-scanned, expensively-read column). Remedy is *rewrite* (R3), which is why R3 is distinct from R2.

**DECISION 4 (locked) — confidence + language.** Every finding carries **High/Medium/Low confidence** and uses **investigation-oriented** language — "Investigate…", "Likely cause…", "Potential contributor…". **No imperative / no fix language** ("Create index…", "Change query…") — that's a later phase.

**Rule set (R1–R6):**

| Rule | What | Measured-first behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** costly full scan | table read sequentially, costly | shipped in Phase 2 (measured SEQ + amplification); migrate into engine, add catalog **% of table scanned** | **Phase 3a** (migrate + enrich) |
| **R3** non-sargable predicate | expression/function blocks index use on an expensively-scanned column | sargability (structural) gated by measured cost; remedy = investigate a predicate rewrite | **Phase 3a** |
